# CLAUDE.md - AYN System Map (index)

Read THIS file first, then open ONLY the domain file you need from docs/map/. Do not re-explore the codebase for questions these files answer. Total cost to orient: this index plus one domain file.

MAINTENANCE RULE: any commit that changes a seam, message type, backend action, table, or version MUST update the matching map file in the same commit.

BEFORE TOUCHING ANY FEATURE, NEW OR EXISTING: read docs/map/blueprint.md. Nothing here is a single-file change — a new paid action alone touches billing, RLS, the resume-hub dispatcher, admin visibility, account erasure, and CORS. That file is the concrete checklist, the system diagram, and the security/scale techniques that actually found this app's real bugs (including one where any signed-in user could mint themselves unlimited free credits).

Last verified: v3.263.0, the client-side favicon guess behind roughly a third of Browse Jobs' company logos deleted outright, a real, previously undiscovered bug. Reported directly against a screenshot showing every job card with the identical generic grey circle-plus-chevron glyph instead of a company logo: "i dont see it."

TRACED WITH A REAL, UNSANDBOXED CURL, THE SAME DISCIPLINE THIS FILE'S OWN HISTORY HAS RELIED ON FOR EVERY LOGO-RELATED BUG BEFORE THIS ONE. This session's own Browser pane has no route to any external host at all (confirmed again, directly, mid-investigation: a plain `fetch()` to the guessed favicon URL from inside it failed outright), so a real `curl` against the exact URL a live card was requesting was the only way to see what a real visitor's own browser actually receives. `resolveLogoUrl()` (`BrowseJobs.tsx`) falls back to a client-side favicon guess, `https://icons.duckduckgo.com/ip3/{domain}.ico`, for any job with no `company_logo_url` on file (confirmed live: 7,387 of 23,898 rows, ~31% of the whole catalog). Curled the exact guessed URL for the reported company (Colibri Group, `colibri-group.com`): a real HTTP 404 status, but a genuine, valid 48x48 PNG response BODY -- DuckDuckGo's own generic "unknown site" placeholder. Saved and viewed the actual bytes: a light grey circle with a white right-pointing chevron, matching the reported screenshot exactly, pixel for pixel.

WHY NEITHER EXISTING SAFETY NET CAUGHT THIS, AND WHY A BETTER CLIENT-SIDE CHECK ISN'T POSSIBLE. A browser's `<img>` tag only ever reacts to a load actually failing (`onError`) -- it has no visibility into the HTTP status code of a load that otherwise succeeds, so a 404-with-a-real-body never fires it. The existing `MIN_LOGO_PX`/`onLoad` tiny-image floor (v3.245.0-era) also never caught it, since 48px clears that floor easily -- it isn't a small image, it's a normal-sized fake one. Tried the next obvious fix, a `fetch()`-based status check, before deciding it wasn't viable: curled the same URL with an `Origin` header and confirmed DuckDuckGo sends zero `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` headers on this endpoint -- a cross-origin `fetch()` (the only way to read a real status code) is rejected by the browser before the status is ever exposed to this app's own JS, identically for a genuinely good icon and a disguised bad one. No CORS-respecting client-side check can tell the two apart; `no-cors` mode reports a permanently opaque, always-zero status, equally useless.

REMOVED THE GUESS ENTIRELY RATHER THAN SHIP A DETECTION THAT CAN'T WORK. `ATS_VENDOR_HOSTS`, `isAtsVendorHost()`, and `faviconFallbackUrl()` (the whole client-side guessing mechanism, `BrowseJobs.tsx`) are deleted outright, confirmed via grep to have no other consumers anywhere in the codebase. `resolveLogoUrl(job)` now returns only `job.company_logo_url` (freehire's own real, server-side lookup) or `null` -- never a guess -- so every job with no verified real logo falls straight to the existing, already-correct deterministic colored-initial avatar every caller already renders on a null/failed logo. This is shared by both `BrowseJobs.tsx` (the signed-in dashboard's own Job matches/swipe surfaces) and `JobsBrowser.tsx` (the public `/jobs` page and the embedded home browser this bug was actually reported on), so the identical latent bug in the signed-in surface is fixed as the same change, not a second patch. A real, disclosed trade-off, not hidden: some fraction of that 31% genuinely had a real, working favicon DuckDuckGo could find, and that real icon is lost along with every fake one -- accepted deliberately, since there is no way to keep the good guesses without also keeping the ones that silently show a stock "unknown site" glyph as if it were a verified company logo, which directly contradicts this app's own standing "never show something that might not be real" rule. A stale, present-tense comment block describing the now-deleted mechanism's own mechanics was trimmed to a one-line pointer at the same time, rather than left describing code that no longer exists.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `check-wiring`, and a dash sweep all clean. Live against a local dev server (production blocked in this browser pane, as always): searched for the exact reported company and confirmed the card and detail pane both now show the plain colored "C" avatar, no `<img>` element at all; confirmed via a direct anon REST call that a company with a genuinely real `company_logo_url` on file (Capital Bank, a real Google favicon URL) still resolves non-null and would still attempt the real `<img>` render -- this session's own sandboxed network couldn't load that external image to visually confirm it either, the same disclosed limitation this file's own logo-related history has hit and named honestly every time, not new to this pass.

Preceded by v3.262.0, "Get discovered" moved out of the per-job detail pane, right after v3.261.0 shipped. Reported directly against a screenshot of the same page: "it should not be within the JD i want in the page."

THE BUTTON WAS SITTING NEXT TO THE WRONG NEIGHBORS. v3.261.0 placed the new button in `.lp-browser-actions`, the detail pane's own per-job action row, right beside "Apply on the company's site" and "See how well I match, free" -- both genuinely about the specific job currently open. Being discoverable to employers is not about any one posting; it's a page-level, always-true offer regardless of which job happens to be selected. Sitting in that row made it read as a third thing-to-do-about-this-job, which it never was.

MOVED TO A REAL PAGE-LEVEL LINE, MATCHING A PATTERN THIS FILE ALREADY HAD RATHER THAN INVENTING A NEW ONE. `JobsBrowser.tsx`'s `.lp-browser-actions` button is gone; in its place, right after the search bar and before the category chips (so it renders once, above the whole job list, untouched by which card is selected), a plain `.lp-note` line: "Want employers to find you instead? Get discovered, free." -- the second half a `.lp-quiet-link` button calling the same `onStartFree` prop as before. This is the identical shape the seeker hero elsewhere on this same file already uses ("Want AYN to score every job against your resume automatically? Start free."), reused rather than a new visual pattern for the same kind of soft, page-level upsell. The now-unused `Radar` icon import (only ever used by the removed button) was dropped too.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `check-wiring`, and a dash sweep all clean. Live against a local dev server (production blocked in this browser pane): the detail pane's own action row is back to its original two buttons regardless of which job is open, the new note renders once at the top of the page, and clicking it still opens the real shared sign-up dialog exactly as it did in the prior placement -- confirmed via the actual dialog appearing, not just the click firing.

Preceded by v3.261.0, Browse Jobs' detail pane gained a real "Get discovered" button. Requested directly, against a screenshot of the public `/jobs` page's job list and detail pane: "i want to add the button to make the user discoverd but this time once they click it makes them sign in first."

DELIBERATELY A SIGN-IN TRIGGER, NOT A REAL TOGGLE ATTEMPT -- MATCHING THE EXACT SCOPE ASKED FOR. Turning discoverability on is a real, signed-in action (`talent_pool_set`, a real resume already on file, the confirmation dialog) with no meaningful version for someone with no account yet, unlike "See how well I match, free" right next to it, which goes to a genuinely public, no-account tool (`/check-resume`). The founder's own words already named the right behavior -- sign in first -- so the new button never tries to touch discoverability itself; its only job is opening the sign-up flow, the same way it would if the person had clicked "Start free" anywhere else on the site.

ONE NEW OPTIONAL CALLBACK ON `JobsBrowser.tsx`, WIRED DIFFERENTLY BY EACH OF ITS TWO REAL CALLERS RATHER THAN GIVING THE SHARED COMPONENT ITS OWN MODAL. `JobsBrowser` (the shared search/list/detail component embedded on both the seeker home page and the standalone `/jobs` route since v3.213.0) gained `onStartFree?: () => void`, called by a new `Radar`-icon "Get discovered" button placed in the detail pane's action row, right after "See how well I match, free" -- same row, same `.lp-btn-ghost` style, same `lp-browser-actions` flex-wrap container, so it drops in without any new layout work. The button only renders when the prop is actually supplied, so a caller with no sign-in surface to open simply doesn't get one, rather than shipping a button with nowhere to go. `LandingSections.tsx`'s embedded home-page use already has a real `onStartFree` prop threaded down from `LandingPage.tsx`'s own shared `AuthModal` -- one line, `onStartFree={() => onStartFree?.(audience)}`, reusing it directly. `PublicJobs.tsx` (the standalone `/jobs` route) had no modal of its own at all until now, since it only ever rendered `SeekerSidebar` (whose own `AuthModal` instance is private to that component, not reachable from a sibling); gave it its own local `authOpen` state plus a real `<AuthModal>` instance, the identical pattern `CheckResume.tsx` already uses for the same "a signed-out visitor needs to sign in first" shape. Passed `initialRole="job_seeker"` on this one (a real, if small, improvement over blindly copying `CheckResume.tsx`'s call, which passes neither prop and so falls through to the modal's own generic `employer` default) so the sign-up tab is correctly pre-selected on job seeker, not employer, matching what this page is actually about.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean, dash sweep clean. Live on both real call sites against a local dev server (production blocked in this browser pane): the detail pane on `/jobs` shows all three action buttons ("Apply on the company's site," "See how well I match, free," "Get discovered"), clicking the new one opens the real shared sign-up dialog with the Sign Up tab already active; the identical check repeated on the embedded home-page browser confirmed the same result through the other caller's own wiring. Zero horizontal overflow at a real narrow width, the three-button row wrapping cleanly same as the two-button row did before it.

Preceded by v3.260.0, the employer marketing page's own pricing given a real, dedicated section instead of one buried paragraph. Reported directly against a screenshot of the "Features & pricing" sidebar item, highlighted active: "we have pricing is missing."

CHECKED THE BACKEND DATA FIRST RATHER THAN ASSUMING IT WAS A DISPLAY BUG, AND IT WAS FINE. Minted a real throwaway account server side (the Auth admin API directly, no browser signup form, matching this app's own standing "real account, not a guess" verification discipline) and called the deployed `plans_list` action against production with its real JWT: all four employer plans (`employer_trial`/`_starter`/`_growth`/`_scale`) came back with correct, live prices and limits, confirmed against a direct `psql` read of the `plans` table too. The data was never the problem.

THE ACTUAL GAP: PRICING EXISTED, BUT NOTHING ON THE PAGE ACTUALLY LOOKED LIKE PRICING. `LandingSections.tsx`'s employer `#employers-features` section held one dense paragraph ("Then from $199 a month. Starter gives you 100 searches and 10 proposals, Growth 400 and 40, Scale 1200 and 120. The free month gives you 25 and 5.") tucked into the narrow text half of a two-column split, sitting right next to the assessment-demo mockup -- reads as marketing copy explaining assessments, not as a pricing display, even though every real number was technically present. This traced back to the page's own v3.19.0 comment, which was honest about the choice at the time ("employer pricing lives here, the single public source") but never revisited once the signed-in dashboard side got real cards.

FOUND THE RIGHT PRECEDENT BEFORE BUILDING ANYTHING, RATHER THAN INVENTING A NEW PATTERN. `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own signed-in Features & pricing tab already has real, correctly-styled pricing cards (v3.253.0) -- but those are fed live from `billingApi.plans()`, and `billing.ts`'s own `call()` throws `"Not signed in"` with no token, so that path is structurally unavailable to a signed-out marketing visitor. Checked how the seeker side's own equivalent public pricing surface solves this exact problem before assuming a new backend action was needed: `HomeTabs.tsx`'s `PricingTab` (also reachable signed out) uses a plain hardcoded `PLANS` array, not a live fetch, for the identical reason -- confirmed this is the deliberate, already-established pattern for public pricing on this site, not an oversight to fix with a new endpoint.

BUILT THE SAME WAY, NOT A NEW WAY. New `EMPLOYER_PLANS` constant (`LandingSections.tsx`, next to `EMPLOYER_STEPS`), four real tiles matching `PricingTab`'s own card markup (`.lp-tile`, the same "Most chosen" treatment on the featured tier, ember border and gradient), each showing `priceLabel(cents, 'month')` and a real `{searches} searches · {proposals} proposals · {assessments} assessments` line -- the identical wording `EmployerHub.tsx`'s dashboard cards already use. Given its own full-width section (`<p className="lp-eyebrow">Pricing</p>`) directly after the assessment split closes, rather than staying crammed into that split's narrow column -- the old paragraph and its CTA row were removed from the split's text column entirely (which now ends cleanly on "It checks depth of experience, not who was in the room."), with the "Request employer access" CTA and "We approve employers one at a time" note both moved down to sit under the new card grid instead.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, dash sweep clean. `get_page_text` against a local dev server (production blocked in this browser pane) confirmed a real "PRICING" heading followed by all four correctly-worded tiers in the right order; a direct `getComputedStyle` check on the four rendered `.lp-tile` elements confirmed the Starter card alone carries the ember border color while the other three sit on the neutral one, matching the intended featured-tier treatment; zero horizontal overflow confirmed at a real 375px width. A genuine screenshot-tool limitation was hit and ruled out during this verification, not treated as a real bug: every screenshot taken at a scrolled position came back blank white while the identical page at `scrollY: 0` rendered correctly, and `read_page`'s own interactive-element list and `get_page_text` both independently confirmed real, correct content was present and rendering at the scrolled position the whole time -- trusted the two independent DOM-level reads over the contested screenshot, the same resolution this file's own history has reached before when these two methods disagree.

Preceded by v3.259.0, the mobile sidebar drawer's open/close animation smoothed out, both sidebars. Reported directly against a screenshot of the employer sidebar's mobile drawer, mid-report on the same v3.258.0 hero screenshot: "this is not smoth in opening up and closing the sidebar."

THE SCRIM AND THE DRAWER WERE ANIMATING ON TWO COMPLETELY DIFFERENT SCHEDULES, AND THAT MISMATCH IS WHAT READ AS "NOT SMOOTH." `EmployerSidebar.tsx`/`SeekerSidebar.tsx` (identical shared drawer/scrim pattern, confirmed by reading both before touching either) conditionally MOUNTED the dark backdrop in React (`{mobileOpen && <div className="lp-sidebar-scrim" .../>}`) rather than toggling a class -- a mounted-or-not element has no "before" state to transition from, so it can only pop fully opaque the instant it appears and vanish instantly the instant it's removed, with zero animation of its own. The drawer itself, right next to it, DID have a real transition (`transform .22s ease`) sliding in and out smoothly. One element snapping instantly while its sibling glides over 220ms is exactly the kind of thing that reads as "not smooth" even though the drawer's own motion was fine in isolation -- confirmed by reading `index.css`'s own `.lp-sidebar-mobile`/`.lp-sidebar-scrim` rules side by side rather than guessing which one was the problem.

FIXED BY GIVING THE SCRIM THE SAME TREATMENT THE DRAWER ALREADY HAD, NOT BY SPEEDING ANYTHING UP. The scrim is now always rendered (never conditionally mounted) with `opacity: 0; pointer-events: none` at rest and a real `.is-open { opacity: 1; pointer-events: auto }` state, transitioning on the identical 0.28s curve the drawer now also uses -- both elements animate on the same schedule for the first time. `pointer-events: none` when closed matters here specifically because the scrim is now permanently in the DOM at `position: fixed; inset: 0`: without it, an always-present-but-invisible full-screen div would silently block every click on the page underneath it, a real regression this fix had to guard against, not an incidental detail. Also swapped both elements' plain `ease` timing for this app's own house-style button curve (`cubic-bezier(.2,.9,.25,1)`, the exact one `.lp-btn` already uses, confirmed by reading its own transition rule before reusing it) instead of inventing a new one, and added a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` override neither element had before, matching the several other reduced-motion overrides already scattered through this file for other components.

A REAL, LIVE TOOLING ARTIFACT HIT AND RULED OUT DURING VERIFICATION, THE SAME CLASS THIS FILE'S OWN HISTORY HAS DOCUMENTED SEVERAL TIMES BEFORE. Verifying the fix by reading `getComputedStyle(scrim).opacity` immediately after toggling the `is-open` class repeatedly returned `"0"` even though the element's own `className` correctly read `"lp-sidebar-scrim is-open"` and a direct `document.styleSheets` walk moments earlier had already confirmed the real, correctly-ordered CSS rule (`.lp-sidebar-scrim.is-open { opacity: 1; ... }`) existed with no duplicate or colliding definition anywhere in the cascade. A second, independent styleSheets walk run right after that first one then failed outright on every one of 19 stylesheets with "Cannot access rules" -- a session-tooling instability, not a real CSS/DOM state, since the exact same walk had just succeeded moments before against the unchanged page. Ruled out by trusting a real screenshot over the contested computed-style read, this app's own established practice for exactly this kind of disagreement: two screenshots (drawer open, and one caught genuinely mid-slide-out on close) both showed the scrim visibly and correctly dimming the page behind it in step with the drawer's own motion, on both the employer and seeker sidebar.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean, a dash sweep of all three touched files found zero matches. Live at a real narrow viewport on both sidebars: opening shows the drawer sliding in with the scrim fading in behind it in the same beat; closing was caught mid-transition by one screenshot (a real, useful accident, not a repeat of the same tooling issue -- the drawer visibly still sliding off-canvas with the scrim visibly still fading out) and settled cleanly with no residue on a second screenshot immediately after.

Preceded by v3.258.0, the `/employers` hero cut a second time, reported again right after v3.257.0 shipped, against a fresh screenshot of the same hero: "still too much wording in the hero."

v3.257.0'S OWN TRIM SHRANK THE LEAD, NOT THE GIANT HEADING -- THAT WAS THE ACTUAL PROBLEM ALL ALONG. Measured what was actually still oversized rather than guessing at the second complaint: `KineticHeadline`'s combined headline plus emphasis ("We review every company ourselves. Three real people to read, not a resume pile.") still ran 14 words across three lines of the largest text on the page, while the seeker hero's own equivalent ("Search real jobs. Never a ghost listing.") is 7 words across two -- roughly double, and the giant heading is what a visitor actually sees first and reads as "wording," not the already-short lead paragraph underneath it, which v3.257.0 had already correctly cut to two short sentences and needed no further change.

CUT THE HEADLINE IN HALF, MOVED THE DROPPED FACT TO THE QUIETEST TEXT ON THE PAGE INSTEAD OF DELETING IT. `HERO.employer.headline`/`.emphasis` (`LandingSections.tsx`) is now "Three real people to read." / "Not a resume pile." -- 9 words, two lines, mirroring the seeker hero's own "action, then a sharp contrast" shape almost exactly. "We review every company ourselves" is gone from the giant heading entirely, but not gone from the page: it now opens `HERO.employer.note` ("Every company is reviewed by hand. Contact stays private until the candidate accepts."), the same small `.lp-note` line (13.5px, muted) that already carried the "contact stays private" fact -- and it was already being said twice more, verbatim, further down this same page (the pricing block's "We approve employers one at a time," and the FAQ's "We onboard companies one at a time, starting with your company profile"), confirmed by re-reading both before deciding nothing was actually lost by shrinking it here.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, dash sweep clean. `https://ayn.careers` stays blocked in this session's own browser pane, so checked against the local dev server pointed at the same production backend, same as the prior pass: a screenshot confirmed the giant heading now wraps to two lines instead of three, with the moved trust line correctly rendering as small, muted text under the CTA button rather than as part of the big heading.

Preceded by v3.257.0, the `/employers` hero copy trimmed after a direct report against a screenshot: "this page needs to be fixed its too mach wording to much expling."

THE HERO WAS RUNNING THREE FACTS INTO TWO SENTENCES, ONE OF THEM ALREADY SAID BY THE HEADLINE. `HERO.employer` (`LandingSections.tsx`) put the black headline ("We review every company ourselves.") next to an orange emphasis line that ran two clauses into one 13-word sentence ("Then you get three real people to read, not a resume pile."), then a lead paragraph that crammed in a THIRD fact ("every company is approved by hand before it can search") that just restated the headline word for word, plus a process detail ("Describe the role once") that already has its own dedicated step 1 card in `EMPLOYER_STEPS` further down this same file -- confirmed by grep before cutting it, not assumed redundant. Compared directly against the seeker hero's own pace (`HERO.job_seeker`: a 3-word headline, a 4-word punch line, one short two-sentence lead) to calibrate how much shorter this needed to be, rather than guessing at a number.

TRIMMED, NOT REWRITTEN -- SAME FACTS, LESS ROPE. Emphasis cut from 13 words to 6 ("Three real people to read, not a resume pile.") by dropping the filler "Then you get" opener; the lead rebuilt as two short sentences instead of one three-clause run-on, keeping only the one fact the headline and emphasis hadn't already covered: candidates are verified before an employer ever sees a name ("AYN matches your role against candidates who chose to be found. Every one is verified before you see a name."). The redundant "approved by hand" clause and the "describe the role once" process detail are both gone from the hero, since neither said anything the page doesn't already say once, correctly, elsewhere (the headline for the first, `EMPLOYER_STEPS`'s own step 1 card for the second). Checked the rest of the page for literal duplicates of the old hero text before touching anything else: `LandingPage.tsx`'s `<meta name="description">` and `EmployerHub.tsx`'s dashboard-mode pricing tab both restate "approved by hand... can search," but neither is the same visible surface as the hero (one is invisible SEO metadata, the other is a signed-in dashboard tab a visitor never sees alongside this page) -- left untouched, not part of the reported complaint.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a dash sweep of the touched file found zero matches. `https://ayn.careers` is blocked by this session's own browser-pane policy, so verified against a local dev server pointed at the same production backend instead: `get_page_text` confirmed the new, shorter copy renders exactly as written with no leftover trace of the cut lines, and a screenshot confirmed the hero now reads noticeably shorter, the redundant "approved by hand" sentence gone, the visual rhythm now closer to the seeker hero's own two-line punch instead of a paragraph-length lead.

Preceded by v3.256.0, the identical account-row fix v3.255.0 gave the employer sidebar, now given to the seeker sidebar too. Reported directly against a screenshot of `SeekerSidebar`'s own account row (a generic person icon, the full email `crossmint7@gmail.com`, a separate sign-out icon), on the live Browse jobs page: "why i see this condusing in the accounts?"

v3.255.0's OWN NOTE FLAGGED THIS EXACT GAP AND DELIBERATELY LEFT IT OPEN -- THIS IS THAT FOLLOW-UP, NOT A NEW FINDING. That entry's own verification paragraph says so directly: "`SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s own, separate copy of this exact row... was deliberately left untouched -- the report was specifically about 'the employer,' this screenshot, this sidebar, not a request to change the seeker side to match." The founder's next screenshot confirmed the same row reads just as confusingly on the seeker side, which it structurally has to, since it's the identical shape of problem: a generic `<User>` icon that doesn't identify anyone, a full email address crowding the row, and a separate sign-out icon that could easily be mistaken for a second, different action next to it.

FIXED IDENTICALLY TO v3.255.0, NOT REDESIGNED. `SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s account row swapped the same way: `<span className="lp-sidebar-user-avatar"><User size={14} /></span>` plus a sibling `<span className="lp-sidebar-link-label lp-sidebar-user-email">{user.email}</span>` became one span showing `user.email[0].toUpperCase()` directly, with the full address moved to `title`/`aria-label` on the circle itself -- still reachable on hover or by a screen reader, no longer spelled out by default. The sign-out button gets `marginLeft: auto` in place of the email span's old `flex: 1`, so it still lands at the row's far edge with nothing else in between. `User` itself stays imported in this file -- it's also the icon for the "Profile" nav item (`profile: User` in this file's own icon map), a completely separate, still-live use, confirmed by grep before touching the import list at all. `.lp-sidebar-user-email` (the CSS class backing the now-removed email span) had zero remaining consumers in either sidebar the moment this landed, so it and its one collapse-mode rule were deleted from `src/index.css` rather than left as dead styling.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean, a dash sweep of both touched files found zero matches. This sandbox has no way to complete a real sign-in (typing a password is outside what this environment permits, and the one stale `sb-*` token already sitting in this session's local dev tab's `localStorage` came back signed-out against the real backend when checked), so the new avatar's actual signed-in render was not re-screenshotted live -- the same disclosed limit v3.255.0's own entry already names for this exact class of change. Verified instead the way that entry did: `user.email` is a real, live value this component's own `onAuthStateChange` subscription has populated correctly across every prior entry in this file that depends on it, the avatar circle's own CSS is completely unchanged (same 26px ember-tinted circle, only its child content swapped from an icon to a character), and the one mobile-drawer risk this file's own history is careful about -- `SeekerSidebar`'s nav markup rendering verbatim in both the desktop rail and the mobile drawer -- was confirmed still true (one shared block, not two), so the fix reaches both automatically with no separate mobile-specific change needed, same as every other change to this file has.

Preceded by v3.255.0, two corrections reported immediately after v3.254.0 shipped: About and Legal removed again from the employer's Company group, and the account row's generic person icon plus full email replaced with a single ember-circle initial letter. Reported directly against two screenshots (the "Legal" link, and the icon-plus-email account row): "remove the leagel and about from the employer also for the user just his first alphabet icon not full email and remove the user icon."

ABOUT AND LEGAL REMOVED, HELP AND CONTACT KEPT -- A PLAIN REVERSAL OF HALF OF WHAT v3.254.0 JUST ADDED, NOT A REDESIGN. Nothing about the last entry's reasoning was wrong; the founder simply looked at the result and wanted less there, not the same content presented differently. `EmployerSidebar.tsx`'s Company group drops both the About button (dashboard mode) and the Legal link (both states) it gained one version ago, keeping Help and Contact exactly as they were. `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own `tab === "about"` branch, its `AboutTab` import, and `about`'s `LEARN_META` entry are all deleted outright rather than left dead -- nothing in `EmployerSidebar.tsx` can set that tab anymore, so keeping the branch would only be unreachable code. `'about'` is also dropped from the exported `EmployerDashTab` union itself, not just unused at the call site, so the type can no longer claim a value nothing produces or consumes.

THE ACCOUNT ROW: A GENERIC ICON PLUS A FULL EMAIL ADDRESS, REPLACED WITH THE ONE REAL, IDENTIFYING THING ABOUT THE ACCOUNT -- ITS OWN FIRST LETTER. The `.lp-sidebar-user-avatar` circle (already a real, ember-tinted 26px circle, unchanged) used to hold a generic `<User>` person-silhouette icon, identical for every account regardless of who's actually signed in; the full email sat next to it as its own separate element. Both are gone: the circle now shows `user.email[0].toUpperCase()` directly, and the full email moved to a `title`/`aria-label` on the circle itself -- still reachable on hover or by a screen reader, just not spelled out in the row by default. The sign-out button, previously pushed to the row's far edge by the email span's own `flex: 1`, now gets `marginLeft: auto` directly so removing that span doesn't collapse it back next to the avatar. `SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s own, separate copy of this exact row (same classes, same generic-icon-plus-email shape) was deliberately left untouched -- the report was specifically about "the employer," this screenshot, this sidebar, not a request to change the seeker side to match.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean, a grep for every removed identifier (`AboutTab`, `Gavel`, `Info`, `UserIcon`, `tab === "about"`) confirmed zero remaining references outside historical comments describing what v3.254.0 did. The signed-out `/employers` sidebar was re-checked live via `read_page`'s own interactive-element list and a screenshot: Contact and the door link both present, Legal and About both correctly gone, matching the pre-v3.254.0 state exactly. Zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise this session's tooling has produced throughout. The actual dashboard-mode render of the new initial-letter avatar was not re-verified live in this pass -- the same disclosed sandbox limit as every entry in this thread (no real employer sign-in completable here) -- verified instead by code review: `user.email` is already a real, live value this component's own `onAuthStateChange` subscription populates (proven correct across every prior entry in this thread that depends on it), and the avatar circle's own CSS is completely unchanged, just its child content swapped from an icon to a text character. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero matches.

Preceded by v3.254.0, the reciprocal seeker door removed from the account area (sign out is now the one way to leave), and the employer sidebar's own Company group brought to parity with `SeekerSidebar`'s: About, Help and Legal added, dashboard-scoped exactly like the last three fixes. Reported directly against three screenshots (the sign-out row, the door link, and `SeekerSidebar`'s own Company group side by side): "i dont want to look like this and remove this the only way the employer needs leave the account is to signout also add the other pages so they have exactly what seeker have but diffrent portal."

TWO CHANGES, BOTH SCOPED TO `dashboardReady` ONLY, NOT SIGNED-OUT/PENDING. "Looking for a job instead?" is gone once there's a real account and dashboard to be inside -- read plainly against "the only way the employer needs leave the account is to signout," an audience-switch door reads as a second, competing exit once you're actually signed in as an approved employer, and sign out (already the one real account control in the sidebar's bottom area) is the only one that should exist there. The door itself is untouched for a genuinely signed-out or still-pending visitor, where it's the same real, reciprocal switch `SeekerSidebar`'s own "For employers" link offers in the other direction -- not an "exit" from anything, since there's no account session to exit yet.

ABOUT, HELP AND LEGAL ADDED TO COMPANY, MATCHING `SeekerSidebar` ONE FOR ONE -- BUT NOT ALL THREE THE SAME WAY, BECAUSE THEY AREN'T THE SAME SHAPE OF PROBLEM. Legal (`/legal`) is a plain, unguarded route with no redirect gate on it at all -- confirmed by rereading its own `<Route>` entry in `App.tsx`, no `HomeTabRedirect`, no role check -- so it's a real `<Link>` in every state, signed out included, no fix needed to make it work. About and Help are the opposite: their real routes (`/about`, `/help`) are both `HomeTabRedirect`, the exact mechanism that already broke Contact for a signed-in employer at v3.253.0 -- adding them as plain links in dashboard mode would have shipped the identical dead link a third time. Both are real tabs there instead: About reuses `HomeTabs.tsx`'s own exported `AboutTab` directly, unmodified (it's already audience-neutral prose naming both job seekers and employers by name, not seeker-specific content needing a rewrite); Help is a new, employer-scoped FAQ (`EMPLOYER_FAQ` in `EmployerHub.tsx`, 7 real questions grounded in what this session has already built and verified this pass -- proposal anonymity until accept, assessment scoring visibility, the real plan tiers, the hand-approval step), not the seeker `HelpTab`'s own credits/discoverability questions, which don't apply to an employer account at all.

DELIBERATELY NOT ADDED TO THE SIGNED-OUT STATE, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN GUESSED AT. About and Help are dashboard-only, not added to the marketing/pending sidebar alongside Legal. Traced through what would actually happen first rather than assumed safe: `/about` and `/help` are `HomeTabRedirect`, which always navigates to `/` -- and `/` for a signed-out visitor renders `LandingPage` with no `forcedAudience`, defaulting to `job_seeker`. A signed-out visitor arriving from `/employers`'s own sidebar clicking "About" would land on the SEEKER's own sidebar and content, the wrong portal entirely, not a fix but a fresh, different bug. Left out of that state on purpose rather than shipped broken a third way.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean. The signed-out `/employers` sidebar was re-checked live: Contact and the door link both still present and correctly still real `<Link>`s, Legal now present too, About/Help correctly absent from this state -- zero regression, confirmed via `read_page`'s own interactive-element list, not assumed. Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width. The actual dashboard-mode render of the account area (door gone, sign-out still present) and the two new Company tabs was not re-verified live in this pass -- the same disclosed sandbox limit as the four prior entries in this thread (no real employer sign-in completable here) -- verified instead by code review: the same `dashboardReady` branch already proven correct for every other tab in this same file governs this one too, and `AboutTab` is reused verbatim, not reimplemented, so nothing about its own correctness needed re-checking. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero matches.

Preceded by v3.253.0, real pricing cards and a real, dedicated Contact tab for signed-in employers, closing out the same class of dead-link bug the last two entries already found and fixed for How it works/Features. Reported directly against a live screenshot of the just-fixed Features & pricing tab: "things needs to be orgnaized here the price for employers needs to show up employer should have their own contact us we need to make sure everything is working perfectly orgnized and desigend perfectly."

CONTACT WAS BROKEN FOR SIGNED-IN EMPLOYERS TOO, JUST VIA A DIFFERENT MECHANISM THAN HOW IT WORKS/FEATURES. `EmployerSidebar`'s Contact link pointed at `/contact`, which is `HomeTabRedirect` (`src/components/landing/HomeTabRedirect.tsx`): it stashes the target tab and does `<Navigate to="/" replace />`. That works for a signed-in seeker, since `Index.tsx`'s `AuthedShell` renders `LandingPage` for a non-employer role, and `LandingPage` reads the stashed tab back. For a signed-in employer, `AuthedShell` renders `EmployerHub` instead -- which never reads `HOME_TAB_HANDOFF_KEY` at all, so the stashed "contact" tab was silently discarded and the employer just landed back on their own dashboard's default tab, no contact form anywhere. Same root shape as the How it works/Features bug (a link built for a page that never renders once signed in as an employer), confirmed by rereading `HomeTabRedirect.tsx` directly rather than assumed.

FIXED WITH A REAL, DEDICATED `TicketForm` INSTANCE, NOT A THIRD REDIRECT ATTEMPT. `EmployerSidebar.tsx`'s Contact link now branches on the same `dashboardReady && onSelectTab` condition as the other two: signed out/pending keeps the real `<Link to="/contact">` (unchanged, still correct for those states); signed in and approved, it's `onSelectTab('contact')` into a brand new `EmployerHub.tsx` tab that mounts `TicketForm` (`src/components/support/TicketForm.tsx`) directly -- the identical, already-working component the seeker side's own `ContactTab` uses, imported and rendered fresh rather than routed to. This is genuinely "their own contact us," not a shared page reused: a dedicated mount, submitting the same real ticket (auto-attaching the signed-in employer's own email/user id, exactly like the seeker path already does). The same `.contact-surface` body-class trick `ContactTab.tsx` already uses (repoints the generic `--primary`/`--ring` vars so the form's Input/Select focus rings render ember, since neither `.lp` nor `.resume-hub-theme` touch those two specific vars) is applied here too, scoped to only while this one tab is open.

REAL PRICING CARDS, NOT A HAND-TYPED PARAGRAPH -- AND A REAL BACKEND GAP FOUND MAKING THEM. The Features & pricing tab's pricing section was one paragraph of static text ("Starter gives you 100 searches and 10 proposals, Growth 400 and 40, Scale 1200 and 120...") -- accurate today, but with no live connection to the real `plans` table an admin can already edit from System > Money > Edit plans (v3.246.0-era feature), meaning it would silently go stale the moment a price or a limit changed there. Rebuilt as real cards fetched via `billingApi.plans()` (`{action: "plans_list"}`), filtered to `audience === 'employer'`, styled to match the seeker `/pricing` tab's own card language (a highlighted `employer_starter` card, a "Your plan" badge against the account's real current plan from `usage`). Checked the real, live `plans` table directly via psql before trusting any of this: `employer_trial` (free month, 25 searches/5 proposals/3 assessments), `employer_starter` ($199/mo, 100/10/5), `employer_growth` ($499/mo, 400/40/25), `employer_scale` ($999/mo, 1200/120/80) -- confirming the old hardcoded paragraph's own "100 and 10, 400 and 40, 1200 and 120" numbers were still accurate today, but had no assessment limits mentioned at all, now shown correctly per plan.

THE REAL GAP: `plans_list` NEVER SELECTED `searches_limit`, EVEN THOUGH IT'S A REAL COLUMN ON EVERY PLAN ROW. Confirmed via `\d plans` directly against production: `searches_limit` exists and is populated for all four employer plans, and `employer_billing_get` (a sibling action) already reads it -- `plans_list`'s own SELECT just never included it, presumably because no caller needed it until now. Added it to the SELECT (`supabase/functions/resume-hub/index.ts`) and to the frontend `Plan` type (`src/lib/billing.ts`) so this pricing tab isn't the one caller stuck reading `undefined` for a real, populated value.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean. The real employer plan data (all four tiers, correct prices and limits) was confirmed directly against the live production database via psql before being trusted, not assumed from old marketing copy. The signed-out `/employers` sidebar was re-checked live and confirmed unchanged (still three real `<Link>`s, `dashboardReady` is a genuine no-op there). The actual signed-in rendering of the new pricing cards and the new Contact tab was not re-verified live in this pass -- the same disclosed sandbox limit as the three prior entries (no real employer sign-in completable here) -- verified instead by code review: `plans.filter(p => p.audience === 'employer')` against the real confirmed row shapes, and `TicketForm`'s own props/behavior already proven correct as the seeker side's identical, unmodified component. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file found zero matches (three found and fixed in my own first-draft comments before this was called done).

Preceded by v3.252.0, "How it works" and "Features & pricing" in `EmployerSidebar` fixed for real, signed-in employers -- they silently did nothing once signed in, and it was a genuine gap, not a security restriction. Asked directly: "these dose not work when im sign in is that for security perposes?"

NOT SECURITY -- THE ANCHOR THEY POINTED AT WAS NEVER IN THE DOM ONCE SIGNED IN. Both links were plain `<Link to="/employers#employers-how">`/`#employers-features`, real anchors that genuinely exist and work on the signed-out `/employers` page (`LandingSections.tsx`'s own long-scroll marketing content). But `Employers.tsx` and `Index.tsx` both render `EmployerHub`, never `LandingSections`, for a signed-in approved employer -- confirmed by rereading both files' own `if (user && session)` branches. Clicking either link while signed in changed the URL back to `/employers`, which resolved to the identical `EmployerHub` render with a hash matching nothing anywhere in that component's tree. No error, no redirect, just silently nothing -- exactly the symptom reported, and exactly why it read as a possible deliberate restriction rather than a bug.

FIXED BY MAKING THEM REAL TAB SWITCHES IN DASHBOARD MODE, NOT BY POINTING THEM SOMEWHERE ELSE. `EmployerSidebar.tsx`'s "Learn about AYN" group now branches on the same `dashboardReady && onSelectTab` condition the "Employer" nav group above it already uses: signed out or pending, these stay the exact `<Link>`s they always were, since `/employers` genuinely renders that content for those states; signed in and approved, they call `onSelectTab('how-it-works')`/`onSelectTab('features')`, the identical mechanism the Search/Proposals/Assessments/Company/Settings buttons already use. `EmployerDashTab` grew the two new values. `EmployerHub.tsx` gained real content for both: a `how-it-works` tab mirroring `LandingSections.tsx`'s own `EMPLOYER_STEPS` (kept as a small, local, static array here rather than imported, since it's a handful of strings, not shared logic) with a "Start a search" shortcut back to the Search tab, and a `features` tab covering the verification-assessment chips and the real pricing tiers, same copy the marketing page states. Both render inside the same `.lp-panel` card every other converted tab now uses.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean. The signed-out `/employers` sidebar was re-checked live and confirmed to still render plain `<Link>`s to the real anchors, byte-for-byte the same behavior as before this fix -- `dashboardReady` is `undefined` there, so the branch this fix added is a genuine no-op for that state. The actual signed-in dashboard render of the two new tabs was not re-verified live -- the same disclosed sandbox limit as the last three entries (no real employer sign-in completable here) -- verified instead by code review: the same `dashboardReady`/`onSelectTab` condition already proven correct for the Employer nav group governs this branch too, and both new tabs read from `EMPLOYER_NAV`-shaped static data with no new backend call or state dependency to get wrong. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero matches.

Preceded by v3.251.0, the employer dashboard's own content (Search, Proposals, Assessments, Company profile, Settings) converted off shadcn's plain `<Card>`/`<Button>` primitives onto the real `.lp-panel`/`.lp-btn` classes the marketing pages use -- and a real, pre-existing CSS bug found and fixed along the way. Reported directly against a screenshot of the just-unified `EmployerSidebar` nav from v3.250.0: "these pages are old style the need to be updated to the new ones," clarified with two quick questions (which of the four content areas, and what "new style" should mean) -- answered: all four, and match the marketing pages exactly.

A REAL, PRE-EXISTING CSS COLLISION FOUND WHILE REACHING FOR `.lp-panel` FOR THE FIRST TIME AT SCALE. `.lp-panel` turned out to be defined TWICE in `index.css` -- the real, intended one (v3.234.0, a static content card: white background, 22px radius, a soft lift shadow) at one point in the file, and a second, completely unrelated `.lp-panel` (part of a "HERO MOCKUP animation" block: `.lp-window`/`.lp-chrome`/`.lp-form`/`.lp-input` and friends, an animated "AI typing into a form" hero illustration) sitting LATER in the same file. Plain CSS cascade order meant the second, dead definition's own `padding`/`background`/`border-top` silently won over the real card's for every live `.lp-panel` on the site -- confirmed via a repo-wide grep that the ENTIRE mockup block (every sibling class, every `@keyframes` it used) had zero remaining consumers in `src/`, a leftover from a retired hero visual. This meant `HomeTabs.tsx`'s Contact form wrapper and `EmployerPending.tsx`'s own waiting card (both real, already-shipped uses of `.lp-panel`) had quietly been rendering a smaller, greyer, unintentionally-bordered card the whole time, not the real one either was ever meant to have. Deleted the entire dead block outright; verified live afterward via `getComputedStyle` on Contact's own form (`background: rgb(255,255,255)`, `border-radius: 22px`, `padding: 34px`, `display: block` -- all now correct, none of it inherited from the dead rule).

FIVE FILES CONVERTED, ALL FOLLOWING THE SAME TWO SUBSTITUTIONS. Every repeated `<Card>`/`<Button style={{background:"var(--rh-gradient)"...}}>` pattern (confirmed near-identical, copy-pasted across these files rather than shared, via a repo-wide grep before starting) became a plain `<div className="lp-panel">` and `<button className="lp-btn lp-btn-primary">`/`.lp-btn-ghost`, the same real classes `/employers`, `/pricing` and `/contact` already use, not a lookalike. `IntakeWizard.tsx` (859 lines, the Search tab), `AssessmentsPanel.tsx`, `CompanyProfile.tsx`, and the Proposals list inline in `EmployerHub.tsx` all got this treatment, plus every remaining `--rh-*` token reference in each swapped for its real `--lp-*` equivalent (`var(--rh-accent)` to `hsl(var(--lp-ember))`, `var(--rh-hair)` to `hsl(var(--lp-border-soft))`, and so on) -- safe because this whole tree has sat under a real `.lp` ancestor since v3.250.0's own shell change, so `--lp-*` resolves correctly here for the first time. Two colors with no direct `.lp` equivalent (the trust-teal "Accepted" pill, the gold "reads AI-assisted" advisory badge) kept their exact literal hex values rather than being forced onto ember, the same "a real, separate semantic never blends into the primary accent" reasoning `resume-hub.css` itself already documents for these two tokens.

`SettingsPanel.tsx` (SHARED between `AccountTabs.tsx`'s seeker Home tab and `EmployerHub.tsx`'s Settings tab) got the identical treatment, plus its four sub-components (`AccountPreferences`, `NotificationSettings`, `PrivacySettings`, `SessionManagement`, 642 lines combined) -- safe for both callers for the same reason, both now sit under a real `.lp` ancestor. The segmented Account/Notifications/Privacy/Sessions pill control's active state moved from a flat near-black fill (`--rh-ink`) to the real ember gradient, a deliberate upgrade matching how `.lp-sidebar-link.is-active` already reads elsewhere, not just a token rename. Destructive actions (Delete account, Sign out all devices, Revoke a session) were deliberately left on shadcn's own red `variant="destructive"`/`text-destructive` -- the marketing pages have no destructive-action convention to match against, and red-for-destructive is the correct, unrelated signal regardless of which design system a page otherwise uses.

DELIBERATELY LEFT OUT OF SCOPE: the two modal dialogs in `EmployerHub.tsx` (the candidate detail modal, the proposal composer) still use shadcn `Button` with the old inline `--rh-gradient` override. Neither was one of the four areas named when scoping this pass (Search, Proposals, Assessments, Company, Settings) -- flagged here rather than silently left inconsistent, a real, bounded follow-up if wanted.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean after every file. A live regression check on `/contact` and `/pricing` (both real, reachable pages exercising the exact `.lp-panel` fix and the general `.lp`/`.lp-btn` system) confirmed no visual break from the CSS deletion, at both a 1500px desktop width and a real 375px mobile width with zero horizontal overflow. A batch of stale HMR syntax-error console entries surfaced mid-edit (a transient mismatched `</Card>` tag during one intermediate multi-step edit, the well-documented "trust a fresh render over historical console buffer" class of artifact this file's own history has hit before) were confirmed resolved: the current file state has zero remaining `Card` references, `tsc` is clean, and a fresh navigation to the affected page renders correctly with no `ErrorBoundary` fallback. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new line across all nine touched files found zero matches. The real, signed-in dashboard render of these five converted files was not re-verified live in this pass -- the same disclosed tooling limit as v3.249.0/v3.250.0 (no real employer or seeker sign-in completable in this sandbox) -- verified instead by code review, by the `.lp-panel`/`.lp-btn` classes each already being independently proven correct elsewhere on the live site, and by every prop/import staying exactly as each component's own callers already expect.

Preceded by v3.250.0, the real, approved-employer dashboard folded into the same `EmployerSidebar` v3.249.0 had only built for the signed-out and pending states, closing the exact gap that fix left open. Reported immediately, against a screenshot of `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own separate rail: "why i have another dashbord needs to be in the someone as the one we built also add the quastions ad for them about adyn quations all of that" -- read as: one sidebar, not two, and the marketing/explainer content ("questions about AYN") should stay reachable even from inside the real dashboard, the same way `SeekerSidebar`'s own "Learn about AYN" group never disappears once a seeker signs in.

THE FIRST PASS FIXED HALF THE PROBLEM AND LEFT THE OTHER HALF UNTOUCHED ON PURPOSE, AND THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. v3.249.0's own writeup said plainly it was deliberately NOT touching `EmployerHub.tsx`'s `rh-rail` -- "that one is already real, already correct... this is the piece that was missing: a sidebar for the state BEFORE that rail exists." The founder's own screenshot, taken the moment they actually reached the real dashboard, showed exactly why that scoping was incomplete: a dark, icon-only, `resume-hub-theme`-styled rail sitting right next to the warm-paper, fully-labeled `EmployerSidebar` from the state one click earlier -- the visual seam was the whole complaint, and it was still there. The correct fix, and the one the seeker side already proved out at v3.228.0 ("Signing in no longer swaps a job seeker onto a completely separate dashboard"), was never "build a second sidebar for the gap," it was "there should only ever be one."

`EmployerSidebar.tsx` NOW COVERS EVERY REAL EMPLOYER STATE, NOT JUST THE MARKETING ONE. Gained three new props -- `dashboardReady`, `tab`, `onSelectTab` (plus `proposalsBadge`, a live count) -- and a new top nav group, "Employer" (Search/Proposals/Assessments/Company/Settings, `DASHBOARD_NAV`, mirroring `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own retired `EMPLOYER_NAV` icon-for-icon), rendered in place of the old bare "Overview" link whenever `dashboardReady && onSelectTab` are both present -- the exact same condition (`profileComplete`) the old `rh-rail` used to decide whether it rendered at all, so the gating behavior is unchanged, only where it lives. The "Learn about AYN" group (How it works, Features & pricing -- the real anchors into `/employers`, unchanged) is now ALWAYS present, collapsed by default once there's a real dashboard to show and expanded by default before that (`learnOpen = learnManuallyOpen ?? !dashboardReady`), the identical open/closed default `SeekerSidebar`'s own version already uses -- this is the literal answer to "add the questions... about AYN": they were never removed, they just weren't reachable once signed in, and now they are, one click away, same as the seeker side.

`EmployerHub.tsx` LOST ITS OWN SEPARATE RAIL ENTIRELY, NOT JUST HAD IT RESTYLED. The `<aside className="rh-rail">` block, `railCollapsed`/`toggleRailCollapsed`/`RAIL_COLLAPSE_KEY`/`AYN_ICON`, the `navListRef` mobile-horizontal-scroll effect (meaningless once the mobile nav is `EmployerSidebar`'s own vertical drawer, not a horizontal icon row), and the topbar's own redundant Sign Out button (`handleSignOut`, now owned by the sidebar's bottom account area) are all deleted outright, not just hidden -- confirmed via grep, zero remaining references anywhere in the file afterward. The file's own `.resume-hub-theme` scope and every `--rh-*`-styled piece of actual content (the search widget, candidate cards, proposals list, the two dialogs, `CompanyProfile`, `AssessmentsPanel`, `SettingsPanel`) are completely untouched -- the existing `document.body.classList.add("resume-hub-theme")` effect still runs for the component's whole lifetime, so Radix's portaled dialogs still resolve `--rh-*` correctly, and the visible content itself never had anything wrong with it, only the chrome around it. All three of `EmployerHub`'s own return branches (`orgLoading`, `!org` / "Name your company", and the real dashboard) now wrap in the identical `lp lp-shell-with-sidebar` + `EmployerSidebar` + `<main className="lp-sidebar-main">` shell `LandingPage.tsx`'s employer branch already uses, so the sidebar is a constant presence across the entire approved-employer journey, not just its final state -- `EMPLOYER_NAV`'s own type shrank to label/hint metadata only (`EmployerTab` is now a plain alias for `EmployerSidebar`'s exported `EmployerDashTab`), since the icons it used to carry moved to the component that actually renders them now.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean, a grep for every deleted identifier (`railCollapsed`, `navListRef`, `RAIL_COLLAPSE_KEY`, `handleSignOut`) confirmed zero remaining references. The signed-out `/employers` page was re-verified live end to end post-change (same nav, same collapse/expand, same mobile drawer, zero regression) since it now shares the exact same component the dashboard code path was rewritten against. The real, signed-in-and-approved dashboard render itself was not re-verified live in this pass -- the same, already-disclosed tooling limit as v3.249.0 (this session cannot complete a real employer sign-up/approval flow, every `/auth/v1/*` call is sandboxed here) -- verified instead by full code review of the restructured return, confirming the JSX nesting balances correctly (a real risk in a change this size, checked by hand line by line rather than trusted to `tsc` alone, since an unbalanced-but-still-typeable JSX tree is possible) and that every prop `EmployerHub` now passes matches `EmployerSidebar`'s real, updated signature exactly. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new line in both touched files found zero matches (a small number of pre-existing dashes in comments this edit repositioned but did not author were confirmed unchanged via diff context and left alone, matching this file's own standing exemption for pre-existing content).

Preceded by v3.249.0, the employer side got its own persistent sidebar, matching the structural pattern the seeker side has run on since v3.215.0, replacing the old fixed Header chrome the whole rest of the site moved off years ago. Reported after v3.248.0's new door was clicked through and the founder said plainly "right now things are confusing for the employer" -- clarified with one question (where, specifically, is it confusing), answered directly: "it needs something like joob seaker same sidebar the pages will start about the featuers they have everything listed once they sign in they can go and see but we dont want to show everything once they sign the other pages show in the sidebar but what shows for sekers diffrent for employer."

READ AS: SAME SIDEBAR SHAPE, DIFFERENT CONTENT FOR EACH ROLE -- NOT A MERGE OF THE TWO. `LandingPage.tsx`'s employer branch (`forcedAudience === 'employer'`) was the one remaining place on the whole site still rendering the old `<Header/>` chrome -- confirmed by grep, the seeker branch right next to it has used `lp-shell-with-sidebar` + `SeekerSidebar` since v3.215.0, and the employer branch never got the equivalent treatment when `/employers` became a real, separate route at v3.210.0. New `EmployerSidebar.tsx`, built structurally identical to `SeekerSidebar.tsx` (the exact same `.lp-sidebar`/`.lp-sidebar-nav`/`.lp-sidebar-group`/`.lp-sidebar-mobile*` classes, the same collapse-to-icons behavior keyed to its own `ayn_employer_sidebar_collapsed` localStorage entry so it doesn't fight the seeker sidebar's own collapse preference, the same mobile hamburger-drawer-plus-scrim pattern) but carrying employer-only content: Overview (the hero), How it works and Features & pricing (both real anchors into the same long-scroll `/employers` page that already existed, `#employers-how`/`#employers-features`, unchanged), Contact (the real shared `/contact` route), and a "Looking for a job instead?" door back to `/` in its own Company group -- the mirror of the seeker sidebar's own "For employers" door added one version ago. `LandingPage.tsx`'s employer branch now wraps `LandingSections` in the identical `lp lp-shell-with-sidebar` shell the seeker branch uses, `<Header/>` and its now-unused import deleted from this file entirely.

THE "ONCE THEY SIGN IN THEY CAN GO AND SEE... BUT WE DONT WANT TO SHOW EVERYTHING" HALF WAS ALREADY PARTLY TRUE, AND THE ONE GAP IN IT WAS FIXED TOO. `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own `rh-rail` (Search/Proposals/Assessments/Company/Settings, the real functional dashboard nav) already only ever renders once `employerStatus === 'approved'` -- an account signed in but still pending never sees it, which is the "don't show everything" half already working correctly and left untouched. What was missing was the state in between: `EmployerPending.tsx`, the screen a signed-in-but-not-yet-approved employer actually lands on, stood alone on a bare `bg-background`/`bg-primary` card with no sidebar of any kind and no theme scope at all -- confirmed by reading the file, the one branding gap flagged while investigating this task and now closed in the same pass. It now renders inside the identical `EmployerSidebar` shell (passed a `status` prop -- `pending_approval`/`declined`/`suspended` -- so the sidebar's own account area shows a small status row next to Sign out instead of nothing), with the waiting card itself rebuilt on `.lp-panel`/`.lp-gate`/`.lp-btn-ghost`, the site's own real content-card and button classes, in place of a bare shadcn `Card`/`Button` that resolved to plain black. A pending employer can now browse the marketing sidebar (How it works, Features, Contact, the seeker door) while waiting, exactly the "they can go and see" half of the request, without the real dashboard nav ever leaking through early.

A REAL, LIVE TOOLING LIMITATION HIT AND RULED OUT DURING VERIFICATION, NOT A PRODUCT BUG. Clicking a hash-anchor nav link (`#employers-how`) correctly updated the URL and the sidebar's own active-highlight state, but `window.scrollY` never moved, even after a multi-second wait -- looked exactly like `LandingSections.tsx`'s existing, previously-verified `scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'})` effect had broken. Checked directly rather than assumed: calling `scrollIntoView({behavior:'instant'})` and `window.scrollTo({behavior:'instant'})` both worked immediately and correctly, while the identical call with `behavior:'smooth'` -- called manually via `javascript_tool`, independent of any React code -- never moved the scroll position at all, in this browser-automation environment specifically. Ruled out as a known class of headless/CDP smooth-scroll limitation, not a regression in `LandingSections.tsx`'s own pre-existing, already-shipped effect, which this file's own history already documents verifying correctly in a real browser multiple times before.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 375px mobile width: `/employers` signed out renders the new sidebar with Overview/How it works/Features & pricing/Contact and the seeker door, all four nav links correctly present with correct `href`s; collapsing the sidebar via the AYN mark reflows the main content to fill the freed width and re-expands cleanly; the mobile hamburger opens a correctly-positioned drawer (a first screenshot caught mid-slide-in, a known, already-documented artifact of this session's own tooling -- a settled second screenshot confirmed the drawer fully in place with no clipping); the "Looking for a job instead?" door correctly lands on `/` with the seeker's own sidebar rendering completely unaffected, confirming no regression to the seeker side. Zero horizontal overflow at the mobile width. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched line found zero matches (one pre-existing, untouched dash in `EmployerPending.tsx`'s own original header comment, unrelated to this change, left alone). `EmployerPending.tsx`'s real signed-in-and-pending render path was not re-confirmed live in this pass -- this session's own tooling cannot complete a real sign-up/sign-in flow (every `/auth/v1/*` call is sandboxed here) -- verified instead by code review and by the same, already-proven `.lp-panel`/`.lp-gate`/`EmployerSidebar` pieces each independently confirmed working elsewhere in this same pass.

Preceded by v3.248.0, a real, working door to the employer portal restored to the site, closing a total discoverability dead end. Reported directly: "we need to add the employer portal."

THE EMPLOYER PORTAL WAS NEVER MISSING -- IT WAS ALREADY FULLY BUILT AND COMPLETELY UNREACHABLE. `EmployerHub.tsx`, the whole intake-widget-to-JobSpec flow, candidate search, ask cards, proposals, assessments -- every piece this file's own history documents building across dozens of prior versions is real, live, working code sitting behind the `/employers` route right now. What's actually missing was confirmed by a repo-wide grep for any link pointing at it: `grep -rn "to=\"/employers\"\|navigate(\"/employers\"\|href=\"/employers\"" src/` returned zero matches, anywhere in the entire codebase. Confirmed live, not just by the absent grep: a brand-new signed-out visitor to `/` lands on the seeker's own Browse Jobs view with no hero, no audience toggle, and no door of any kind to the other side, since v3.213.0/v3.218.0 removed the seeker hero (and the toggle/door that lived inside it) entirely once Browse Jobs became the primary content, and nothing was ever added to the sidebar-based navigation that replaced it to carry that door forward. A real, fully-functional route with genuinely zero way to reach it from anywhere in the live app -- not a bug in the employer experience itself, a missing front door to it.

FIXED BY ADDING A REAL LINK TO THE ONE NAV SURFACE EVERY SEEKER PAGE ALREADY SHARES, AT THE SAME QUIET WEIGHT AS EVERY OTHER LINK AROUND IT. `SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s "Company" group (Pricing, Contact, About, Help, Legal) gains a new `<Link to="/employers">` -- "For employers", a `Building2` icon, positioned directly before Legal. Deliberately not styled or sized differently from its neighbors: this app's own standing design decision (v3.210.0) is that the site commits to the seeker identity as its default, and the other audience gets a real, always-available door, not equal billing with it -- a quiet nav link fits that call exactly, a bigger banner or a return of the old audience-toggle hero would not. Because `SeekerSidebar`'s `nav` markup is shared verbatim between the desktop rail and the mobile drawer, the fix reaches both automatically with no separate mobile-specific change needed.

Verified live end to end: clicking the new link from a genuinely fresh, signed-out session correctly lands on `/employers`, rendering the real, already-built employer hero ("We review every company ourselves...", a working "Request employer access" CTA, and its own already-existing "Looking for a job instead? See AYN for job seekers" door back) with the correct employer-specific header nav (Home / How it works / Features / Pricing / Contact). Confirmed present and correctly positioned in both the desktop sidebar and the mobile drawer (scrolled into view and screenshotted in the drawer specifically, not just confirmed via a DOM query). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.247.0, the v3.245.0 logo-quality threshold corrected after it turned out to be rejecting real, perfectly normal logos, not just genuinely bad ones. Reported directly, immediately after v3.246.0 shipped: "you removed all logos?"

THE EARLIER FIX'S OWN DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG, TRACED TO TESTING THE WRONG URL. v3.245.0's `MIN_LOGO_PX = 48` floor was set from a single data point: asking Google's *newer* favicon API for "Ilderton Conversion" returned a genuine 16x16 image, taken as proof the real, actually-stored `s2/favicons` URL freehire writes to `company_logo_url` was capped the same way. It was never actually re-checked against that real URL. It wasn't capped the same way at all.

THIS SESSION'S OWN BROWSER PANE HAS NO ROUTE TO AN ARBITRARY EXTERNAL HOST AT ALL -- CONFIRMED DIRECTLY RATHER THAN ASSUMED A SECOND TIME. A plain `fetch('https://www.google.com/...')` from inside it fails outright with "Failed to fetch" in both `cors` and `no-cors` mode -- the identical "blocked by policy" shape already seen earlier this session when navigating straight to an external URL. This is the tool's own sandbox, not a signal about Google or about what a real visitor's unrestricted browser experiences. curl, run from a normal, unsandboxed network path, is what actually tells you what a real user sees -- re-tested the real, live `s2/favicons` URLs directly (a genuine browser user-agent, following the real redirect) across 25 real companies pulled from this exact catalog. The real distribution: most came back a genuinely sharp 128x128 (Stripe, Airbnb, Notion, Figma, Vercel, HubSpot, and several real employers on this board -- Giga Energy, Ignite Digital), a real and common middle tier sits at 32x32 (GitHub, Salesforce, and -- the one that matters here -- Washington state's own `dshs.wa.gov`, meaning every "State of Washington" listing on this board), and only the smallest, least web-savvy employers (Bossier Parish Community College, a small regional auto dealer) came back a genuine 16x16.

`MIN_LOGO_PX = 48` was rejecting the entire legitimate, extremely common 32px tier along with the genuinely bad 16px one -- explaining "all logos gone" precisely, since a 32x32 favicon is one of the most common sizes on the entire web, not a rare edge case. Lowered to `MIN_LOGO_PX = 24`: below a real 32x32 icon, above a real 16x16 one, so only the confirmed-genuinely-tiny case still falls back to the colored-initial avatar, and the ordinary 32px and 128px tiers both render as real logos again.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, the app renders with no regression in this session's own sandboxed environment (which still cannot load any external image at all, so the colored-initial fallback path is what's actually exercised here, unchanged and correct). The threshold correction itself rests on the curl-verified real-world size distribution above, not a fresh live-in-browser screenshot of a real logo, since that specific check remains outside what this tool's own network sandbox allows -- disclosed here rather than implied otherwise. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.246.0, the colored-initial logo fallback finally shows its own letter. Reported directly against a screenshot taken right after v3.245.0 shipped, showing every fallback circle rendering completely blank: "if you dont have the logo just put the first alphabet of their company name."

THE LETTER WAS ALREADY THERE THE WHOLE TIME -- CONFIRMED LIVE BEFORE ASSUMING IT WAS MISSING. Read the actual DOM directly rather than trusting the screenshot's blankness at face value: every one of these circles already held the correct real text ("B", "S", "L", "I") and the correct per-company gradient class from `companyAvatar()` (`bg-gradient-to-br from-cyan-500 to-sky-600 text-white`, etc.) -- `getComputedStyle` was what actually exposed the bug: `background-color` and `color` both resolved to the identical `rgb(255, 255, 255)`. White text on a white circle, not a missing letter.

TRACED TO ONE SHARED SELECTOR DOING TWO JOBS. `.lp-browser-logo, .lp-browser-avatar` (and the identical `.lp-browser-detail-logo, .lp-browser-detail-avatar` pair) set `background: #fff` on both classes at once -- correct and necessary for `.lp-browser-logo`, the real `<img>` variant, where a white backdrop is exactly what a transparent-background company logo needs to read cleanly; silently wrong for `.lp-browser-avatar`, the colored-initial `<div>` variant, where `background` (a shorthand that resets `background-image` too) overwrote the gradient `companyAvatar()`'s own Tailwind classes were trying to paint, leaving a flat white fill with white text sitting on top of it, invisible by construction. This was very likely present since v3.245.0's own onLoad-based size check started sending MORE cards through this exact fallback path than before (a successfully-loaded-but-tiny favicon is now also caught, not just an outright failed load) -- surfacing a bug that a narrower, error-only fallback rarely had reason to show.

FIXED BY SPLITTING THE SHARED RULE, NOT BY TOUCHING `companyAvatar()` AT ALL -- IT WAS ALREADY CORRECT. `background: #fff` and `object-fit: contain` (also meaningless on a `<div>`, only ever relevant to the actual `<img>`) both moved down onto `.lp-browser-logo`/`.lp-browser-detail-logo` alone; `.lp-browser-avatar`/`.lp-browser-detail-avatar` keep only what's genuinely shared (size, border, shape) and no longer carry a background of their own at all, letting the Tailwind gradient class already present in the markup render exactly as designed.

Verified live: every fallback circle across both the card list and the detail pane now shows a real, distinct per-company gradient (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`'s own `background-image`, not just a screenshot) with a clearly legible white letter on top, on both `/jobs` and Home's embedded view. Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width, where the same fix is visible across a full scrolled list of ten real companies, each a different color. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.245.0, the job detail pane now pins and scrolls independently like real Indeed does, and a genuinely low-resolution company logo falls back to the clean colored-initial avatar instead of rendering blurry. Reported directly against a screenshot of a job posting cut off mid-sentence: "the section on the right needs to go down when i scrol down so i can see the JD also why the logos feel very bad quality."

THE SCROLL COMPLAINT WAS BEHAVIORAL, NOT A BUG -- CHECKED LIVE BEFORE ASSUMING EITHER WAY. Measured every layer of the actual CSS/layout stack first: `.lp-browser-grid`'s `overflow-y` was `visible` everywhere up the tree, `document.documentElement.scrollHeight` correctly matched the page's own full content height, and a direct `window.scrollTo` test moved the page exactly as expected, with no clipping, no trapped overflow, no sticky-anything on the detail pane at all. The mechanics already worked; what was missing was the actual master/detail PATTERN real Indeed uses -- the list and the detail pane each pin in place and scroll their own content independently, so reading a long JD never means losing the list you were just browsing. AYN's version just let both columns scroll together as one long page, meaning a long JD really did run off the bottom of a normal viewport with nothing to page through except scrolling the WHOLE page far down, past the list entirely.

FIXED WITH A REAL STICKY, INDEPENDENTLY-SCROLLING DETAIL PANE, DESKTOP ONLY. `.lp-browser-detail` (the wrapper around both the populated detail card and its own empty state) now gets `position: sticky; top: 20px; max-height: calc(100vh - 40px); overflow-y: auto;` at the same `min-width: 1024px` breakpoint the grid already goes two-column at -- on a stacked single-column mobile layout the "two panes" are really one column, where pinning either half would only get in the way, so mobile is untouched. Verified live: scrolling the page moves the list up and out of view while the detail pane's own top edge stays pinned at 20px from the viewport; once the pane's own content is taller than the available space, scrolling directly within it (`detail.scrollTop`) moves independently, confirmed against a real 2501px-tall JD inside an 860px-tall pinned box, with the full JD readable start to finish without ever losing the job list.

THE LOGO COMPLAINT TRACED TO A REAL, UNFIXABLE-AT-THE-SOURCE LIMITATION, NOT A CLIENT BUG. `company_logo_url` is freehire's own stored value, not something this app builds -- pulled the real row for the exact company from the screenshot ("Ilderton Conversion") directly from production and found it was already `https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=ildertonvans.com&sz=128`, Google's own service, already asked for a full 128px icon. Checked Google's own *newer* favicon API for the identical domain too, fetched directly and inspected the raw PNG bytes: still a genuine 16x16 image, regardless of what size was requested. No favicon service, old or new, can invent detail a smaller company's own site never published in the first place -- the "bad quality" was real, but it was never something a different URL or a different service could fix.

FIXED BY NEVER SHOWING A LOW-RES IMAGE AT ALL, FALLING BACK TO THE ALREADY-POLISHED COLORED-INITIAL AVATAR INSTEAD. Both the card and detail-pane `<img>` elements already had an `onError` handler for a load that fails outright; added a matching `onLoad` handler (`handleLogoLoad`, a shared `MIN_LOGO_PX = 48` floor) that checks the image's own real, decoded `naturalWidth` once it finishes loading and treats anything under 48px exactly like a failed load -- adding it to the same `logoFailed` set the existing fallback already reads, so a successfully-loaded-but-genuinely-tiny icon (the actual shape of this bug: not a broken image, just a real, small one stretched across a 38-56px box) now never renders stretched and blurry.

ONE HONEST LIMIT, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: Google's own favicon service (`s2/favicons`) returned a hard network failure for every one of 7 real, diverse company domains tested directly from this session's own testing environment -- confirmed the identical URLs work correctly in the app's own established live-verification pattern elsewhere, so this reads as an environment-specific block (this session's network path, not a defect in the shipped code), but it also meant the new `onLoad`-based size check could not be exercised end to end against a real freehire-provided URL in this pass. Verified instead in two narrower, still-real ways: the underlying `naturalWidth < 48` condition confirmed correct against a real, isolated tiny image load (a 1x1 PNG, `naturalWidth: 1`, correctly satisfying the check), and the existing `onError` fallback (which every one of the blocked Google URLs DOES correctly trigger, live, in this same environment) confirmed rendering the clean colored-initial avatar with no regression from the new code sitting alongside it.

Verified live: no horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width, `.lp-browser-detail` correctly falls back to `position: static` below the 1024px breakpoint. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.244.0, selecting a job in Browse Jobs no longer navigates away, closing the exact "feels like two pages" gap between Home's embedded search and real Indeed-style browsing. Reported directly: "job search i feel it have two pages also dose not behave like indeed."

TRACED LIVE TO A REAL, CONFIRMED ROUTE TRANSITION ON EVERY SINGLE JOB CLICK. `JobsBrowser.tsx`'s `openJob` called `navigate(`/jobs/${job.id}`, {replace:true})` unconditionally -- from Home's embedded view (URL `/`) AND from the standalone `/jobs` page itself. `/jobs`, `/jobs/:id`, `/jobs/category/:x` and `/jobs/location/:x` are four separate `<Route>` entries in `App.tsx`, each independently rendering `PublicJobsPage` -- so react-router remounts the whole component fresh on every transition between them, discarding the typed search, filters, and loaded list every time.
