Confirmed with a real, trusted click (typed "manager" via the actual keyboard-input path, not a synthetic DOM event): before the fix, clicking any job cleared the search box back to empty and reset the whole list, on Home and on `/jobs` alike -- a real route change dressed up as a simple selection, exactly what real Indeed never does.

A FIRST ATTEMPT AT A FIX WAS BUILT ON A FALSE LEAD, CAUGHT AND CORRECTED BEFORE SHIPPING. Tried bypassing `navigate()` with a raw `window.history.replaceState()` call instead, reasoning that skipping react-router's own API would dodge the remount -- and a synthetic `.value` + `dispatchEvent(new Event('input'))` test appeared to confirm this STILL reset the search, seemingly proving react-router intercepts even direct History API calls. Re-tested with real, trusted keyboard input instead (this session's own `computer` tool typing real keys, not a scripted DOM mutation) and the "bypass doesn't work" result did not reproduce at all -- the real cause of the earlier failed reading was the test method itself: setting an `<input>`'s `.value` directly and firing a bare `input` event does not reliably register as a genuine React state change, so the visible "typed" text was sometimes never real React state to begin with, and any later re-render would silently snap it back to empty regardless of what the actual fix did. Caught before trusting the wrong root cause into a shipped comment.

FIXED BY NEVER TOUCHING THE URL ON A PLAIN SELECTION AT ALL, VERIFIED WITH REAL CLICKS THIS TIME. `openJob` now only calls `setSelected(job)` plus a new `onSelectedChange` callback -- no navigation, no History API call, nothing. `PublicJobs.tsx`'s own duplicate `routeId`-driven fetch (kept in sync with `JobsBrowser`'s identical internal fetch only because a click used to force a real route change) is deleted outright; `onSelectedChange={setSelected}` is now the one source of truth, firing only for a genuine explicit focus (a real `/jobs/:id` mount, or an actual click) and never for the "show something in the preview pane" default on a bare list -- so the tab title, meta description and `JobPosting` schema all stay correct as you click through jobs purely via local state, with the address bar deliberately never moving. A genuine hard navigation (a shared link, a bookmark, browser back/forward) is completely untouched by this change and still lands on a fresh, correctly-rendered `/jobs/:id` with full `JobPosting` schema, since that path never goes through `openJob` at all.

Verified live with real, trusted clicks (not synthetic events) on both surfaces: Home's embedded view stays on `/` with the typed search ("engineer") and detail pane both correctly updating on a real click; `/jobs` stays on `/jobs` through two consecutive real clicks ("nurse" preserved both times), with the browser tab title correctly updating per job each time; a fresh direct navigation to a real `/jobs/:id` URL still renders the correct title, canonical link, and a complete `JobPosting` schema block; the bare `/jobs` list correctly emits no `JobPosting` schema until a job is genuinely selected; the category/location chip navigation and the "All jobs" back button (real, deliberate route changes, untouched by this fix) still work. Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.243.0, How it works (and every other split text-plus-mockup section sitewide) now starts flush at the top instead of centering against its own mockup. Reported directly against a screenshot: "this page dose not start from the top like other pages."

TRACED TO A CHOICE THAT WAS CORRECT ONCE AND STOPPED BEING CORRECT ONCE THE BASELINE CHANGED UNDER IT. `.lp-split`'s `align-items: center` was a deliberate v3.225.0 call, made specifically so a short text column and a taller mockup would read as balanced side by side rather than "top-jammed" -- reasonable when every page still opened with 72-132px of top padding and the resulting offset was a small fraction of that. Once v3.241.0 cut that shared top padding to a tight 24-40px, the identical centering became the dominant, most visible thing on the page: How it works' own text column, sitting next to `TailoredDocsMockup`'s taller footprint, was pushed down to meet the mockup's vertical midpoint, landing at `eyebrowTop: 130` against every sibling's `eyebrowTop: 50` -- the exact gap in the screenshot, now large enough on its own to be the first thing noticed rather than a minor balancing touch.

FIXED AT THE SHARED CLASS, CHECKED ACROSS EVERY ONE OF ITS SEVEN REAL USES BEFORE TRUSTING IT WOULDN'T BREAK SOMETHING ELSE. `align-items: center` to `align-items: start`, one line in `src/index.css`. Grepped every `.lp-split` call site first rather than assuming How it works was the only one affected: `WhyAynTab`, `GetDiscoveredTab`, the messaging section, and two employer sections (`#employers-features`, the assessment/candidate-card section) all share the identical class. Verified each one live rather than trusting the fix by inspection alone -- How it works confirmed `eyebrowTop: 50`, matching every sibling exactly; Why AYN and Get discovered both screenshotted correctly, text and mockup both starting at the same top edge with the taller one simply running further down its own column, the ordinary way two differently-sized columns sit side by side; both employer `.lp-split` sections confirmed via direct measurement of each column's own `getBoundingClientRect().top` (`-110`/`-110` and `556`/`556`, exactly matching pair by pair, not approximately).

A REAL TOOLING QUIRK HIT WHILE VERIFYING THE EMPLOYER SECTIONS, RULED OUT BEFORE TRUSTING IT. Scrolling to `#employers-features` and screenshotting returned a flat, empty cream frame twice in a row, even after a 2s wait -- looked exactly like the reveal-stuck bug from two versions back. Checked directly rather than assumed broken: `getComputedStyle` on the section showed `opacity: 1` and `is-in` already present, `get_page_text` returned the section's own real copy in the correct reading order, and the column-alignment measurement above came back correct and consistent. Ruled out as the same class of stale/unreliable screenshot read this session's own history has hit before on this exact reveal mechanism (v3.222.0), not a real rendering defect -- the direct DOM checks, not the screenshot, are what confirmed this fix.

Verified live: no horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width (the `.lp-split` breakpoint collapses to one column below 940px, where `align-items` has no visible effect either way, confirmed unaffected). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.242.0, the AYN mark is now the sidebar's own open/close control, and the "Start free" pill got a real icon. Reported directly against three cropped screenshots: the AYN mark, the separate panel-collapse icon that used to sit beside it, and a solid orange pill with nothing visible inside it. "now make the ayn icon is the one that close and open the mnue bar. also we need better icon for sign in."

THE BLANK PILL WAS REAL, CONFIRMED BY READING THE ACTUAL MARKUP, NOT GUESSED FROM THE CROP. `SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s "Start free" button (the sidebar's only sign-in/sign-up entry point -- confirmed by grep, there is no separate "Sign in" control anywhere in this file) had exactly one child, a `<span className="lp-sidebar-link-label">`, and `.lp-sidebar.is-collapsed .lp-sidebar-cta .lp-sidebar-link-label { display: none }` already hid that span when the rail collapses -- with no icon to fall back on, collapsing the sidebar left a genuinely empty, solid ember pill, exactly the third screenshot.

THE AYN MARK NOW OWNS THE TOGGLE THE SEPARATE `.lp-sidebar-toggle` BUTTON USED TO. `<Link to="/" className="lp-sidebar-brand">` (which navigated home and reset to the search tab) is now `<button className="lp-sidebar-brand" onClick={toggleCollapsed}>` -- nothing is lost by retiring its home-navigation duty, since "Job search", the very first nav item directly below it, already calls the identical `selectTab('search')` handler and has since v3.223.0. The standalone `PanelLeftOpen`/`PanelLeftClose` button is deleted outright, along with its now-fully-dead `.lp-sidebar-toggle` CSS (confirmed via grep, zero remaining references anywhere). `LogIn` from lucide-react is added to "Start free" as a real icon, visible both expanded (icon plus label, matching every other nav row's own layout) and collapsed (a recognizable glyph instead of a blank shape), with a `title="Sign in or start free"` tooltip added for the collapsed state.

ONE REAL WRINKLE, HANDLED RATHER THAN LEFT AS A LATENT BUG: THE SAME `nav` MARKUP RENDERS INSIDE THE MOBILE DRAWER TOO. The mobile drawer's own `<aside>` never applies `.is-collapsed` to itself (confirmed by reading its className expression directly), so a tap on the brand button there was never going to visually break the drawer -- but it would still flip the underlying `collapsed` state variable, a change that would only surface later and confusingly if the same session was ever resized back to a desktop width. `toggleCollapsed` now also calls `setMobileOpen(false)`: a harmless no-op on desktop (the drawer isn't open to close), and on mobile it gives the logo a second, sensible job -- tap it to close the menu, the same real action closing via the X button already does.

Verified live: clicking the AYN mark collapses and expands the desktop rail correctly (confirmed via the `.lp-sidebar` element's own className, not just a screenshot), the "Start free" pill shows a real `LogIn` glyph at both a real 1500px desktop width and the collapsed rail width (54px, icon-only, confirmed present via `querySelector('svg')`), and tapping the mark inside the real mobile drawer correctly closes it, confirmed by the drawer's own className losing `is-open` after the closing transition finished. Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.241.0, the top padding every page shared was cut by roughly two thirds, and a real, separate stuck-animation bug was found and fixed along the way. Reported directly against a deliberately cropped screenshot, isolating just the blank space above the "JOB SEARCH" chip: "why i have a lot of space on top... all pages need to have same hight postion... we need all the content to start from the top going down." Confirmed with a follow-up question that this was every page, not one, and that the crop was intentional, not the full window.

TRIED TO REPRODUCE AT THREE REAL WIDTHS FIRST AND COULDN'T -- WHICH TURNED OUT TO BE THE RIGHT SIGNAL, NOT A DEAD END. 1440px, 1500px and a genuinely wide 2560px viewport (confirmed via `window.innerWidth`, not assumed) all rendered the v3.240.0-consistent ~72-132px gap, correctly matching sitewide but with nothing visibly excessive in a full-page screenshot at any of them. The follow-up question resolved the mismatch: the complaint was never about a broken/doubled value on one page, it was that the ALREADY-CONSISTENT shared value itself read as too generous once seen in isolation, cropped tight to just the gap.

FIXED AT THE ONE SHARED RULE EVERY PAGE ALREADY POINTS AT. `.lp-section`'s `padding-block: clamp(72px, 11vw, 132px)` applied the identical clamp to both the top and bottom edge -- the 72-132px this file's own v3.226.0 entry settled on as "the real, correct spacing every tab needs" was always meant as generous room before a footer, not as the gap before a page's own opening line. Split into `padding-block-start: clamp(24px, 3vw, 40px)` (a real, deliberate reduction, not zero -- the mobile sticky top bar's own real document-flow height already provides separation, this is just breathing room on top of that) and an unchanged `padding-block-end: clamp(72px, 11vw, 132px)`, since nobody has ever reported the bottom spacing as a problem and cramming content against the footer would trade one complaint for another.

A SECOND, REAL, SEPARATE BUG SURFACED WHILE VERIFYING THE FIRST FIX ACROSS EVERY TAB, CONFIRMED BY SCREENSHOT NOT JUST A COMPUTED-STYLE READ. Comparing the new, tighter baseline against a HomeTabs tab reached by a real in-page click (Features, Pricing, and every sibling tested) found every one of them sitting a real, confirmed ~30px lower, and a screenshot showed why: the tab's own opening content was still washed out near `opacity: 0`, the same `.lp-reveal` scroll-reveal mechanism this file's own v3.219.0/v3.222.0 history has hit twice before. Reproduced after a full clean restart (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab) to rule out stale HMR, and confirmed a fresh, unrelated `IntersectionObserver` on a brand-new element also never fired within 1.5s in this session's own automation tooling -- strong evidence the underlying trigger is a timing limitation of driving a page through this specific tooling, not something a real visitor's browser would ever leave permanently stuck. Rather than leave it unresolved a third time on that same "probably just the tool" reasoning, fixed it properly this time: a 900ms `setTimeout` fallback now force-adds `.is-in` to any `.lp-reveal` node the `IntersectionObserver` hasn't already caught, since a decorative reveal animation must never be the thing standing between a real visitor and real content, regardless of why the observer didn't fire. Re-verified live: the same Pricing tab that screenshotted washed out before now renders fully visible within about a second of arriving, and its own top spacing lines up with every other tab and page to within 3px (the one remaining difference being Pricing's own `<Badge>` element running a few pixels taller than the shared `.lp-eyebrow` chip, not a bug).

Verified live across `/`, `/jobs`, `/salary-guide`, `/check-resume`, `/legal`, `/pricing`, and five more HomeTabs tabs reached by a real click, all landing within 3px of each other at a real 1500px viewport, both fixes confirmed together. Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width, with the sticky mobile top bar still correctly clearing page content by its own real document-flow height, not just the new smaller padding. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched diff found zero new matches.

MEASURED LIVE ACROSS SIX ROUTES BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING, THE SAME DISCIPLINE AS EVERY PRIOR ROUND OF THIS COMPLAINT. `getBoundingClientRect()` on the real `.lp-eyebrow`/`<h1>` at a real viewport, one real navigation per route, not a synthetic history hack (an earlier `pushState`/`popstate` shortcut was tried first and discarded once it became clear it couldn't be trusted to fully re-mount a lazy route). `/jobs`, `/salary-guide`, `/check-resume` and `/legal` all measured `eyebrowTop: 125`, `h1Top: 190` -- identical, confirming v3.226.0's and v3.237.0's own `.lp-section` consolidation still holds everywhere it was meant to. `/` (Home's seeker "search" view) measured `eyebrowTop: 93`, `h1Top: 158` -- a real, confirmed 32px gap, not approximate.

TRACED TO THE ONE BLOCK BOTH EARLIER PASSES LEFT UNTOUCHED, SITTING BETWEEN THEIR TWO SCOPES. `LandingSections.tsx`'s seeker `{seeker && (...)}` branch (the block that embeds `JobsBrowser` directly on Home, added at v3.213.0/v3.218.0, well before either later fix existed) wrapped its content in a bespoke `<div className="lp-shell" style={{paddingBlockStart: 'clamp(40px, 6vw, 72px)'}}>` -- its own, smaller top-padding clamp, never routed through `.lp-section`'s shared `padding-block: clamp(72px, 11vw, 132px)` at all. v3.226.0's fix only touched the general `.lp-audience` tab-content wrapper further down this same file; v3.237.0's fix only touched standalone routed pages. This one early-return seeker branch was never in either sweep's scope.

FIXED BY GIVING IT THE SAME `.lp-section`/`.lp-shell` PAIR, NOT A THIRD BESPOKE VALUE. Replaced the bare `.lp-shell` with `<section className="lp-section" style={{paddingBlockEnd: 0}}><div className="lp-shell">` -- `paddingBlockEnd: 0` kept deliberately, since the content directly below (the audience-scoped body sections) already carries its own top spacing, and giving this block `.lp-section`'s full bottom padding too would have introduced a new, unasked-for gap where none existed before.

A SECOND, SEPARATE FINDING SURFACED WHILE INVESTIGATING, DELIBERATELY NOT ACTED ON. Comparing Home against a HomeTabs tab (Features, Pricing) reached by a real in-page click turned up every one of that tab's own `.lp-reveal` scroll-reveal blocks sitting frozen at `opacity: 0`/`translateY(18px)` -- a real, visibly faded screenshot, not just a stale computed-style read. Investigated rather than assumed real: a fresh, unrelated `IntersectionObserver` created from scratch on a brand-new element appended to `<body>` also never fired in this session's own Browser-pane automation, even after 1.5s on an element clearly on screen -- strong, independent evidence this is a limitation of driving a page through this session's own automation tooling (the observer never reliably firing in a CDP-automated tab), not a genuine defect in the shipped app, and consistent with this file's own v3.222.0 precedent of this exact reveal mechanism producing misleading reads under this same tooling. `https://ayn.careers` and every other external origin were both blocked by this session's own browsing policy, so the one direct way to settle it -- checking real production -- was not available. Flagged honestly rather than "fixed" on inconclusive evidence; not treated as a confirmed bug.

Verified live: `/` now measures the identical `eyebrowTop: 125`, `h1Top: 190` as every other checked page, reconfirmed after a full clean restart (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab) to rule out stale HMR before trusting the number. TRACED TO A REAL, NARROW GAP THE v3.236.0 SWEEP NEVER COVERED. That pass rebuilt `.lp-eyebrow` sitewide and confirmed 25 call sites across `HomeTabs.tsx`, `AccountTabs.tsx`, `LandingSections.tsx` and `LegalIndex.tsx` -- every one of them a tab inside the shared `.lp` shell. `SalaryGuide.tsx` and `CheckResume.tsx` are their own standalone routes, never counted in that sweep, and had only ever carried a bare decorative bar (`<span className="h-1 w-14 rounded-full" style={{background: EMBER}} />`, zero label text) above their own `<h1>`. `JobsBrowser.tsx` -- the shared component rendering "Browse real jobs" on both the signed-out homepage and `/jobs` -- was worse: its heading block had no eyebrow element of any kind, not even the bare bar, confirmed by grep finding zero `lp-eyebrow` occurrences anywhere in that file before this fix.

FIXED WITH THE SAME REAL CHIP EVERY OTHER PAGE ALREADY HAS, NAMED TO MATCH EACH PAGE'S OWN SIDEBAR LABEL. `<p className="lp-eyebrow">Salary guide</p>` and `<p className="lp-eyebrow">Check my resume</p>` replace the two bare bars, matching `SeekerSidebar.tsx`'s own nav labels for these exact routes rather than inventing new wording. `JobsBrowser.tsx` gained `<p className="lp-eyebrow">Job search</p>` above its heading in both the `asH1` and plain `<h2>` render branches, matching the sidebar's "Job search" label -- kept static rather than switched per category/location filter, the same way "Proposals"'s own eyebrow never changes per proposal. `CheckResume.tsx`'s now-fully-unused `EMBER` constant was removed; `SalaryGuide.tsx`'s stayed, since it still colors a real progress-bar fill further down the page.

Verified live at a real desktop viewport and a real 375px mobile width: all three pages render a real solid ember chip ("SALARY GUIDE", "CHECK MY RESUME", "JOB SEARCH") above their heading, confirmed on `/`, `/jobs`, `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume`. Preceded by v3.238.0, the sidebar's three collapsible-group labels now sit at an identical height and gap to their first item. Reported directly against a screenshot of the full signed-in sidebar ("YOUR ACCOUNT" with Profile active, then a collapsed "LEARN ABOUT AYN"): "looks better but they need to match on hight they are diffrent in hight one is down and one is up so they need all to match so looks nice."

MEASURED THE REAL GAP LIVE RATHER THAN GUESSING FROM THE SCREENSHOT. `getBoundingClientRect()` on all three groups: "Your account" and "Company" (both a plain `<span className="lp-sidebar-group-label">`) measured `gapLabelToFirstLink: 0`; "Learn about AYN" (a `<button className="lp-sidebar-group-toggle">` wrapping the same label span plus a `ChevronDown` icon, the v3.233.0 collapsible-disclosure treatment) measured `gapLabelToFirstLink: 6`, a real, confirmed 6px difference, not a subjective impression. Traced to the button element itself, not its content: `getComputedStyle` showed the toggle's own `line-height` computing to `24px` against the plain label's `16.5px`, even though the label span nested inside the button correctly rendered at the same 11px font-size as its siblings -- `.lp-sidebar-group-toggle` never reset `font` on itself, so as a bare `<button>` its own invisible line-box fell back to the browser's default UI-control font metrics (a 16px font-size, not this page's real 11px), inflating the row's height regardless of what the visible text inside it was doing.

FIXED BY RESETTING THE BUTTON'S OWN FONT, NOT BY TOUCHING THE LABEL OR THE PADDING. Both elements already shared the identical `padding: 4px 12px 6px` -- the padding was never the problem, the button's own default line-box was. Added `font: inherit; line-height: 16.5px;` to `.lp-sidebar-group-toggle` in `src/index.css`, matching the plain label's own measured line-height exactly rather than a value that happened to look close.

Verified live: re-measured all three groups after the fix and all three now report the identical `gapGroupTopToLabel: 9`, `gapLabelToFirstLink: 0`, `labelBoxHeight: 27` -- not close, the same three numbers across every group. A screenshot confirmed the visual result: all three group labels now sit flush against their first nav item with no visible extra gap on "Learn about AYN." Zero horizontal overflow at a real 375px mobile width. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched CSS found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.237.0, every page's own content now starts at the identical x position. Reported directly, two things in one message: a screenshot of the sidebar's own "Job search" active-highlight pill ("still there are pages [that] dose not have [this] in the image"), and "every page we have it have diffrent hight and wedith also difrent postiong why they all need to match in layout hight and wedith and postioning."

BOTH HALVES TRACED TO REAL, LIVE, MEASURABLE CAUSES BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING, NOT GUESSED AT FROM THE DESCRIPTION. The screenshot's own active pill: confirmed live that `/jobs` (a real route, the identical `JobsBrowser` component Home's own "Job search" tab embeds, per v3.223.0's history) left the whole sidebar with zero active links, since neither "Job search"'s own `onHome && activeTab==='search'` check nor `TOOL_LINKS`' exact-pathname check ever matched it. The width/positioning claim: measured live, not assumed -- About's own heading rendered at `x=560px` while Features' identical left-aligned heading rendered at `x=292px` in the same sidebar layout at the same viewport, a real, confirmed 268px shift, not a subjective impression. Root cause: `.lp-shell`'s own `margin: 0 auto` centers whatever width it's given, so a tab with a narrower `maxWidth` override doesn't just show narrower content, it shows content that starts further right too. Grepping wider than the four tabs (Pricing/Contact/About/Help) already known to override `.lp-shell`'s width surfaced the real scope: `/jobs`, `/check-resume`, `/salary-guide` and `/do-not-sell` were each running their own bespoke `container mx-auto max-w-*` convention (five different values found across the codebase: 720, 736, 768, 896, 960, 1152, 1160, 1360px, no two pages agreeing), every one of them centered the identical way.

FIXED BY GIVING EVERY PAGE THE SAME OUTER SHELL, AND MOVING THE READING-WIDTH CONSTRAINT INSIDE IT WHERE ONE WAS STILL GENUINELY WARRANTED. `/jobs` (`SeekerSidebar.tsx`): "Job search"'s own active check widened to `location.pathname.startsWith('/jobs')`. Every other touched page (`HomeTabs.tsx`'s Pricing/Contact/About/Help, `LegalIndex.tsx`, `LegalPage.tsx`, `CheckResume.tsx`, `SalaryGuide.tsx`, `PublicJobs.tsx`, `DoNotSell.tsx`): the outer wrapper is now the same `.lp-section`/`.lp-shell` pair every left-aligned tab already used, so every page's content starts at the identical x position and shares the identical top padding. Where a narrower reading measure still made real sense (About's prose, Help's intro copy, Contact's and Do Not Sell's own content, Legal's actual document text) the cap moved onto an inner wrapper with no `margin: auto`, left-aligned, not centered -- readability preserved without reintroducing the positioning bug. Where it didn't (Pricing's card grid, `/jobs`, Check Resume's two-column paste layout, Salary Guide's data table, both already looking cramped rather than over-wide at their old narrower caps) the cap was dropped outright to the shared 1360px default. Pricing's own centered intro (`textAlign: center`, `marginInline: auto`) was switched to left-aligned to match every other tab's convention, rather than leaving one page centered while all the others weren't. Two shadcn `<Button>` pairs found along the way (Check Resume's own two CTAs, `/jobs`' cross-link pair) were converted to `.lp-btn` for the same reason the button-system pass two versions back existed: one page's controls should not look like a different app's.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 375px mobile width, a full clean restart first (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab): every one of Features, `/jobs`, About, Contact, Pricing, Help, the Legal hub, an individual legal document, Check Resume, Salary Guide, and Do Not Sell measured its own heading's `getBoundingClientRect().left` at exactly `292px`, not approximately -- the same number, ten times over, confirmed via direct DOM measurement, not eyeballed from a screenshot. `/jobs` confirmed showing "Job search" as the active sidebar link for the first time. Zero horizontal overflow at the mobile width across all eleven pages checked. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `node scripts/check-wiring.mjs` both clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file's own diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.236.0, the site-wide eyebrow label rebuilt as a real, solid chip. Reported directly, against a screenshot of the exact hairline-plus-caps "PROPOSALS" label on the Proposals sign-in gate: "i notised all pages they dont have titile highlight all they have things like this which is bad makes me feel its powerpoint presantion and this is a website needs proper highlights for each page."

TRACED THE EXACT SCREENSHOT TO A REAL, LIVE ELEMENT BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING, NOT GUESSED AT FROM THE DESCRIPTION ALONE. Reproduced the identical signed-out `SignInPrompt` gate locally and screenshotted it side by side with the founder's own image -- pixel-for-pixel the same element: `.lp-eyebrow`, a thin 22x1px hairline plus 12px letter-spaced uppercase text, no fill, no weight. Grepped every real call site before deciding this was genuinely sitewide rather than a one-off: 25 occurrences across 5 files (`HomeTabs.tsx`, `LandingSections.tsx`, `AccountTabs.tsx`, `BeforeAfterProof.tsx`, `LegalIndex.tsx`) -- every marketing tab, all six sign-in gates, the Legal hub. A real, already-shipped exception existed one tab over: Pricing's own eyebrow was never `.lp-eyebrow` at all, it's `<Badge className="ayn-ember-badge">`, a solid filled ember-gradient pill -- confirming the "thin hairline" complaint wasn't inherent to the whole design system, just to the one class every other page happened to use instead of the one page that already had it right.

FIXED AT THE CSS CLASS ITSELF, NOT AT THE 25 CALL SITES. `.lp-eyebrow` rebuilt to the identical solid-pill recipe Pricing's own badge already uses (`var(--lp-gradient-ember)` background, white text, `border-radius: 999px`, a real padded chip, an inset highlight plus a soft ember glow shadow) -- one CSS change, every `<p className="lp-eyebrow">` across all 25 sites picks it up with zero markup edits, the same "fix the shared token/class, not each usage" approach that already worked for the button system and the resume-hub cards two passes back. The old `::before` hairline is gone outright, not hidden -- a filled chip doesn't want a redundant line in front of it, and grep confirmed nothing else in the stylesheet depended on that pseudo-element existing.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 375px mobile width, a full clean restart first (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab): the Proposals sign-in gate now shows a solid `PROPOSALS` chip matching the reported screenshot's exact complaint point, directly resolved; FAQ ("QUESTIONS"), Contact ("CONTACT"), and the Legal hub ("LEGAL") all confirmed rendering the same solid-pill treatment with no layout breakage; Pricing's own pre-existing `ayn-ember-badge` confirmed visually consistent with the new chip, no clash or duplication between the two similar-but-separate elements. Zero horizontal overflow at mobile width. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (CSS-only change, no component files touched). A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched region found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.235.0, asked directly "what can we do" to make the pages better, given a four-item menu (unify the signed-in tabs' theme with the marketing pages, add visual interest to the plain text tabs, extend the button/card polish to the standalone pages, add real motion), and told "all of them."

THE SIGNED-IN ACCOUNT TABS WERE MEASURED LIVE, NOT ASSUMED, BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING. A real throwaway account was created directly against the live auth API and its session injected into the browser's own localStorage (the standing "verify it live" technique this app's history already uses, documented in docs/map/deployment.md) so Profile/Saved jobs/Proposals/Assessments/Settings could actually be seen signed in, not guessed at from reading component code. The hypothesis going in ("these run on a totally different color system") was wrong -- `resume-hub.css`'s own `--rh-*` tokens have matched `.lp`'s ember/warm-paper system since v3.171.0, confirmed by reading the file before assuming otherwise. What was real: `--rh-shadow-card`'s second layer sat at 0.10 opacity against `.lp-panel`'s own 0.22 (nearly imperceptible depth), every card ran Tailwind's plain `rounded-xl` (12px) against the marketing pages' 22-24px system, and every section heading (`Group`'s own "Your resume"/"About you," plus "Saved jobs"/"Proposals"/"Assessments"/"Settings"/"Browse jobs") was plain `rh-display` text with no eyebrow-style accent, reading with far less visual weight than a marketing page's own headings.

Fixed at the token/pattern level so it cascades rather than touching dozens of call sites individually: `--rh-shadow-card`/`--rh-shadow-lift` enriched to real, visible depth; a scoped `.resume-hub-theme .rounded-xl { border-radius: 18px }` override (checked first: 36 occurrences across five files, confirmed via grep to be almost entirely card containers, not controls, before trusting a blanket override was safe) softens every card at once; and the same short ember accent mark `.lp-eyebrow::before` already uses on the marketing pages was added to `Group`'s own heading (covers all five Profile sections in one edit) and to the six other tabs' own top-level titles (Saved jobs, Job matches, Proposals, Assessments, Settings) individually, since each lives in its own file with no shared heading component to edit once.

THE PLAIN-TEXT TABS GOT VISUAL TREATMENT SUITED TO WHAT EACH ONE ACTUALLY IS, NOT THE SAME FIX COPY-PASTED TWICE. FAQ ("Good to know") was eight identical white blocks in one long column -- genuinely a wall of text, since `.lp-faq-item` was never an accordion at all (that's a different tab, Help), every question and answer sat fully expanded and stacked. Rebuilt as a real two-column grid with a genuine per-question icon badge (Sparkles/Building2/Ban/Eye/Lock/ShieldCheck/FileCheck2/Gift, each matched to that question's own real topic, not a decorative repeat), reusing `.lp-tile-icon`, the same badge language Features' own tile grid already established. About was deliberately rebuilt as a flowing narrative once already (v3.54.0, after the earlier three-section pitch-deck structure was reported as reading like a sales pitch) -- re-decorating it with icons or tiles risked reintroducing exactly that. Gave it a real pull-quote instead (`.lp-pullquote`, an ember left-border accent on the one sentence the whole page's argument turns on), the correct editorial device for prose like this rather than a UI pattern borrowed from elsewhere on the site. Help was left alone on purpose -- a dense, searchable reference page benefits from staying plain and scannable, not from being decorated for its own sake.

THREE STANDALONE PAGES WERE STILL ON A THIRD, DIFFERENT BUTTON LANGUAGE, MISSED BY THE PREVIOUS PASS BECAUSE THAT PASS NEVER GREPPED FOR IT. `/salary-guide`'s own closing CTA pair used a bespoke `rounded-lg px-4 py-2` shape with an inline `background: EMBER` override -- a third visual language next to `.lp-btn` (pill) and shadcn's own default. `/check-resume` had two shadcn `<Button>`s doing the identical thing. All three converted to the same `.lp-btn`/`.lp-btn-primary`/`.lp-btn-ghost` classes every other CTA on the site already uses; the now-unused `Button` import and its inline `style` overrides removed rather than left dead.

MOTION: `.lp-reveal` (the site's existing scroll-triggered fade, IntersectionObserver-driven since well before this pass, respecting prefers-reduced-motion) already covered every page at the block level -- confirmed by reading `LandingSections.tsx`'s own effect before assuming motion was missing outright. What it never did was animate multiple items in a group as anything other than one flat block. Added a per-item staggered reveal to the new FAQ grid specifically (`.lp-faq.is-in .lp-faq-item:nth-child(n)`, a 60ms stagger per card, keyed off the exact same `.is-in` class the existing observer already toggles -- no JS change needed) with its own `prefers-reduced-motion` override, rather than inventing a second motion system.

A REAL TOOLING QUIRK, CHASED DOWN AND RULED OUT BEFORE TRUSTING IT. Verifying the FAQ stagger, `getComputedStyle` reads via this session's own browser-automation tool repeatedly reported every `.lp-faq-item` frozen at `opacity: 0` even seconds after the parent's own `.is-in` class was confirmed present and the exact selector confirmed matching all eight items (`document.querySelectorAll('.lp-faq.is-in .lp-faq-item').length === 8`) -- looked exactly like a real cascade bug. A direct screenshot, the actual rendered pixels rather than a JS-read computed value, showed all eight cards fully visible and correctly staggered the whole time. Ruled out as a stale/cached computed-style read specific to this tool's own JS-execution path, not a real defect -- the same "trust the screenshot over a possibly-stale programmatic read" lesson this file's own history has documented once already for the screenshot tool itself, just the inverse tool this time.

Verified live end to end: a real throwaway account (created and fully erased after via `self_delete_account`, confirmed zero rows remaining in `auth.users`) walked through Profile/Job matches/Saved jobs/Proposals/Assessments/Settings, confirming the accent-mark headings, the 18px card radius, and the richer shadow all render correctly (`getComputedStyle` on a real Group card: `border-radius: 18px`, a real two-layer `box-shadow` at 0.22 opacity, not the old 0.10). FAQ and About confirmed both signed in and signed out, desktop and a real 375px mobile width, zero horizontal overflow on either. `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume` confirmed their CTAs now carry `lp-btn`/`lp-btn-primary`/`lp-btn-ghost` classes directly, not just visually similar. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `node scripts/check-wiring.mjs` both clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file's own diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.234.0, real design/layout/button polish, not another reuse of what was already there. Reported directly, right after v3.233.0's fixes shipped: "i still feel i dont have best pages layout sizes and pulish buttons and better pages design you are just keeping using what we built you can go boyand that to make better user experince."

THE ACTUAL COMPLAINT WAS ABOUT THE UNDERLYING SYSTEM, NOT THE INDIVIDUAL FINDINGS. v3.233.0's fixes were each correct on their own terms but every one of them reused an existing class or pattern rather than reconsidering it -- a real, if narrow, way to satisfy a literal bug report while leaving the thing actually driving the "not polished" feeling untouched. This pass rebuilt the pieces that read thin instead of patching around them.

THE BUTTON SYSTEM ITSELF WAS REBUILT, NOT RESTYLED. `.lp-btn` (`src/index.css`) had exactly one size everywhere on the site -- a hero's own primary decision and a dense pricing card's "Choose plan" both ran the identical 13px/26px padding -- a flat one-stop hover with no active/press state and no focus ring of its own (relying on the browser default outline). Added a real size scale (`.lp-btn-lg` for a page's one primary decision -- the hero CTA, both closing CTAs, the sign-in gate's own "Start free" -- `.lp-btn-sm` for dense/repeated contexts), a snappier cubic-bezier easing, a real `:active` press state (`scale(0.97)`), an ember-tinted `:focus-visible` ring instead of the default outline, and real depth on `.lp-btn-primary` (an inset top highlight plus a richer shadow, brightening slightly on hover, darkening on press) and `.lp-btn-ghost` (real border weight and its own hover-lift, rather than a nearly invisible 4%-opacity tint). One CSS change, applied everywhere `.lp-btn` is used.

PRICING'S OWN CARDS WERE MEASURED AND FOUND GENUINELY CRAMPED, NOT JUST PLAIN. Four cards at a 220px floor inside a 1080px shell worked out to about 256px each -- tight for a price, a credit line, a description and a full-width button. Widened the shell to 1160px and the column floor to 248px; the price itself now renders at 32px in the site's own Outfit display face (was 24px, same weight as plain body text) so it reads as the card's own headline instead of one line among several. `Starter` (the "Most chosen" card from v3.233.0) gained a genuine soft ember tint behind its existing border, not just an outline, so it reads as emphasis rather than a slightly different-colored line.

CONTACT'S FORM WAS STILL A BARE SHADCN DEFAULT INSIDE A WIDENED SHELL. v3.226.0 widened the shell around it to 960px; the form itself stayed a plain `rounded-2xl border bg-card p-3` wrapper (12px of padding) with a generic rectangular shadcn `<Button>` for "Send message" -- the one control on the page not shaped like every other call to action on the site. New `.lp-panel` class (a static content card, generous clamp() padding, its own soft shadow, no hover-lift since it's not a clickable tile) replaces the bare wrapper; `TicketForm.tsx`'s submit button is now a plain `.lp-btn.lp-btn-primary`, the same pill every other primary action on the page already uses, with a real `Loader2` spin while sending instead of static "Sending..." text. Verified this component has exactly one real caller (`ContactTab`, confirmed via grep) before restyling its button off the shared, cross-app shadcn component -- safe today, would need reconsidering if this form is ever mounted somewhere outside `.lp`.

THE LEGAL HUB WAS THE ONE PAGE ON THE WHOLE SIDEBAR SHELL WITH NO TRACE OF THE SITE'S OWN VISUAL IDENTITY. `LegalIndex.tsx` ran on plain shadcn tokens (`divide-border`, `hover:bg-muted/40`, a bare `text-3xl font-semibold` heading) despite living inside the same `.lp lp-shell-with-sidebar` every other tab uses -- confirmed by reading the file, not assumed from the screenshot. Heading rebuilt with the same `.lp-eyebrow`/`.lp-display` pattern every tab already uses (and given a real, short phrase instead of the bare single word "Legal," matching how every sibling tab has a substantive heading, not a label). Each of the nine list rows is now a real card (`.lp-legal-row`, new class): a soft border and background matching `.lp-panel`'s own language, an ember-tinted border and a lift-plus-shadow on hover, the chevron sliding right and turning ember to match. `LegalPage.tsx` (the individual document renderer) was already correctly branded from v3.230.0 and untouched here; only the hub's own list needed the fix.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real (session-floor) 375px mobile width, a full clean restart first (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab): Pricing's cards render visibly wider with a bold Outfit-face price and a real tinted `Starter` card; Contact's form sits inside a real elevated panel with a pill-shaped, ember-gradient submit button; the Legal hub's heading and all nine rows render the new card treatment, confirmed a resting row carries no shadow and `border-radius: 16px` via `getComputedStyle`, matching the "only lift on hover" design; the sign-in gate's "Start free" reads visibly larger (`.lp-btn-lg`) and stays centered against the footer exactly as v3.232.0 left it; the "Already have an account? Sign in" link still correctly opens `AuthModal` on its Sign In tab, re-confirmed after this pass's changes, no regression. Zero horizontal overflow at the mobile width on Pricing, Contact, and the Legal hub (`document.body.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` on all three). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `node scripts/check-wiring.mjs` both clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file's own diff found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.233.0, a full-site design audit run as a professional-designer review, then every finding in it fixed the same day. Asked directly: "as a perfational designer i want you to look into all the pages and tell me what element we need to change things need to be better buttons titiles subtitiles and wording moackup all the elemnts we need to make this website the best experince" -- answered first with a findings-only report (an Artifact, page by page, nothing changed yet), then, once the founder said "fix them all," every actionable finding was implemented the same session.

THE AUDIT ITSELF WAS A REAL PASS, NOT A GUESS FROM MEMORY. Walked every seeker-facing surface live: all sixteen HomeTabs tabs, the six signed-in-only account tabs' sign-in gate, the Legal hub and a document page, the standalone `/jobs` board, and `/employers`. Found and reported 2 high-priority, 4 medium, and 2 polish items, plus what was already working well per page (the report's own honesty check, not just a list of complaints).

FOUR REAL FIXES SHIPPED. One, Pricing's `Starter` card had every visual signal of "the one to pick" (an ember border, a filled button) with no word anywhere saying why -- now carries a `Most chosen` badge (`Badge className="ayn-ember-badge"`, reusing the existing pill rather than inventing new chrome), and every plan's button now names the plan (`Choose Starter`, not four identical `Choose plan`s in a row). Two, the Contact form's Priority selector -- asking a first-time visitor to self-rate their own message low/medium/high before anyone had read it -- is gone from the UI entirely (still sent at a fixed `'medium'` default, since the schema requires it); Category is relabeled from the raw admin-panel term to `What's this about?` with plain-language options ("Something isn't working," not "Technical"). Three, the "Learn about AYN" sidebar group (seven explanation tabs) used to sit at the same visual weight as the two-item "Your account" group a returning user actually needs -- it's now a real disclosure, collapsed by default once signed in (auto-expanding if the active tab already lives inside it, so it never hides its own active state), always expanded signed out, since deciding whether to sign up is the whole point there. Four, the FAQ tab's nav label never matched its own heading (`FAQ` in the sidebar, `Good to know` on the page) -- moved the nav label to match the friendlier heading rather than the reverse.

THE SIGN-IN GATE'S SINGLE BUTTON WAS DOING TWO JOBS, AND FIXING IT SURFACED A REAL BUG A ONE-ARGUMENT PASS-THROUGH HAD BEEN HIDING. `Sign in or start free` put the whole decision on a button label instead of the interface. Split into a primary `Start free` plus a lighter `Already have an account? Sign in` link, each meant to open the existing `AuthModal` straight to the matching tab -- `AuthModal` gained a new `initialTab` prop, `onStartFree`'s signature grew an optional second argument (`role?, tab?`), and `LandingPage.tsx` threads a new `authTab` state into the modal's own remount `key`, the same trick already used for `authRole`. First live test of the new "Sign in" link still landed on the Sign Up tab -- traced to `LandingSections.tsx`'s own `Props` type and its one-argument wrapper (`onStartFree={(role) => onStartFree?.(role)}`) silently dropping the second argument on its way to the tab component, a real, previously-invisible gap in a callback that had only ever been called with one argument before this. Fixed both the type and the wrapper; re-verified live that "Sign in" now opens on the Sign In tab and "Start free" still correctly opens on Sign Up, no regression.

TWO MORE FOUND WHILE FIXING WHAT WAS REPORTED, NOT PART OF THE ORIGINAL LIST. Reading the Help Center's own "Response aims: free plan best effort, Starter 3 business days, Growth 2, Scale 1" against the real Service Level Agreement (`sla.md`) found it was quoting that document's *employer-only* support table verbatim -- `Growth`/`Scale` are employer plan names that appear nowhere on seeker Pricing, and the SLA itself states plainly it "does not apply... to job seeker plans." This Help tab is only ever reachable through the seeker sidebar (`/employers` runs its own separate chrome and never mounts it), so the line was rewritten to a real, honest, non-plan-specific aim instead of borrowing a commitment that was never seeker-scoped to begin with. Separately, Browse Jobs' company-logo fallback (a bordered square with a single initial letter, `.lp-browser-avatar`) read as an unchecked checkbox at list density -- rounded into a full circle (real `<img>` logos, `.lp-browser-logo`, stay rounded squares, the conventional app-icon shape), and the identical fix applied to all four fallback-avatar render sites in the signed-in `BrowseJobs.tsx` (resume-hub's own, differently-themed "Job matches" tab), which uses its own `companyAvatar()` helper and was flagged in the same report section but not separately screenshotted.

ONE FINDING DELIBERATELY LEFT UNTOUCHED. The report's `/employers` item (still on the old fixed `Header`/`Footer` chrome, not the sidebar shell) was written with its own caveat -- "Not urgent, and not a small change. Flagging rather than recommending immediate action" -- since it has no concrete fix short of moving the whole employer dashboard onto the sidebar shell, a real, separate undertaking outside this pass. Left as a disclosed, open item rather than force-fit into a same-day scope it was never sized for.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport, a full clean restart first (`preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start`, a genuinely fresh tab): the `Most chosen` badge and per-plan button text render correctly on Pricing; the Contact form shows exactly one selector (`What's this about?`), confirmed via `read_page`'s own interactive-element list, not just text extraction; the sidebar's `Learn about AYN` group starts expanded signed out with a working collapse toggle; `Good to know` renders as both the nav label and the page heading; the sign-in gate shows both buttons with the primary still centered against the footer exactly as v3.232.0 left it; the "Sign in" link opens `AuthModal` on its Sign In tab (`data-state="active"` on the right `TabsTrigger`, checked directly, not assumed) while "Start free" still opens Sign Up; `/jobs`' avatar circles confirmed via `getComputedStyle` (`border-radius: 50%`). `npx tsc --noEmit` and `node scripts/check-wiring.mjs` both clean throughout. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file's own diff (not the whole file, which already carries exempt historical comments) found zero new matches.

Preceded by v3.232.0, the sign-in gate's own footer positioning fixed -- a real structural bug plus a genuine UX gap, not just one or the other. Reported directly, against a screenshot of the Profile tab signed out: "why the fotter like this not like the other pages."

TWO SEPARATE THINGS WERE WRONG, FOUND BY MEASURING LIVE RATHER THAN GUESSING FROM THE SCREENSHOT ALONE. First, a real structural bug: `.lp-shell-with-sidebar`'s own `align-items: stretch` forced `.lp-sidebar-main` (the only child that actually reads it -- `.lp-sidebar` itself already opts out via its own `position: sticky` + explicit `height: 100vh`) to stretch to match the sidebar's full 100vh, regardless of how short its real content was. Measured directly: `.lp-sidebar-main` reported a height of exactly `1000px` in a `1000px` viewport on the Profile sign-in gate, even though its actual content (an eyebrow, a heading, one line, a button) only needed `596px`. On a content-rich tab this was invisible, since real content already exceeds `100vh` on its own; only a genuinely short page ever exposed it. Fixed by changing `align-items` to `flex-start` -- `.lp-sidebar-main` now sizes to its own content, and the sidebar (which sets its own height independently) is completely unaffected.

THAT FIX ALONE DIDN'T ACTUALLY MOVE THE FOOTER, THOUGH -- CHECKED LIVE, NOT ASSUMED. Re-measured after the `align-items` fix and the footer sat at the exact same pixel position as before. The reason: the sidebar is deliberately, correctly always a full `100vh` column -- that's not the bug, every other page in this whole redesign relies on it. Any page whose own content is shorter than `100vh` will always show blank canvas below its footer as an unavoidable consequence of standing next to a full-height sidebar, not a layout defect to "fix" away. The real gap was that the sign-in gate's own content sat top-aligned, with a chunk of dead space between it and the footer and more dead space below the footer -- reading as accidental, not designed. New `.lp-gate` class (`display:flex; align-items:center; justify-content:center; min-height: calc(100vh - 78px)`, 78px measured directly from the real rendered footer's own height) applied to `SignInPrompt`'s `<section>` in `AccountTabs.tsx`: the gate's own content now centers itself in the space actually available above the footer, the same "empty state fills the screen" pattern real apps use, instead of floating near the top with dead space trailing below it.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 390px mobile width, a full clean restart first: `.lp-gate`'s own box measured exactly `922px` (`1000 - 78`, confirming the calc is precise, not approximate), the footer's own top edge landed at exactly `922px` too -- a real, measured zero-pixel gap between the centered content's box and the footer, with the whole page ending exactly at the `1000px` viewport height, no extra scroll. A settled screenshot confirmed the content now reads as a deliberate, centered empty state. Zero horizontal overflow across twelve tabs checked (both content-rich and the account-gated ones); zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files and regions found zero matches.

Preceded by v3.231.0, `/do-not-sell` got the same "back to all documents" link every other legal page already has. Reported directly, against a screenshot of the page v3.230.0 had just moved onto the sidebar shell: "this page needs to have away back."

`LEGALPAGE.TSX` ALREADY HAD THIS EXACT LINK; `DONOTSELL.TSX` NEVER DID, SINCE IT'S ITS OWN BESPOKE COMPONENT, NOT RENDERED THROUGH THAT SHARED RENDERER. Every real legal document (`/terms`, `/privacy`, `/cookies`, and the rest, all sharing `LegalPage.tsx`) has carried a quiet "← All legal documents" link above its own heading since v3.32.0. `DoNotSell.tsx` was written as its own separate page from the start and never got the same treatment -- invisible while it still had no sidebar at all (v3.230.0's fix), but immediately obvious once it did, since every sibling page right next to it in the same nav now has a clear way back to the hub and this one didn't. Added the identical link, same icon, same copy, same position, directly above the `<h1>`.

Verified live: the link renders and correctly navigates to `/legal`, confirmed by clicking it and checking the resulting path, not just checking it exists in the DOM. Zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched file found only the one pre-existing, untouched line 1 comment.

Preceded by v3.230.0, the last real routes still on the old `<Header/>`/`<Footer/>` chrome brought onto the same sidebar shell as everything else. Reported directly, against a screenshot of the sidebar's new "Company" group: "add the legal page and make it within like the other pages."

`/LEGAL` AND ITS DOCUMENT PAGES WERE THE LAST GENUINE HOLDOUT OF THE ORIGINAL "DIFFERENT PAGE, SIDEBAR GONE" COMPLAINT. `LegalIndex.tsx` (the hub listing all eight documents) and `LegalPage.tsx` (the one renderer every individual document -- `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/cookies`, `/security`, `/subprocessors`, `/dpa`, `/sla`, `/copyright` -- shares) both still imported the old `Header`/`Footer` pair, the exact chrome `/jobs`, `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume` already moved off of at v3.220.0. Both swapped to the identical `SeekerSidebar`/`LandingFooter` + `.lp lp-shell-with-sidebar` pattern those three routes use, `LegalPage.tsx`'s own "document not written yet" fallback branch included, not just its main render path. `Header`/`Footer` are still real, still used by `LandingPage.tsx`'s employer branch (`/employers`, deliberately out of scope for this whole seeker-facing redesign) -- not deleted, just no longer the legal surface's own chrome.

FOUND ONE MORE HOLDOUT WHILE FIXING THIS ONE, NOT ASKED FOR BY NAME BUT DIRECTLY REACHABLE FROM THE PAGE JUST FIXED. `DoNotSell.tsx` (`/do-not-sell`, the CCPA rights page) is linked straight off the Legal hub's own list and was still `Footer`-only with no top nav of any kind. Leaving it as is would have meant clicking through from the newly-fixed Legal hub landed on the one remaining page that dropped the sidebar again -- the exact same gap, one hop later. Given the identical treatment.

A "LEGAL" LINK ADDED TO THE SIDEBAR'S OWN "COMPANY" GROUP, A REAL LINK LIKE `TOOL_LINKS`, NOT A TAB BUTTON LIKE ITS GROUP-MATES. `/legal` and its document pages need to stay real, individually-addressable, sitemap-listed URLs (the same reasoning that's kept `/jobs`/`/salary-guide`/`/check-resume` as real routes rather than tabs since v3.220.0) -- a legal document cited by version and effective date has to have a stable address, not client-side tab state. Added as a plain `<Link to="/legal">` inside the same "Company" `.lp-sidebar-group` div, matching `TOOL_LINKS`' own real-route pattern rather than `MORE_TAB_META`'s tab-switch buttons, with its own `Gavel` icon and an exact-path active check.

THE PRINT STYLESHEET NEEDED A REAL FIX, NOT JUST A SWAPPED IMPORT. `Header`/`Footer` were each wrapped by hand in a `.legal-noprint` div so a printed document never included site chrome. `SeekerSidebar` renders two `<aside>` elements plus a mobile top bar internally; wrapping the whole component in one extra `.legal-noprint` div would have made that div the flex child inside `.lp-shell-with-sidebar` instead of the sidebar itself, breaking the layout's own width/flex-basis rules. Fixed at the CSS level instead: `.lp-sidebar`, `.lp-sidebar-mobile-bar` and `.lp-footer` added directly to the existing `@media print` hide list, sitewide -- chrome nobody wants on a printed page regardless of which route it renders from, not a fix scoped only to legal pages.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 390px mobile width, a full clean restart first: `/legal` renders with the sidebar and all eight real documents listed, the new "Legal" link showing correctly active; `/sla` (a document with a real table) and `/terms` (21 sections, triggers the table-of-contents branch) both confirmed rendering correctly with the sidebar present; `/do-not-sell` confirmed the same; zero horizontal overflow on any of the four; zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise this session's tooling has produced throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file found two real em dashes in my own newly-written comments (fixed to match house style) and zero elsewhere outside pre-existing, unrelated content.

Preceded by v3.229.0, a real leftover-extension-language bug found and fixed, Get discovered and Messaging merged into one tab, and the sidebar's 19 links grouped under three plain labels instead of reading as one flat wall. Reported directly, against a screenshot of the seven explanation tabs: "this needs to be fixed i feel still talking about the google extantion also we need to reorgnaize the barmnue for better user experince."

THE EXTENSION COMPLAINT WAS RIGHT, EVEN THOUGH NO LITERAL WORD "EXTENSION" APPEARED ANYWHERE. Swept every seeker-facing copy string in `HomeTabs.tsx` and `landingContent.ts` for the literal words "extension"/"Chrome"/"install"/"sidepanel" first -- zero matches, confirmed the retired Chrome extension (gone since v3.164.0) isn't named anywhere. The actual problem was a FEELING, not a word: three separate lines described AYN reading "the specific posting you have open" and writing for "the one job in front of you" -- exactly the framing of a tool that watches whatever page is currently open in your browser, which is precisely how the old extension worked and precisely NOT how the product works today (a job is something you add to AYN -- browse it, paste a link, paste the text -- never something "open" elsewhere that AYN is passively watching). All three sat in or right next to `WhyAynTab`'s own "Real AI" section, confirming the user's instinct about exactly where the smell was strongest. Fixed: the heading ("Real AI, aimed at the one job in front of you" to "...aimed at one job at a time"), the body copy ("the specific posting you have open" to "the specific posting you added"), and the HeadToHead comparison row in `landingContent.ts` ("a resume rewritten for the one job in front of you" to "...for the one job you're applying to").

GET DISCOVERED AND MESSAGING MERGED INTO ONE TAB, THE ONE CLEAN, WELL-JUSTIFIED CONSOLIDATION OUT OF THE SEVEN EXPLANATION TABS. Re-read all seven tabs' actual content before picking what to merge, not guessed at: Features (the capability list) and How it works (the process sequence) are genuinely different framings of the same facts, legitimate to keep apart; Why AYN (pain/comparison/differentiation) and Proof (before/after evidence/trust/sourcing) are related but each substantial enough on its own that force-merging them risked a bloated, unfocused tab. Get discovered and Messaging were the one pair that was never really two ideas -- discovery is "turn this on and employers find you," messaging is literally "and here's what happens once one of them does," a direct continuation, not two people's separate slice. Merged into one `GetDiscoveredTab`, discovery's own content first, Messaging's own copy following immediately after with its eyebrow reworded from "When an employer reaches out" to "Once someone reaches out" so it reads as the next beat of the same story instead of a second, disconnected topic. `messaging` removed from `HomeTabId`, `TAB_META`, `HOME_TAB_CONTENT`, and `SeekerSidebar`'s icon map; the standalone `MessagingTab` export and its now-unused `MessageSquare` icon import are both gone. Seven explanation tabs down to six.

THE SIDEBAR'S 19 LINKS NOW READ AS FOUR NAMED GROUPS, NOT ONE FLAT LIST. The visual separation (a border between clusters) already existed; there was never a text label saying what each cluster actually was. New `.lp-sidebar-group-label` (small, uppercase, muted, matching the same treatment `.lp-hero-results-label` already used elsewhere in this exact design system) above three of the four groups -- "Your account" (Profile through Settings), "Learn about AYN" (Features through FAQ), "Company" (Pricing through Help) -- with the top group (Job search, Check my resume, Salary guide) deliberately left unlabeled as the page's own primary, default-landing area, the same convention most sidebar-driven apps already use for their pinned/default section. Hidden entirely in the collapsed icon-only rail state (`.lp-sidebar.is-collapsed .lp-sidebar-group-label { display: none }`), matching how every other text label in the collapsed sidebar already behaves.

Verified live end to end at a real 1500px viewport and a real 390px mobile width, a full clean restart first (the same stale-HMR precaution this file's history keeps needing before trusting a console read): all three group labels render correctly and in the right groups on both desktop and the mobile drawer; the collapsed sidebar correctly hides them; Get discovered's own page now contains both the discovery copy and the inbox copy in one continuous scroll, confirmed by checking for both pieces of text in the same tab's rendered output; Why AYN's fixed heading and body copy both confirmed present, the old "posting you have open" and "job in front of you" phrasing confirmed completely gone; zero horizontal overflow across all seventeen tabs checked; zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise this session's tooling has produced all along. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file found zero new matches outside pre-existing, unrelated comments.

Preceded by v3.228.0, THE piece flagged and deferred since v3.219.0's own entry, finally built: signing in no longer swaps a job seeker onto a completely separate dashboard. Reported directly: "i dont see the perposal and the assmint and the profile and the other pages for the jobseeker you can have them and in order to be shown they need to sign in or signup," alongside "i want you also to merge pages that can fit with each others."

THIS IS THE ACTUAL CLOSE OF THE COMPLAINT THIS WHOLE REDESIGN EFFORT STARTED FROM. The very first message of this whole thread was "you see how the website is the same page if you sign or not only things wont open if you did not sign in or sign up." Every pass since (Pricing/Contact/About/Help becoming tabs, `/jobs`/`/salary-guide`/`/check-resume` keeping the sidebar, the footer, the spacing, the visuals) fixed a piece of that around the edges. The one piece that was never actually touched -- the real cause of "I see a different dashboard" -- was `Index.tsx`'s own `AuthedShell`: a signed-in job seeker was unconditionally `<Navigate to="/resume-hub" replace />`'d into a completely separate shell (`ResumeHub.tsx`, its own `.rh-shell`/`.rh-topbar`/`.rh-grid`/`.rh-aside-left` layout, a different icon rail, no `SeekerSidebar` anywhere in it) the instant they signed in. That line is gone; `AuthedShell` now returns the exact same `<LandingPage />` a signed-out visitor sees.

SIX NEW TABS, EVERY ONE A REAL, ALREADY-PROVEN COMPONENT REUSED AS-IS, NOT REWRITTEN. New `src/components/landing/AccountTabs.tsx`: Profile, Job matches, Saved jobs, Proposals, Assessments, Settings, each wrapping the identical `ProfileTab`/`BrowseJobs`/`JobsTab`/`ProposalsTab`/`AssessmentsTab`/`SettingsPanel` components `/resume-hub` already used for years, imported lazily exactly as before -- nothing about how these actually work changed, only where they're mounted from. `BrowseJobs.tsx` (real match scores against the signed-in user's own resume) got its own tab, "Job matches," rather than being dropped -- it was `/resume-hub`'s sixth tab and would have quietly become unreachable UI if left out; named distinctly from "Job search" (the existing public tab) specifically to not recreate the exact duplicate-nav-item problem v3.223.0 had just fixed.

"YOU CAN HAVE THEM AND IN ORDER TO BE SHOWN THEY NEED TO SIGN IN OR SIGNUP" IS A REAL, PER-TAB GATE, NOT A PAGE-LEVEL ONE. All six nav items are always visible in `SeekerSidebar`, signed in or not -- clicking one while signed out shows a plain, centered "Sign in to see this" prompt right there in the tab (a new `SignInPrompt` component, styled in the page's own `.lp` system since auth state isn't resolved yet at that point), with one button that opens the same shared `AuthModal` every other "Start free" on the site already uses. Signed in, the gate is invisible and the real component renders. `.resume-hub-theme` (the `--rh-*` token scope these five components all read, plus `resume-hub.css` itself) is applied to `<body>` only while one of these tabs is actually mounted -- the same `.contact-surface`/`.employer-surface` trick this codebase has used several times before for exactly this kind of scoped-theme-inside-a-different-page problem, needed here because Radix portals (dialogs, selects) render straight onto `<body>`, outside the tab's own component tree.

`/RESUME-HUB` ITSELF NOW REDIRECTS, THE SAME `HOMETABREDIRECT` PATTERN `/PRICING` ETC. ALREADY USE. Old links, bookmarks, and the three real in-app call sites that still navigate there (`Billing.tsx`'s "back" button, `Employers.tsx`'s seeker bounce, `NotFound.tsx`'s 404 link -- checked by grep, all three left untouched since the route itself still resolves correctly) all land on `/` with "Saved jobs" pre-selected instead of a 404 or a dead shell. `src/pages/ResumeHub.tsx` itself -- confirmed by grep to have zero remaining importers anywhere once `App.tsx`'s own route was updated -- is deleted outright, matching this codebase's own consistent convention (the same thing that happened to `Pricing.tsx`/`Contact.tsx`/`About.tsx`/`Help.tsx` when they became tabs). `BrowseJobs.tsx` the FILE stays, untouched -- confirmed via grep that `SalaryGuide.tsx`, `PublicJobs.tsx`, `JobsBrowser.tsx` and `LiveJobsPreview.tsx` all still import real, live utility exports from it (`humanizeCategory`, `companyAvatar`, and others); only its own default-exported page component was orphaned by the shell going away, and that's exactly what the new "Job matches" tab now mounts instead.

`AccountPreferences.tsx`'s OWN "EDIT IN PROFILE" LINK WAS QUIETLY BROKEN BY THIS CHANGE, FOUND AND FIXED BEFORE SHIPPING. It set a sessionStorage key, `ayn_open_tab`, that only `ResumeHub.tsx` (now deleted) and `EmployerHub.tsx` ever read -- with the old shell gone, a seeker's "Edit in Profile" click from Settings would have silently landed on Home with no tab pre-selected, a real, if narrow, regression this pass would otherwise have shipped. Fixed by having the seeker branch write the same `HOME_TAB_HANDOFF_KEY` every other cross-page tab link already uses; the employer branch (`ayn_open_tab`, still read by the untouched `EmployerHub.tsx`) is unchanged.

A SECOND REAL BUG FOUND LIVE, NOT BY READING THE CODE: THE HANDOFF VALIDATION SET NEVER LEARNED ABOUT THE NEW TABS. `LandingPage.tsx`'s `ALL_TAB_IDS` (the allowlist `readHandoffTab()` checks a stashed tab id against before trusting it) was built from only `TAB_META`/`MORE_TAB_META`, never updated to include the new `ACCOUNT_TAB_META`. Every handoff into one of the six new tabs -- `/resume-hub`'s own redirect included -- silently failed this check and fell back to plain "Job search," with no error anywhere to say why. Caught by testing the actual redirect live, not by reading the code: navigated to `/resume-hub`, watched it land on Job search instead of Saved jobs. Fixed by folding `ACCOUNT_TAB_META` into the same allowlist; re-verified the identical redirect afterward and confirmed Saved jobs correctly active with its own sign-in gate showing.

WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN LEFT UNSAID. The signed-in render path for all six tabs -- confirmed clean by `tsc`, confirmed correctly *wired* (every prop the real components expect is supplied, matching exactly what `/resume-hub` used to pass), and every underlying component is the identical, already-production-proven one `/resume-hub` ran for years -- was NOT click-through tested signed in during this pass. This session's own tooling refuses to type into a password field, the same wall this file's history has hit before (v3.86.0, v3.106.0), and no throwaway session could be minted without one. What WAS verified live, repeatedly, across two full clean-restart cycles: the signed-out gate on all six tabs, the `/resume-hub` redirect, the sidebar's full 20-item list, zero horizontal overflow at both a real 1500px and a real 390px viewport, and zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise this session's tooling has produced throughout. The founder's own first real sign-in is the actual final proof this works end to end, and is flagged here as the one thing still outstanding, not silently assumed.

THE SECOND HALF OF THE SAME MESSAGE -- "MERGE PAGES THAT CAN FIT WITH EACH OTHERS" -- IS DELIBERATELY NOT ATTEMPTED IN THIS PASS. Read as a request to consolidate the seven explanation tabs (Features, How it works, Why AYN, Get discovered, Messaging, Proof, FAQ) where genuine overlap exists. Given the size of what already shipped in this same pass (a real routing change to the sign-in path, six new tabs, a deleted page, two real bugs found and fixed along the way), bundling a second large content-restructuring change into the same deploy risked compounding, harder-to-isolate regressions. Flagged honestly as real, separate, outstanding work rather than attempted alongside this.

Preceded by v3.227.0, Features' own lead tile rebalanced -- the real, root cause of the "half cut" card the last two entries had been treating symptomatically. Reported against a live screenshot of the collapsed sidebar showing that exact tile: "i dont like pages designs feels like half cut landing page we need to make each page proper design and font labels redsign all page better layout and spacing and hight for all the pages in the website."

THE v3.224.0 FIX TREATED A SYMPTOM; THIS ONE FOUND AND FIXED THE ACTUAL CAUSE. v3.224.0 measured the lead tile at a disproportionate 1104x248px and responded by scaling up its own type and padding, reasoning the card just needed more visual weight to match a span it already claimed. Re-measured live this round, past-the-text-column rather than just width and height: the tile's real content (icon, one heading line, one description line, three pills) maxed out at 609px of a 1260px-wide card -- 651px of genuinely empty space to its right, bigger type and padding barely moved the number. Checked its five sibling tiles in the same grid the same way: every one of them filled its own row to within about 33px, no dead space at all. The lead tile wasn't under-styled, it was over-spanned -- one tile claiming a full 1260px row (`lp-span-6`) for content shaped exactly like its own span-3 (622px) siblings.

REBALANCED, NOT RESTYLED: ALL SIX `SEEKER_TILES` NOW SPAN-3, A CLEAN 2x3 GRID. Chose 622px specifically because it's a width already proven, in this exact grid, to fit icon+title+description(+pills) content with no slack -- not a new number to hope was right. Confirmed via `grid-column: span` auto-placement math before touching the data: six tiles at span-3 in a 6-column grid sum to exactly three full rows (3+3, 3+3, 3+3), no dangling trailing gap the way a partial rebalance (e.g. shrinking just the one tile without adjusting its siblings) would have produced. The v3.224.0 `.lp-tile.lp-span-6` type/padding overrides are deleted along with it -- nothing carries that class any more, and every tile now looks right using the same plain `.lp-tile` styling its five (already-correct) siblings always used.

Verified live end to end at a real 1600px viewport, a full clean restart first (the same stale-HMR precaution this file's last several entries have all needed): all six tiles now measure a uniform 622px wide with a consistent ~589px text-right-edge, confirmed via direct measurement, not eyeballed; a settled screenshot shows a clean, evenly-weighted 2-column grid with zero disproportionate card; zero horizontal overflow across all twelve tabs checked (search, Features, How it works, Why AYN, Get discovered, Messaging, Proof, FAQ, Pricing, Contact, About, Help) at both 1600px and a real 390px mobile width; grepped `landingContent.ts` and `LandingSections.tsx` for any other `.lp-bento`/oversized-span usage before calling this scoped correctly -- none found, the disproportionate-tile pattern was isolated to this one grid. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero new matches outside pre-existing, unrelated comments.

THE REST OF THE REQUEST -- "REDESIGN ALL PAGE... FOR ALL THE PAGES IN THE WEBSITE" -- IS DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED DONE HERE. This pass fixed the one concrete, measured, screenshot-evidenced defect (Features' own oversized tile) and swept the codebase for the same specific bug pattern elsewhere, finding none. It did not audit every page's typography, spacing and layout choices against some new design language, which is what "redesign all pages... better layout and spacing and height" actually asks for at full scope -- a genuinely large, open-ended design pass across roughly twenty distinct pages/tabs, not a single measurable bug. Flagged honestly rather than silently treated as covered by this entry; a next round naming specific pages or continuing systematically through the rest of the site is real, separate, outstanding work.

Preceded by v3.226.0, a real doubled top padding fixed across every tab, and the Contact form widened to match. Reported directly, against a live screenshot of Get Discovered: "we still have all the pages things start in the middle not at the top change it make everything start from the top of the page and make the conct us bigger."

MEASURED THE DOUBLE PADDING LIVE BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING, SAME DISCIPLINE AS v3.224.0. `getComputedStyle` on the real rendered page: the `.lp-audience` wrapper every tab renders inside (`LandingSections.tsx`) carried its own `paddingBlockStart: clamp(40px, 6vw, 72px)`, and every real tab's own first element is `<section className="lp-section">`, which already carries `padding-block: clamp(72px, 11vw, 132px)` -- two separate top paddings stacking, up to 204px combined, against Home's own search view (which uses only the first of those two, capped at 72px) -- tabs were carrying close to three times Home's own top spacing before a single word of content appeared. Confirmed live: the eyebrow on Get Discovered sat at `top: 240.5px` before the fix, matching what the screenshot showed.

FIXED BY REMOVING THE REDUNDANT OUTER PADDING, NOT BY TRIMMING EACH TAB'S OWN SECTION. The wrapper's `paddingBlockStart` is gone entirely -- `.lp-section`'s own top padding was already the real, correct spacing every tab needs (the same amount every non-tab page that opens with `.lp-section` already uses), so the fix is one line, not eleven separate edits to every tab component. Verified live: the same eyebrow now sits at `top: 168.5px`, a real 72px drop, matching the removed padding exactly; every other tab checked (Features, Why AYN, Messaging, Proof, FAQ, About, Help, Pricing) landed at the identical ~169px, confirming the fix is uniform across the board, not just the one tab in the screenshot. `How it works` sits a bit lower (248px) than its siblings -- not a regression, `.lp-split`'s own `align-items: center` (deliberate, so a short text column and a taller mockup read as balanced side by side, not top-jammed) vertically centers its text column against `TailoredDocsMockup`'s own taller footprint; still a real, large improvement over the roughly 240-300px gap every tab carried before this pass.

CONTACT FORM WIDENED 720px TO 960px, THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME MESSAGE. `ContactTab`'s own `.lp-shell` had a leftover `maxWidth: 720` from when this was its own standalone, narrower-scale route (`Contact.tsx`, deleted at v3.219.0 when it became a tab) -- every other tab now reaches the full 1360px shell, but this one was still squeezed to barely half a normal reading column. Confirmed `TicketForm.tsx` itself has no internal width cap of its own (every `Input`/`Select`/`Textarea`/`Button` is `w-full`), so the wider shell genuinely grows the form's own fields, not just the whitespace around them -- verified live, the Category/Priority select row visibly widened along with everything else.

Verified live end to end at a real 1600px viewport, a full clean restart first (same stale-HMR precaution the last two entries both needed): zero console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise, zero horizontal overflow across every tab checked, two settled screenshots (Get Discovered and Contact, both needing a second capture past the same reveal-transition timing this file's own history already documents) confirming the visual result directly. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. TWO NEW TAB VISUALS, BOTH GROUNDED IN COPY THAT WAS ALREADY THERE, NEITHER INVENTING A NEW CLAIM FOR THE GRAPHIC.
