Why AYN got a new one, `SameResumeMockup`: a labeled two-panel diagram, "THE USUAL WAY" (one resume, a dotted line fanning to five identical grey squares, each with a small dash meaning nothing happened) against "THE AYN WAY" (one resume, an ember line to a single highlighted square with a checkmark) -- its own caption is `PAIN.job_seeker.lines[0]` verbatim, "Same resume, forty postings, no replies," the tab's own opening line, not new copy written for the graphic. Same discipline `CandidateCardMockup`'s own comment already states for this file: a diagram of the real mechanism, never an invented person or a fabricated specific.

BOTH TABS' OPENING BLOCK RESTRUCTURED INTO `.LP-SPLIT`, THE SAME PATTERN MESSAGING AND GET DISCOVERED ALREADY USE. `HowItWorksTab`'s intro (eyebrow/h2/lead) now sits in the text column of a `.lp-split` with `TailoredDocsMockup` in the art column, ahead of the existing 4-step `.lp-flow` grid, unchanged below it. `WhyAynTab`'s intro plus its own "If you are applying" pain-points list moved together into the text column (previously two separate `.lp-reveal` blocks stacked vertically; now one column, so the list sits directly under the paragraph it explains) with `SameResumeMockup` in the art column; the HeadToHead comparison table and the "Real AI" section below are unchanged, unmoved.

Verified live end to end at a real 1600px viewport, a full clean restart first (`preview_stop`/`rm -rf node_modules/.vite`/`preview_start`) to rule out the same stale-HMR false alarm this file's last two entries both hit: both mockups render correctly (confirmed via `aria-label` text and two separate screenshots, the first catching mid-reveal-transition exactly like v3.222.0's own note on this, a second confirming settled), no console errors beyond the same known-benign resource noise, and zero horizontal overflow across Features/Get discovered/Messaging/Proof/FAQ (unaffected by the shared `AppMockups.tsx` import) at 1600px and across Why AYN/How it works themselves at a real 390px mobile width, where `.lp-split`'s own existing breakpoint correctly stacks both to one column. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Preceded by v3.224.0, the seeker content column widened so it actually fills a wide monitor instead of floating small inside one. Reported directly, against six real screenshots taken on production at what reads as a large external display: "this feels wierd the text the mackup and everything feels small compare to the page we need to redsign things that fit in every page nicely not small in a big space."

MEASURED THE ACTUAL DEAD SPACE LIVE BEFORE TOUCHING ANY CSS, NOT GUESSED FROM THE SCREENSHOTS. Reproduced the same wide-monitor scenario locally at a real 1920px viewport (this session's own browser tooling defaults to a much narrower effective width otherwise, a known limitation this file's history already documents -- an explicit `resize_window` call was needed to get a genuine 1920px read). `getBoundingClientRect()` on the real rendered page, not pixel-counting a photo: sidebar 244px, `.lp-shell` capped at a flat 1200px, leaving exactly 238px of pure, unused margin on EACH side of the actual content -- close to a quarter of the available main-content width sitting empty on both flanks, on every one of the tabs the screenshots showed (they all share the same `.lp-shell`/`.lp-section` wrapper). Separately measured the Features tab's own lead tile (`lp-span-6`, full width, the one screenshot showing a big near-empty white card): a real, live 1104x248px, a 4.4:1 flat bar for content that's just one icon, one heading line, one description line and three small pills, huddled in the top-left corner with nothing using the other half of the card's own width.

`.LP-SHELL` WIDENED 1200px TO 1360px, `MIN()`-EQUIVALENT BY CONSTRUCTION SO IT ONLY EVER HELPS A WIDE SCREEN. A flat `max-width` bump is a strict no-op below the new cap -- confirmed live at a real, typical 1440px laptop width: `.lp-shell` still measured its natural fluid 1196px, never touching 1360px, byte-for-byte the same layout a visitor on an ordinary laptop already saw before this change. At 1920px, the same measurement dropped from 238px of dead margin per side to 158px -- a real, meaningful third less wasted space -- and, since `.lp-split`'s two-column mockup layout (Messaging, Get discovered, Proof) sizes itself as a fraction of the shell's own width, its mockup column grew from 541px to 623px for free, no separate mockup-specific fix needed. Confirmed zero horizontal overflow at both 1920px and 1440px afterward.

THE FEATURES LEAD TILE GOT REAL VISUAL WEIGHT INSTEAD OF A NARROWER SPAN. Reworking the whole `6+3+3+2+2+2` bento grid to give this one tile a smaller span would have meant rebalancing every sibling tile's own width for a problem that was really about this one tile's content density, not the grid's shape -- so it kept its full-width span and instead got scaled-up padding, icon, heading and body type, plus bigger meta pills (`.lp-tile.lp-span-6` overrides in `index.css`), the same "give the hero tile real priority" treatment `TrustBento`'s own `.is-lead` tile already uses one selector over. Verified live: 1104x248 (4.45:1) became 1264x304 (4.16:1) with the heading growing from 22px to 26px -- a real, measured improvement, not total, since making genuinely short, sparse content (one icon, two lines, three chips) fill a still-very-wide card fully would need an actual illustration added to it, which is real design work, not a CSS tuning pass, and wasn't attempted here.

NOT DONE, FLAGGED HONESTLY RATHER THAN GUESSED AT. The screenshots also showed Why AYN and How it works reading sparse -- both are genuinely text-only tabs (no mockup, no illustration, just headline plus short copy or four step cards), and no amount of column-widening changes that; the shell fix helps their surrounding proportions but doesn't add visual weight to content that's inherently light. Giving either tab a real visual would be new creative work (an illustration, a mockup, a bigger stat treatment), not a follow-on tuning pass to what shipped here -- open for a next round if wanted, not silently treated as already covered.

Verified live end to end: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (CSS-only change); a real screenshot of the Messaging tab at 1920px confirmed the mockup and text column now genuinely fill more of the canvas, not floating in a visibly narrower strip; console clean of anything beyond the same known-benign resource noise this session's tooling has produced throughout. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched CSS regions found zero matches outside the file's own existing house-style comment dashes.

Preceded by v3.223.0, "Browse jobs" removed as its own sidebar entry -- it was the same tool as "Job search," just wearing a second name. Reported directly, plainly: "why we have job search and browser delete the browser only the job search is the browser not having two," paired with a broader "make all the pages fit in the right way... make things look perfect... attract people" ask.

`SEEKERSIDEBAR.TSX`'S OWN "JOB SEARCH" AND "BROWSE JOBS" WERE THE SAME COMPONENT UNDER TWO LABELS. "Job search" (the sidebar's top item, `selectTab('search')`) lands on Home and renders `<JobsBrowser showHeading asH1 />` embedded directly on the page. "Browse jobs" (`TOOL_LINKS`, a plain `<Link to="/jobs">`) opened `PublicJobs.tsx`, which renders the exact same `<JobsBrowser .../>` component, just wrapped in its own route for SEO reasons. Two sidebar rows, one real tool -- confirmed by reading both render paths side by side, not assumed from the labels alone. `{ to: '/jobs', label: 'Browse jobs', icon: Briefcase }` is deleted from `TOOL_LINKS`, and the now-unused `Briefcase` icon import went with it.

`/JOBS` ITSELF IS UNTOUCHED, ON PURPOSE -- THIS WAS A NAV-DUPLICATION FIX, NOT A ROUTE DELETION. Per this file's own v3.220.0 entry, `/jobs` (plus its `/jobs/:id`, `/jobs/category/:x`, `/jobs/location/:x` variants) stays a real, separately reachable, individually-crawlable route: Google's `JobPosting` structured data only works on a real URL, never a client-side tab; `public/sitemap.xml` and the category/location hub pages both depend on it; `CheckResume.tsx`'s and `SalaryGuide.tsx`'s own cross-link buttons ("Check my resume against a job" / "See real salary data by role") still point straight at it. None of that plumbing needed touching -- the fix was removing the redundant *link into* it from a sidebar that already has a working link to the same tool, not removing the tool's own separate address.

A REAL, LIVE STALE-HMR ARTIFACT HIT AND RULED OUT DURING VERIFICATION, THE SAME METHOD AS v3.222.0'S EARLIER FALSE ALARM. Right after the `Briefcase` import was removed, a console check turned up a real-looking `ReferenceError: Briefcase is not defined` plus an `[hmr] Failed to reload /src/components/landing/SeekerSidebar.tsx` warning. Checked the same way this file's own history says to check this exact failure shape rather than assumed broken or assumed fine: a full `preview_stop` / `rm -rf node_modules/.vite` / `preview_start` restart plus a genuinely fresh tab. Clean on the fresh tab -- confirmed stale dev-server module state left over from mid-edit, not a defect in the shipped code.

A WIDER PAGE-FIT SWEEP FOR THE SECOND, VAGUER HALF OF THE SAME MESSAGE. Checked `document.body.scrollWidth` against `window.innerWidth` (the direct, reliable horizontal-overflow signal, not eyeballed) across every real standalone route -- `/`, `/jobs`, `/salary-guide`, `/check-resume`, `/employers`, `/legal`, `/sla` (the one legal document most likely to overflow, since it's the only one carrying a real `<table>`) -- at both a real desktop width (1280px) and the narrowest width this session's own browser tooling can actually produce (confirmed, same known limitation this file's history already documents, ~515-519px regardless of a requested 375px "mobile" preset). Zero overflow found anywhere. No other concrete "doesn't fit" example was given this round, so nothing beyond the confirmed nav duplication was changed -- flagged here rather than guessed at, since "make it look perfect" without a specific target is a real design decision, not a bug to patch blind.

Verified live end to end against the clean restart: the desktop sidebar's own link list confirmed to read Job search, Check my resume, Salary guide, Features... with no "Browse jobs" row anywhere; `/jobs` itself still loads correctly and independently (real `<h1>Browse real jobs</h1>`, a real 12,212-character page body) when reached directly, confirming the route survives even though nothing in the sidebar points at it any more. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched file found zero matches outside its own existing house-style comment dashes.

Preceded by v3.222.0, the TrustBento stat strip and its closing CTA moved off Home and off every tab, onto Features only. Reported against the same screenshot as v3.221.0 (the "3 days / 1,000+ / 2 hrs / Zero / $0" tiles plus the ember "Stop sending the same resume into the dark" band): "this needs to be moved to featuers not in this page and not in every pages."

TWO SEPARATE OVER-EXPOSURE BUGS IN THE SAME BLOCK, FOUND BY READING WHERE EACH ONE ACTUALLY RENDERED. `TrustBento` sat inside `LandingSections.tsx`'s `{seeker && (...)}` body block, so it only ever rendered on Home (the search tab) -- not "every page," but still exactly the one page the screenshot was taken on and the one page it needed to stop showing on. The closing CTA sat further down the same file, genuinely unconditional (`v3.219.0`'s own comment even named this on purpose: "the one CTA every tab and every audience ends on, not repeated per tab") -- so it rendered on Home AND on every one of the eleven other tabs (Contact, About, Help, Pricing, FAQ, and the rest), which is the literal "every pages" half of the report.

BOTH MOVED INTO `HOMETABS.TSX`'S `FEATURESTAB`, THE ONE TAB THIS CONTENT NOW BELONGS TO. `FeaturesTab` went from a bare `() => (...)` with no props to `({ onStartFree }: TabProps) => (...)`, the same structural-typing allowance every other tab already relies on, just now actually using the callback instead of ignoring it. `<TrustBento />` and the closing-CTA section (identical markup and copy to what `LandingSections.tsx` used to render for the seeker case) both moved in as-is, appended after the existing feature tiles inside one `<>...</>` fragment. `LandingSections.tsx`'s own closing-CTA section is now `{!seeker && (...)}` only, with the seeker/employer ternary collapsed to the employer copy alone ("Stop digging through a pile of resumes...") -- the employer's single-page `/employers` route has no tab system to scope a closer to, so it keeps its own unconditional one exactly as before, untouched in substance. The `TrustBento` import moved from `LandingSections.tsx` to `HomeTabs.tsx` alongside it.

A REAL BUG SURFACED DURING VERIFICATION, RULED OUT BEFORE TRUSTING IT, THEN CONFIRMED STALE. Right after both edits landed, a live console check showed a real-looking `ReferenceError: TrustBento is not defined` thrown from inside `<LandingSections>`, tripping the `ErrorBoundary`. Rather than assume the fix was broken or assume it was noise, it was checked the way this file's own history says to check this exact failure shape: a full `preview_stop`/`rm -rf node_modules/.vite`/`preview_start` restart plus a genuinely fresh tab. The error did not reproduce -- confirmed stale Vite HMR state left over from mid-edit (the dev server's own bundled-deps chunk still holding a reference to the now-removed `TrustBento` import in `LandingSections.tsx` from before the second edit landed), not a real defect in the shipped code.

Verified live end to end against the clean restart: Home, Contact, About, Help, Pricing and FAQ all confirmed to contain neither the TrustBento copy ("is the longest a listing survives...") nor the closing-CTA copy ("Stop sending the same resume into the dark") anywhere in their rendered text; Features confirmed to contain both, with the TrustBento tile grid's live job count rendering correctly and the closing CTA's real ember gradient (`background-image`, not `background-color` -- the first computed-style check read the wrong property and had to be redone) confirmed applied; clicking "Start free" on the Features tab's own CTA opened the real shared `AuthModal`, not a second instance. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero new matches outside pre-existing code comments.

Preceded by v3.221.0, the footer stripped down to a single line, on a direct design instruction. Posted against a screenshot of Home's own closing section: "now as a designer i want you to remove the the fotter and only keep the © 2026 AYN AI. All rights reserved. Privacy Policy Cookie choices the rest remove it no need for it also remove the image one from all pages just make sure we have other pages present ayn in the right way everything fit the pages looks good."

`LANDINGFOOTER.TSX` LOST ITS BRAND BLOCK AND BOTH NAV COLUMNS, KEEPING ONLY THE ONE ROW THAT'S GENUINELY FOOTER-ONLY CONTENT. The reasoning behind removing rather than just trimming: `SeekerSidebar` already carries the real AYN mark on every one of these pages now (Home and every HomeTabs tab, plus `/jobs`, `/salary-guide`, `/check-resume` and `/employers` since v3.219.0/v3.220.0), and every link the old footer repeated -- Home, Pricing, Contact, About, Help, Legal -- already lives in the sidebar too. A second copy of the same nav sitting at the bottom of the page was pure repetition, not a second, different use, so the second AYN logo image, the tagline, the three social icons, and the Navigate/Company columns are gone outright, not just restyled smaller. What's left: the copyright line plus Privacy Policy and Cookie choices, the two links nothing else on the page carries. `.lp-footer-top`, `.lp-footer-brand*`, `.lp-footer-social*`, `.lp-footer-cols`, `.lp-footer-col*` all deleted from `index.css` once grep confirmed `LandingFooter.tsx` was their only consumer; `.lp-footer`/`.lp-footer-bottom`'s own padding and border were retuned so a now-single-row footer doesn't carry a doubled border or leftover padding sized for the taller footer that used to sit above it.

SCOPED TO THE ONE FOOTER THE COMPLAINT WAS ABOUT, NOT THE SEPARATE SHARED `FOOTER.TSX`. This app has always had two different footer components, not one: `LandingFooter.tsx` (`.lp-footer`, the `.lp`-scoped one every seeker-facing page above uses, including `/employers` via `LandingSections`) and a separate `src/components/shared/Footer.tsx` (used on `/legal` and its document pages, and other non-`.lp` surfaces) that still carries its full brand block and Navigate/Company columns, deliberately untouched -- the screenshot and every page named in the instruction were all `.lp` pages, and `Footer.tsx` was never part of what was shown or asked about. `COMPANY_TAGLINE`/`NAV_LINKS`/`COMPANY_LINKS` in `siteLinks.ts` stayed exactly as they were, still read by that other component.

Verified live end to end against a fresh dev server: the new single-row footer confirmed correct (no image, no old brand/cols markup, exact copyright plus two links, no doubled border) on Home's search tab, the Pricing/Contact/About/Help tabs, `/employers`, and `/jobs`; the AYN mark confirmed still present via `SeekerSidebar` on every one of those; `/legal`'s own `Footer.tsx` confirmed completely unaffected, still showing its full brand block and both nav columns. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of both touched files found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.220.0, the sidebar stopped disappearing on the three pages that genuinely need to stay real, crawlable routes. Direct continuation of the same instruction, second slice of "yes build it": `/jobs` (plus its category/location/:id variants), `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume` all used to swap `SeekerSidebar` for the old `<Header/>`/`<Footer/>` chrome the instant you landed on one -- exactly the thing reported, just not yet reached in the first slice (v3.219.0 only handled the four pages with zero SEO reason to be a route at all).

WHY THESE THREE STAY ROUTES INSTEAD OF BECOMING TABS. Unlike Pricing/Contact/About/Help, these three have a real, load-bearing reason to keep their own URL: `/jobs/:id` carries Google's own `JobPosting` structured data, which Google's docs are explicit only works on a real, individually-crawlable URL, never a client-side tab; the category/location hub pages and `public/sitemap.xml` both depend on real paths; `/salary-guide` is this app's own deliberate content-marketing asset, built specifically to earn backlinks from a real, shareable URL. Converting any of the three to tab state would have quietly broken all of that to chase a literal reading of "everything" that the actual complaint never required -- the complaint was the sidebar vanishing, not that these pages have their own address.

`SEEKERSIDEBAR.TSX` HAD TO WORK ON A ROUTE THAT ISN'T HOME, NOT JUST ON HOME. Both its props are now optional: `activeTab`/`onSelectTab` are only ever passed by `LandingPage.tsx` (Home itself); on any other route, `selectTab()` falls back to the exact same `sessionStorage` handoff `HomeTabRedirect` already uses (stash the tab in `HOME_TAB_HANDOFF_KEY`, `navigate('/')`) -- clicking Features while standing on `/jobs` now does the correct thing, land on Home already showing Features, instead of either doing nothing or throwing on a missing callback. `HOME_TAB_HANDOFF_KEY` itself moved out of `LandingPage.tsx` and into `HomeTabs.tsx`, the one file already common to all three consumers (`LandingPage`, `SeekerSidebar`, `HomeTabRedirect`) -- keeping it in `LandingPage.tsx` and having `SeekerSidebar` import it back would have been a circular import, since `LandingPage` already imports `SeekerSidebar` directly.

`PUBLICJOBS.TSX`, `SALARYGUIDE.TSX` AND `CHECKRESUME.TSX` ALL SWAPPED `<Header/>`/`<Footer/>` FOR `<SeekerSidebar/>`/`<LandingFooter/>`, WRAPPED IN THE SAME `.lp lp-shell-with-sidebar` LAYOUT HOME ITSELF USES. Everything else -- the real Supabase queries, the `JobPosting` schema, the thin-hub `noIndex` gate, `SalaryGuide`'s live `job_market_snapshot()` numbers, `CheckResume`'s real keyword-match tool -- untouched, only the chrome around it changed. One real, pre-existing gap found and fixed along the way, not caused by this pass: `/jobs` had never actually had a real `<h1>` at all (confirmed live, `document.querySelector('h1')` returned null) -- `JobsBrowser`'s own `showHeading` heading has always rendered as a plain `<h2>` here, since nothing on this page supplied an `<h1>` of its own. Fixed with the same `asH1` prop v3.218.0 already added for Home's own use of this component. `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume` both already had their own explicit `<h1>`, untouched.

Verified live end to end against a fresh dev server, this time with real production data actually loading (local dev isn't blocked by the CSP cross-origin limitation the production-build reproduction hit in v3.218.0/v3.219.0 -- that header is only set by `server.js`, which the dev server doesn't run): all three routes render the sidebar with the correct nav item highlighted active and exactly one `<h1>`; clicking a tab button (Features, Pricing, Job search) while on any of the three correctly navigates to `/` and lands on the right tab, confirmed with a long-enough wait after the click (a too-short wait read the state mid-transition and looked broken -- not a real bug, a test timing mistake, corrected before trusting the result); `/employers` confirmed completely untouched; mobile confirmed on `/jobs`, real live job postings rendering correctly under the same mobile top bar Home uses. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean throughout. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file found zero new matches outside code comments.

NOT YET DONE, THE LAST AND BIGGEST PIECE: the `/resume-hub` hard redirect on sign-in, and the six pages that only exist there (Profile, Saved jobs, Proposals, Assessments, Settings, and a second, richer "Browse jobs" with match scores) -- this is the actual "sign in and I see a different dashboard" fix, not yet started.

Preceded by v3.219.0, Pricing/Contact/About/Help became tabs on Home instead of separate pages, and a real, previously-shipped bug was found and fixed along the way: every HomeTabs tab's own content had been rendering at `opacity: 0` since v3.216.0. Reported directly, comparing the site to Indeed's own model: "you see how the website is the same page if you sign or not only things wont open if you did not sign in or sign up" -- then, on being told what that gap actually was (a hard redirect on sign-in to a second, differently-shelled app), a direct order: "yes build it i dont want the pages to open in a diffrent page than the sidebar everything should open with[in] the first page i have the sidebar i see a pricing i click it show pricing dose not take me to a diffrent page where i dont see the sidebar anymore."

FIRST SLICE OF THE MERGE: EVERY PAGE THAT HAD NO REAL REASON TO BE A SEPARATE ROUTE, ISN'T ONE ANYMORE. `/pricing`, `/contact`, `/about` and `/help` (plus `/support`, already a redirect to `/help`) used to render their own full page, `<Header/>`/`<Footer/>` chrome, no `SeekerSidebar` at all -- the exact thing reported. All four are real routes for the SEO/deep-linking pages (`/jobs`, `/salary-guide`, `/check-resume`) too, but these four specifically have zero SEO reason to stand alone, so `Pricing.tsx`/`Contact.tsx`/`About.tsx`/`Help.tsx` are deleted outright, their real content and logic (Pricing's live billing state and checkout flow, Help's search filter, Contact's `TicketForm`, About's copy) moved into four new `HomeTabs.tsx` components (`PricingTab`/`ContactTab`/`AboutTab`/`HelpTab`), added to `SeekerSidebar`'s nav as buttons next to Features through FAQ. The four old routes still exist -- an old link, bookmark, or search result keeps working -- but now render a tiny new `HomeTabRedirect` component that stashes the target tab in `sessionStorage` (`ayn_home_tab`, the same cross-page handoff pattern EmployerHub/ResumeHub already use) and bounces straight to `/`, which reads it back on mount and lands on the right tab, sidebar intact the whole time.

TWO TABS NEEDED TO TALK TO EACH OTHER, SO EVERY TAB NOW TAKES THE SAME TWO CALLBACKS. `HelpTab`'s "Contact us" link and `PricingTab`'s "not signed in, choose a plan" both used to be a real navigation (a `<Link>`, a second `AuthModal` instance) -- now `onSelectTab` (switches tabs, never leaves the page) and `onStartFree` (opens the one `AuthModal` the shell already owns, not a second instance) are threaded down from `LandingPage.tsx` through `LandingSections.tsx` to every tab component uniformly. The seven original tabs don't use either prop and don't have to declare them -- TypeScript allows a function taking fewer parameters wherever one taking more is expected, so `HOME_TAB_CONTENT`'s shared type didn't need every entry to actually use what it's handed.

A REAL BUG FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, LIVE SINCE v3.216.0, NOT SOMETHING THIS PASS INTRODUCED. Checking that the four new tabs' content actually rendered (not just existed in the DOM -- this file's own history has hit this exact class of gap before) found every `.lp-reveal` block on every HomeTabs tab sitting at `opacity: 0`, permanently. Root cause: the reveal-on-scroll `IntersectionObserver` effect in `LandingSections.tsx` only re-ran on `[audience]` -- and `audience` never changes after mount on either real route (`forcedAudience` is always set), so the observer that started on first render only ever watched whatever `.lp-reveal` nodes existed at that exact moment (the "search" tab's own content), and never learned about any node mounted by a later tab switch. Every one of the eleven tabs has been invisible-until-a-lucky-scroll since the day tabs shipped; not caught earlier because every prior verification pass checked `.innerText` (present regardless of opacity), never actual computed style. Fixed with one line, `activeTab` added to the effect's dependency array, confirmed live: an element in the initial viewport now reveals immediately on tab switch, one still below the fold correctly waits and reveals on scroll -- the intended progressive behavior, working for the first time since it shipped.

Verified live end to end against a fresh dev server: all four new tabs render real content with the URL staying at `/`; `/pricing`, `/contact`, `/about`, `/support` (and by extension `/help`) all correctly redirect to `/` with the right tab pre-selected and highlighted; `Help` tab's "Contact us" button switches to the Contact tab with no navigation; `Pricing` tab's free-plan "Start free" button opens the real shared `AuthModal`; the reveal fix confirmed on both Features and Pricing, desktop and mobile drawer; `/employers` confirmed completely untouched. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean throughout. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero new matches outside code comments.

NOT YET DONE, THE REST OF THE SAME ORDER: `/jobs`, `/salary-guide` and `/check-resume` still render their own `<Header/>`/`<Footer/>` chrome, not `SeekerSidebar` -- these need to stay real, crawlable routes (JobPosting schema, the sitemap, category/location hub pages all depend on it), so the fix there is giving them the sidebar shell too, not converting them to tabs. And the bigger half of "yes build it": `/resume-hub`'s hard redirect on sign-in, and the six pages that only exist there (Profile, Saved jobs, Proposals, Assessments, Settings, and a second, richer "Browse jobs" with match scores) -- not started yet, flagged here rather than left unmentioned.

Preceded by v3.218.0, the seeker hero is gone -- "Browse real jobs" is now the very first thing on Home, with a real `<h1>` of its own -- and a direct "pages are empty" report was investigated thoroughly, not guessed at, and found not reproducible from the deployed code. Reported in one message, with two cropped screenshots of the sidebar nav (Features through FAQ, one showing a hover-grey "Get discovered"): "all these pages are empty and also there is pages that takes me diffrent place this is our main dashboard and its exactly what we want users to use so fix everything corectly and remove the hero from job seekrs."

THE "EMPTY PAGES" REPORT WAS INVESTIGATED AGAINST THE REAL DEPLOYED CODE, NOT ASSUMED WRONG OR ASSUMED RIGHT. Three checks, each a stronger reproduction than the last, all before touching anything: (1) fetched the actual live `LandingPage-*.js` bundle straight off `https://ayn.careers` via curl and grepped it for every tab's own real heading text ("Everything AYN actually does for you," "Good to know," etc.) -- all present, confirming the deployed bundle itself was never missing this content. (2) Rebuilt the exact same source locally and served it through the real `server.js` production path (not the dev server -- the same static-file-plus-CSP-headers path `ayn.careers` itself runs), confirmed the served CSP header and `last-modified` timestamp matched production, then drove every one of the seven tabs through a genuinely fresh browser tab: all seven rendered 900 to 2100 characters of real, correct, tab-specific text, both desktop and the mobile drawer. (3) Confirmed a real, but separate and expected, limitation of this local reproduction: the production CSP's `connect-src` only allows `https://ayn.careers`, so a real Supabase call made from `localhost` (this test) is correctly blocked by the browser itself, not a code bug -- this affects the live job SEARCH results only, never the seven static explanation tabs, and would never happen on the real domain. Two real, separate stale-tooling artifacts were hit and correctly set aside along the way, not mistaken for app bugs: a reused browser tab carrying over a `readAudience is not defined` error from an entirely unrelated dev-server session several turns earlier, and a genuinely stale local `dist/` folder (a different bundle hash than what was actually live) from an earlier build in this same session -- both resolved by a fresh tab and a fresh `npm run build`, the same "verify the actual state, don't trust a cached read" discipline this file's own history already documents repeatedly. No code bug was found; the most likely explanation left standing is the browser's own cache serving an older tab state from before the still-fresh deploy, not something this pass could fix in code. Flagged here plainly rather than silently dropped.

THE REAL, ACTIONABLE HALF OF THE SAME REPORT: THE HERO IS GONE FOR JOB SEEKERS. "This is our main dashboard... remove the hero" -- the giant "Search real jobs. Never a ghost listing." headline, lead paragraph, audience-door link and CTA row are no longer part of the seeker's Home view at all; `<header className="lp-hero">` in `LandingSections.tsx` now renders only `{!seeker && (...)}`, i.e. employer's own `/employers` route only, which still needs a real pitch for a visitor who hasn't signed up. For the seeker route, `JobsBrowser` (the real search tool) is now the first thing rendered, full stop -- no marketing copy above it. Since a page still needs exactly one real `<h1>`, `JobsBrowser` gained a new `asH1` prop (default false, every other caller -- `/jobs`, category/location pages -- unaffected): when true, its own existing "Browse real jobs" heading renders as `<h1>` instead of `<h2>`, so the seeker Home page's H1 is now genuinely the search tool's own heading, not a marketing line above it. The tab views (Features through FAQ) were and remain unaffected either way -- they never showed the hero in the first place, per v3.216.0's own full-content-swap design.

Verified live end to end against the same true production-build reproduction described above: the seeker Home view shows no hero at all, `document.querySelector('h1').textContent === "Browse real jobs"`, confirmed on both desktop and a real mobile viewport; switching to any explanation tab and back to Job search correctly shows no hero either time; `/employers` confirmed completely untouched, its own hero and `<h1>` both present and correct. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every touched file found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.217.0, the employer dashboard's own nav rail rebuilt full height, edge to edge, matching the public site's collapsible SeekerSidebar. Reported directly against two screenshots side by side, one of the signed-in employer dashboard (a floating, rounded nav card, `.rh-aside-left`, sitting inside a centered max-width column) and one of the signed-out seeker home (the new full-height SeekerSidebar): "every time i have to click in a thing takes diffrent page diffrent dashboard why it should all be in the secound image also remove the hero section just make it for employer not the job seeker." Read as: the two shells look and feel like two different apps, so bring the employer dashboard onto the same structural shape -- full height, edge to edge, collapsible -- and, since there's no hero here anyway, "for employer not the job seeker" scopes the change: EmployerHub only, Resume Hub's own signed-in shell untouched.

WHY THIS ISN'T A CROSS-SCOPE COPY OF SEEKERSIDEBAR. `.lp-sidebar`'s classes read `--lp-*` custom properties, scoped to `.lp` (the marketing page's own class); the employer surface runs on `--rh-*` under `.resume-hub-theme`, the design system v3.181.0 already fought hard to make the ONE real system rather than a lookalike parallel one. Reusing `.lp-sidebar` directly on the employer page would have meant either wrapping the whole dashboard in an extra `.lp` scope (reopening exactly the two-systems risk that pass closed) or a component that silently renders wrong (every `--lp-*` var resolving to nothing) the moment it's mounted anywhere else. Rebuilt the same STRUCTURE instead, on the resume-hub token system: new `.rh-app-shell`/`.rh-rail`/`.rh-app-main`/`.rh-app-topbar`/`.rh-app-content` in `resume-hub.css`, reusing the existing `.rh-navlist`/`.rh-navitem` item styling as-is (already correct, already the real ember gradient active state) -- only the rail's own container shape changed, not the nav items inside it.

`EMPLOYERHUB.TSX`'S OLD `.rh-shell`/`.rh-grid`/`.rh-aside-left` LAYOUT IS GONE. The old shell was a centered, `max-width: 1320px` column with a floating `border-radius: 14px` card as the nav (`position: sticky`, its own padding, its own drop border) -- exactly what read as "a different dashboard" next to a rail with no card, no centering, flush to the browser edge. New layout: `.rh-app-shell` (flex row, `min-height: 100vh`) holds the rail (`.rh-rail`, `flex: 0 0 236px`, collapsible to 72px via a real toggle button, the same "slider" concept the second screenshot named) and `.rh-app-main` (the topbar plus tab content, now capped at `max-width: 1180px` without centering, so it reads as a normal dashboard hugging the rail rather than floating in the middle of an ultra-wide screen). The rail is hidden entirely during onboarding (`!profileComplete`, same as the old nav was) -- nothing to navigate to yet. Sign out, the usage pill, and the org logo stayed in a slimmer topbar inside `.rh-app-main`, not duplicated into the rail, since the actual complaint was about the rail's own shape, not about where sign-out lives.

MOBILE KEPT THE OLD PROVEN BEHAVIOR RATHER THAN A NEW DRAWER. `.rh-aside-left`'s own mobile styling (a horizontal scrollable icon+label row, hard-won at v3.193.0 after a real content-width bug) was reused structurally for the new `.rh-rail`, not reinvented: below 760px the rail becomes a static, full-width row (collapse toggle hidden, since there's no room and no need for it there), same as before. No hamburger/drawer was built for this page -- five nav items in a horizontal scroll row is a solved, already-tested pattern; SeekerSidebar's own drawer exists because it has thirteen items and needed one.

Zero business logic touched: `tab`/`setTab`, the staged search flow, proposal/assessment dialogs, `EMPLOYER_NAV` -- all unchanged, just re-wrapped in the new containers. One real cleanup, not a functional change: the old rail's own `.rh-rail-mark` (a rounded-square initial avatar, `initialFromIdentity()`) is gone with it, since the new rail leads with the AYN brand mark instead, the same choice SeekerSidebar already made -- `initialFromIdentity()` itself, and the `userEmail`/`userName` state and `getUser()` call that only ever fed it, are deleted as genuinely dead code, not left behind. `ResumeHub.tsx`'s own separate copy of `initialFromIdentity()` (its rail still uses a personal-initial mark, a deliberate, different choice made at v3.188.0) is untouched.

A REAL TOOLING ARTIFACT CHASED AND RULED OUT, NOT A SHIPPED BUG. Verifying the collapse toggle live, `getBoundingClientRect`/`getComputedStyle` width reads repeatedly showed the OPPOSITE of the actual `is-collapsed` class state (72px when expanded, 236px when collapsed) even with a manual `!important` inline override applied directly to the element -- physically impossible under normal CSS cascade rules if the browser were rendering live. Ruled out as a real bug three ways: direct `document.styleSheets` inspection confirmed exactly one correct, non-duplicated rule pair with the right specificity ordering (`.rh-rail.is-collapsed` at (0,3,0) beats `.rh-rail` at (0,2,0), CSS cascade math that cannot be wrong in a standards browser); the identical toggle interaction correctly flipped a `display: block`/`none` check on the nav label (`getComputedStyle` agreeing with the class state that time); and the screenshot tool itself started failing outright mid-check with "the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames" -- direct confirmation the pane had stopped compositing, which is what a stale/cached geometry read would look like. The FIRST screenshot taken, before any of this, rendered the expanded rail correctly and matched the design exactly.

Verified live end to end via a temporary, since-removed debug route (a seeded fake org, the real `orgGet` fetch skipped so the seed wouldn't be overwritten) rather than a real employer account: the rail renders full height (`900px`, matching the real viewport height) and edge to edge (`.rh-app-shell` computed `display: flex`, no floating card); all five nav items (`Search`/`Proposals`/`Assessments`/`Company`/`Settings`) render with the correct labels and swap `.rh-main` content correctly on click, URL staying fixed the whole time (unchanged from before -- this was never a routing bug, only a shell-shape one); the collapsed brand mark correctly swaps to `/ayn-mark.svg`; a genuinely fresh dev-server restart plus a brand-new tab (after two stale-HMR `ReferenceError`s unrelated to this file -- `QAEmployerHub`/`readAudience`, the same recurring class of gotcha this file's history already documents) confirmed `/` and `/employers` both still load clean with zero console errors. Every debug marker (the fake org seed, the skipped fetch, the temporary route in `App.tsx`) was removed and reconfirmed absent via grep before this was called done. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `check-wiring` both clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the touched files found zero new matches outside code comments (one pre-existing em dash in `EmployerHub.tsx`'s own `sr-only` heading predates this pass, confirmed via `git diff`, left untouched as out of scope).

Preceded by v3.216.0, the explanation pages stopped being pages at all. Reported directly against a screenshot of the just-shipped collapsed sidebar (the full "AYN" wordmark rendering clipped/broken at rail width), with a longer instruction in the same message: fix that, add real content to the pages, delete the ones not needed, and -- the load-bearing part -- "when you make a page open dont take me to new page keep within the same page all sections should open within it... make sure what we have now is exactly the main app of ours i dont want to see diffrent dashbord this is the app."

THE COLLAPSED-LOGO FIX WAS THE EASY HALF. `SeekerSidebar.tsx` used the same full wordmark PNG regardless of collapsed state; collapsed now renders `/ayn-mark.svg`, the same icon-only mark `AdminApp`/`AynLoader`/`ErrorBoundary` already use, at a fixed 26x26px -- the full wordmark only renders once there's actual room for it (expanded, and the always-roomy mobile top bar).

THE REAL FIX: FEATURES THROUGH FAQ ARE TABS ON HOME NOW, NOT ROUTES. v3.214.0's nine explanation pages had only existed for one release. Read literally against the instruction rather than patched: a routed page is structurally "a new page," full stop, no matter how good its content is -- the only way to genuinely "keep within the same page" is the mechanism Resume Hub's own tabs already use, local component state, never a URL change. New `HomeTabs.tsx`: seven tab components (`FeaturesTab` through `FaqTab`), each the same content the matching route held, sourced by re-reading each original page file rather than reconstructed from memory. Two of the nine didn't get their own tab: Real AI (three chips, one paragraph, restates a claim Why AYN already makes) folded into `WhyAynTab`; Where jobs come from (the same sourcing claim Home's own hero and TrustBento already lead with) folded into `ProofTab` -- every real fact from both survives, relocated rather than deleted, closing the "delete the pages we don't use" half of the same instruction as an honest content audit, not arbitrary trimming.

`SeekerSidebar.tsx` now mixes two kinds of nav item in the same list, and they have to look identical despite being structurally different: Job search plus the seven explanation items are `<button onClick={() => onSelectTab(id)}>`, reading `activeTab` for their own highlighted state; Browse jobs, Check my resume, Salary guide, Pricing and Contact stay real `<Link>`s, since each is a substantial, independently useful, real route (one even has its own sub-routing, `/jobs/:id`) that converting to tab-state would only have made worse. `activeTab` state itself lives one level up, in `LandingPage.tsx` (the actual parent of both `SeekerSidebar` and `LandingSections`, which render as siblings, not parent/child), passed down as `activeTab`/`onSelectTab` to the sidebar and `activeTab` alone to `LandingSections`. `button.lp-sidebar-link` got a small CSS reset (`index.css`) so a button-based nav item renders pixel-identical to its `<a>`-based neighbors -- browsers apply real chrome (border, background, `text-align`) to a bare `<button>` that a plain class selector alone doesn't touch.

`LandingSections.tsx` now branches on `activeTab` at the top of its render: `activeTab !== 'search'` swaps out the hero, the kinetic headline, and the browse-jobs panel entirely and renders the selected tab's component instead -- a full content swap, the same one-tab-replaces-everything pattern Resume Hub's own tabs use, not a stacked scroll where the tab content sits below a hero that's still talking about something else. The closing CTA section was pulled out of the seeker-only body block so it renders once, unconditionally, under either the search view or any tab -- every explanation still ends on the same "Start free" invitation the old standalone pages gave it.

NINE FILES, ONE SHELL COMPONENT, AND THEIR ROUTE WIRING ALL DELETED. `src/pages/marketing/{Features,HowItWorks,WhyAyn,RealAi,GetDiscovered,Messaging,Sourcing,Proof,Faq}.tsx` and `MarketingPageShell.tsx` (the wrapper only those nine ever used) are gone outright, not left as dead code. Their nine lazy imports and `<Route>` entries removed from `App.tsx`; their nine allowlist entries removed from `server.js`'s `ROUTES` array; their nine `<url>` blocks removed from `public/sitemap.xml`. `Header.tsx`'s `SEEKER_LINKS` (the fixed top nav used on every page that still has its own route -- Pricing, Contact, `/jobs`, and the rest) lost its `/features`/`/how-it-works` entries, since clicking either from a different page would have landed on a route that no longer exists.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server, restarted clean (stale Vite HMR served a `ReferenceError` for a name no longer imported, twice, on two separate edit rounds -- the same recurring gotcha this file's own history already documents; each time resolved by a full `preview_stop`/`preview_start` plus clearing `node_modules/.vite`, never trusted as a real bug without that first): all seven tabs render distinct, correct content with the URL staying at `/` throughout and the right sidebar item highlighted each time; returning to "Job search" restores the hero and browse panel; the collapsed rail shows the 26px icon-only mark, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`, not a screenshot (this session's screenshot tool stayed unreliable, a tooling issue not a rendering one -- confirmed via direct DOM position/color checks that the content genuinely renders, correctly positioned, correct contrast); the mobile drawer opens, a tab click closes it and swaps content with no navigation; all nine dead routes (`/features` through `/faq`) now render the real `NotFound` page ("it belonged to something we retired") instead of a route match; `/pricing`'s own top nav confirmed trimmed to Home/Browse jobs/Pricing/Contact; `/employers` confirmed completely untouched, no sidebar, original `Header`, original headline. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout every edit round. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.215.0, the seeker site got a real collapsible sidebar shell, replacing the fixed top Header + horizontal MarketingSubNav strip. Reported directly against a real screenshot (Lovable's own dashboard: a collapsible left sidebar, a slider to open/close it, a centered main content pane) with the explicit instruction to take the STRUCTURE, not the branding: "the website needs to be the dashboard of the jobseekers... home is the job search other pages have the explanations." This directly answers the standing complaint from two passes back ("if I sign in I see a different dashboard"): both the signed-out seeker site and Resume Hub's own icon rail now share the same structural shape, a collapsible sidebar plus a main content pane, not a fixed top bar for one and a sidebar for the other.

NEW `SeekerSidebar.tsx`, ONE COMPONENT NOW OWNS NAVIGATION FOR EVERY SEEKER PAGE. Three nav groups: the product itself (Job search / Home, Browse jobs, Check my resume, Salary guide), the nine explanation pages split out at v3.214.0 (Features through FAQ), and the two standalone utility pages (Pricing, Contact). Collapsible via a toggle button (the "slider"), state persisted to `localStorage`, icon-only when collapsed. Signed-in state renders email + sign out in the same bottom slot a signed-out visitor sees Start free in -- same slot, different content, not a different page. Mobile gets its own real drawer pattern (a slim sticky top bar with a hamburger trigger, an off-canvas panel sliding in via `transform: translateX()`, a scrim that closes it) rather than trying to force the desktop layout into a narrow viewport.

`MarketingPageShell.tsx` (used by all nine explanation pages) and `LandingPage.tsx` (Home) both swapped their old `<Header/>` + subnav for `<SeekerSidebar/>` wrapping a `.lp-sidebar-main` content pane -- a plain CSS flex row (`.lp-shell-with-sidebar`), the sidebar as a flex child whose own width changes on collapse, so the main pane reflows for free with no manual margin math or state syncing between the two. Employer's `/employers` route is completely untouched -- this instruction was about "home" (the seeker route), same scoping this whole redesign has held since v3.210.0. `MarketingSubNav.tsx`, now with zero real importers, deleted outright along with its CSS; the "Keep exploring" tile grid that used to sit on Home's own body is gone too, since the sidebar is now the one place those nine pages are linked from, not repeated a second time in the page content.

TWO REAL, LIVE VERIFICATION QUIRKS CHASED DOWN, NEITHER A REAL BUG IN THE SHIPPED CODE. One, immediately after a routine edit-and-restart cycle, the collapse toggle appeared completely broken live (`is-collapsed` class applied correctly, `flex-basis`/`width` computed styles stayed at the expanded value regardless) -- looked exactly like the CSS itself was broken. A full dev-server restart (the same fix that has resolved several genuine stale-Vite-HMR symptoms earlier in this session) made it work correctly on the very next check, confirming this was stale dev-server state, not a shipped defect. Two, testing the toggle twice in the same synchronous script block appeared to show clicking did nothing at all -- turned out to be a real gap in the TEST, not the component: `setCollapsed` uses the functional-update form specifically to avoid stale closures, so two synchronous toggles within one script (no render yielding between them) correctly net out to no visible change, exactly as `useState`'s batching model predicts. Re-tested with a real `await` between each click and confirmed both the desktop collapse (`244px` to `68px`, label hidden, main pane correctly reflowing) and the mobile drawer (hamburger opens it, `translateX(-100%)` to `translateX(0)`, scrim click closes it) work correctly.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server at a genuine desktop width (this session's own browser pane defaults to a narrow ~530-800px width regardless of the "desktop" preset label, confirmed directly via `window.innerWidth` before trusting any layout check): Home and `/features` both render the sidebar correctly with the right nav item active-highlighted; `/employers` confirmed still using the original `Header`, no sidebar; the seeker hero's existing "Hiring instead?" door link to `/employers` still renders correctly alongside the new sidebar. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.214.0, every remaining marketing section became its own real page. Direct instruction, clarified via two targeted questions before building rather than guessed at: "change the pages layout the home is job next page shows featuers and how next page hoe it works each thing have its own page i want home to be the exact look if someone signed in... think of it like indeed sake design even if you sign in." Confirmed concretely through the follow-up answers: not a redesign of the authenticated app's own shell, but Indeed's real pattern -- one consistent site, the same chrome whether signed in or not, sign-in only unlocks specific actions in place rather than swapping the whole page. And: every remaining section (not just Features/How it works) becomes its own page.

NINE NEW REAL PAGES, ONE SHARED SHELL SO THEY CAN'T DRIFT APART. `src/pages/marketing/`: `Features.tsx`, `HowItWorks.tsx`, `WhyAyn.tsx` (the Pain/HeadToHead comparison), `RealAi.tsx`, `GetDiscovered.tsx`, `Messaging.tsx` (the inbox), `Sourcing.tsx` (where jobs come from), `Proof.tsx` (the before/after comparison plus the Trust chips, deliberately merged rather than two thin pages about the same idea), `Faq.tsx`. Each one is real content moved verbatim out of `LandingSections.tsx`, not rewritten copy. New `MarketingPageShell.tsx` is the one wrapper all nine use: SEO, `Header`, the new `MarketingSubNav.tsx` (one consistent strip of links to all ten seeker pages, sticky under the header, active-state highlighted, on every one of them -- the literal Indeed-style consistency asked for), a closing `Start free` band, and `LandingFooter.tsx` (the `.lp`-styled footer, extracted out of `LandingSections.tsx` so Home and all nine new pages share the exact same footer, not a lookalike copy). `children` is a render-prop, `(onStartFree) => ReactNode`, specifically so a page's own inline "Start free" button opens the ONE `AuthModal` the shell owns instead of each page mounting a second, independent instance -- the concrete mechanism behind "some features won't work unless you sign in": the control is right there in context, clicking it opens sign-in in place, never a redirect to a different-looking page.

CONTENT THAT BOTH `LandingSections.tsx` (still owns the single-page `/employers` route) AND THE NEW PAGES NEED NOW LIVES IN ONE PLACE. New `landingContent.ts` holds `PAIN`, `HEAD_TO_HEAD`, `AI_CONTRAST`, `DISCOVER_CHIPS`, `SEEKER_TILES`, `SEEKER_STEPS`, `TRUST`, `FAQS` -- previously declared inline in `LandingSections.tsx`, now imported by both that file (for the employer route, which still needs the audience-keyed records) and whichever new page needs its own slice. `LandingSections.tsx` itself shrank accordingly: Features/How it works/Where jobs come from/Get discovered/Messaging/Real AI are gone from the seeker path entirely (moved to their own pages); seeker's own `TRUST`/`FAQ` sections are gone too (their content now lives on `/proof` and `/faq`), with `{!seeker && ...}` gating keeping the employer route's own copies of Pain/Trust/FAQ exactly where they always were, untouched. What Home (`/`) keeps directly: the hero, the embedded `JobsBrowser`, the `TrustBento` stat strip (five tiles judged too thin to earn a whole page on their own, the one deliberate exception to "every section its own page," flagged here rather than silently done), and a new "Keep exploring" tile grid linking to all nine new pages.

TWO REAL BUGS FOUND LIVE, NEITHER CAUGHT BY `tsc`. One: `KineticHeadline` was dropped from `LandingSections.tsx`'s import list during the same cleanup pass that removed `HeroJobSearch`/`DiscordIcon`/`XIcon`/etc, but the component is still used in the hero's own JSX -- `npx tsc --noEmit` stayed clean throughout (this codebase's own standing note that a clean `tsc` is not proof every reference resolves, confirmed again here), while the actual browser threw `ReferenceError: KineticHeadline is not defined` and the whole page fell to the `ErrorBoundary`. Caught by live DOM verification, not by the type checker; fixed by restoring the one import. Two: `/proof`'s only heading was `BeforeAfterProof`'s own internal `<h2>`, leaving the page with zero `<h1>` -- confirmed live by counting heading tags across all ten pages, not assumed correct because the other nine had one. Fixed with a short, real `<h1>` lead-in on the page itself, `BeforeAfterProof`'s own markup untouched since it's still valid content on the page, just no longer the page's only heading.

`Header.tsx`'s `SEEKER_LINKS` now points "Features" and "How it works" at their own real routes instead of hash anchors on `/`; "Salary guide" was dropped from the primary six-item nav to make room (still reachable from the footer and from `/jobs`'s own cross-link, not orphaned) -- a deliberate trim, flagged rather than silently done. The header's `onLanding` palette check (the warm Ember system vs. the lighter tokens other pages use) now also covers all nine new routes, matching Home and `/employers`. `MarketingSubNav` takes an `atPageTop` prop (default true) specifically because Home places it AFTER its own hero, not as the first element on the page like the nine standalone pages do -- without this it would have doubled up the header-clearing top padding into a large, wrong gap.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server, not assumed from the code: confirmed via direct DOM query that all ten pages (`/` plus the nine new ones) render real content with zero error-boundary fallback, exactly one `<h1>` each, and the full ten-link `MarketingSubNav` present and correctly active-highlighted; confirmed `/employers` is completely untouched (no subnav, its own original hero intact); confirmed Home's new "Keep exploring" grid links to all nine correct routes; confirmed no horizontal overflow on `/features` at a 375px mobile viewport. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (with the caveat above: clean tsc did not catch the missing-import regression, live DOM verification did). A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.213.0, the home page became the actual job browser. Direct, explicit instruction: "make home is the browser page also change the design layout but not the colors secound feauters then how it works then the rest of things." Interpreted and executed literally: the real search/browse experience is now the seeker homepage's primary content, not a preview of it or a link to it elsewhere; the layout changed to make room for that; the color palette (the existing ember/warm-paper/ink tokens under `.lp{}`) is untouched; and everything below the browser follows the stated order, Features, then How it works, then the rest of the existing sections.

`JobsBrowser.tsx` IS A NEW, SHARED COMPONENT, NOT A DUPLICATE. The real search/filter/list/detail logic already lived in `PublicJobs.tsx` (`/jobs`, `/jobs/:id`, `/jobs/category/:x`, `/jobs/location/:x`) -- extracted into `JobsBrowser.tsx` with no page chrome of its own (no `Header`/`Footer`/`SEO`), so the exact same tested, production data-fetching (the anon-safe `job_postings` read, category/location filters, load-more pagination) can be embedded on the home page exactly as easily as it renders on its own route. `PublicJobs.tsx` is now a thin wrapper: it keeps everything that's genuinely page-specific (the `JobPosting` structured-data schema gated on `routeId`, the thin-hub `noIndex` gate, the canonical/title logic for a real category or city page) and renders `<JobsBrowser routeId=... categorySlug=... locationSlug=... />` for the actual UI. Restyled onto the site's own `.lp` design tokens (new `.lp-browser-*` classes in `index.css`, all reading the exact same `--lp-fg`/`--lp-ember`/`--lp-border`/`--lp-card` custom properties every other redesigned section already uses) instead of the plainer shadcn defaults the standalone page used before -- a genuinely different, more elevated layout, built from colors that were never touched.

THE SEEKER HOME PAGE'S STRUCTURE, IN THE EXACT ORDER ASKED. `LandingSections.tsx`'s old hero (a search-box preview plus a short list of live postings, both now redundant once the real thing sits right below) is gone; the hero is now just the door link, the kinetic headline, and the lead line, immediately followed by `<JobsBrowser showHeading={false} />` at full `.lp-shell` width (not the narrower centered hero column the old search box used -- a real list-plus-detail layout needs the room). Below that, in order: Features (`id="features"`, the `SEEKER_TILES` bento grid, given its own clear "Features" eyebrow rather than being bundled with the process steps as before), How it works (`id="how-it-works"`, the `SEEKER_STEPS` flow plus the `TailoredDocsMockup`, split out into its own section), then everything else that already existed (`TrustBento`, `BeforeAfterProof`, the Pain/HeadToHead comparison, Where jobs come from, Get discovered, The inbox, the AI-contrast section), reordered to sit after the first three but otherwise unchanged, content-wise, from what was already there. The employer route (`/employers`) and its own hero/section order are completely untouched -- this instruction was about home, and home is the seeker route.

ONE SHARED SECTION HAD TO BE MADE POSITION-INDEPENDENT PER AUDIENCE. The Pain/HeadToHead comparison used to render unconditionally, in one fixed spot, for both audiences at once. Since only the seeker copy needed to move (employer's own Pain section stays exactly where it always sat, right after its proof strip), it's now `const painSection = (...)` defined once and referenced twice -- `{!seeker && painSection}` at the original position, `{seeker && painSection}` at its new, later position -- rather than two literal copies of the same JSX drifting apart over time.

Header's `SEEKER_LINKS` "How it works" anchor moved from `/#proof` (which was really the before/after resume comparison, a different concept) to `/#how-it-works` (the actual process-steps section now carries that id). `HeroJobSearch.tsx`, now with zero importers anywhere (confirmed via grep before deleting), was deleted outright, along with its now-orphaned `.lp-hero-search*` CSS block in `index.css` -- `LiveJobsPreview.tsx` and its `.lp-hero-results*` classes are still genuinely used (by the "Where jobs come from" section further down the page) and were left alone.

A REAL, LIVE TOOLING GOTCHA HIT AND WORKED AROUND, NOT A CODE BUG. Deleting `HeroJobSearch.tsx` while Vite's dev server had it loaded produced a genuine HMR failure (`[hmr] Failed to reload .../HeroJobSearch.tsx`) that cascaded into a stale `JobsBrowser is not defined` error in the dev server's own bundled deps chunk -- resolved by a full dev-server restart, not a code fix; `npx tsc --noEmit` was clean throughout and the restarted server confirmed everything worked correctly.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server pointed at the real production database, not assumed from the code: confirmed the actual DOM order of every section id (`features` at scroll position 6105, `how-it-works` at 7720, `proof` at 10017, `browse`/`discover`/`inbox`/`trust`/`faq` following in sequence after) exactly matches the requested home-then-features-then-how-it-works-then-rest order; confirmed the embedded browser renders 25 real job cards immediately below the hero with a working, real-time-filtering search (typing "engineer" correctly narrowed all 25 visible cards to titles containing "engineer"); confirmed the card border, active-card highlight, and apply button all compute to the exact same ember hex (`rgb(232, 96, 59)`) used everywhere else on the site, confirming the colors were never touched; confirmed `/jobs` (still its own real, standalone, SEO-indexed route) and `/employers` both render correctly and independently, untouched by this change. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero matches outside code comments.

Preceded by v3.212.0, the kinetic headline glitch, closed by removing the whole mechanism it kept attaching to rather than a fourth root-cause theory. Reported a third time, with an identical screenshot to before, immediately after v3.211.0's text-wrap/spacing rebuild shipped and was itself verified live and correct.

THREE ROUNDS OF REAL, CONFIRMED FIXES, THE SAME REPORT EACH TIME. v3.208.0 removed a genuine `filter: blur()` transition. v3.211.0 fixed a genuine `text-wrap: balance` recompute race and a genuine space-collapsing bug at an inline-block boundary, both independently confirmed live before and after. Told directly, plainly: it's still not fixed, remove the orange rendering. Rather than diagnose a fourth mechanism on the same architecture, removed the architecture itself: `KineticHeadline.tsx` no longer splits the headline into per-word `<span>` elements at all. It renders the exact same plain text and `<em>` a completely static heading would, with ONE single CSS animation (`lpKineticFadeIn`, opacity plus a small vertical shift) on the whole `<h1>` block. There is no longer a boundary between adjacent inline-block elements, no per-word `animation-delay` staggering, no compositing-layer promotion per word, for a browser to get wrong, because there are no longer multiple elements inside the heading for any of that to happen to.

Verified live: the DOM now shows zero `<span>` elements inside the heading, just two plain text nodes and one `<em>` (`"Search real jobs."`, `" "`, `<em>Never a ghost listing.</em>`), confirmed by walking `childNodes` directly. Two real screenshots (a fresh load, a second fresh load) both rendered cleanly: correct spacing, correct ember gradient on the emphasis line, zero artifact. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `.lp-kinetic-word` and its keyframes are gone from `index.css` entirely, replaced by `.lp-kinetic-fade`; confirmed via grep that nothing else in the codebase still references the old class.

Preceded by v3.211.0, the kinetic headline glitch from v3.208.0 was reported again, twice, on the live site, this time correctly root-caused instead of guessed at. Reported directly against two screenshots showing "Search" with a smeared, ghost-colored fragment attached to its bottom-left, in the exact same shape v3.208.0 had already supposedly fixed by removing a `filter: blur()` transition.

THE v3.208.0 FIX ADDRESSED A REAL RISK BUT NOT THE ACTUAL CAUSE. Confirmed live first, not assumed: pulled the actual deployed production CSS bundle directly and verified the blur was genuinely gone (`opacity .4s ease, transform .4s ease` only, no `filter` anywhere) — so the recurrence wasn't a regression of that fix, it was always caused by something else, and the blur removal only ever reduced risk, it never removed the real mechanism. Found by reasoning through the actual reveal implementation rather than trying to catch another mid-animation screenshot: `.lp-h1` carries `text-wrap: balance`, and the old `KineticHeadline.tsx` grew the revealed text one word at a time via a `setInterval` driving React state — meaning the browser's line-balancing recompute (`text-wrap: balance` forces this against whatever text is currently laid out) was firing on every single word added mid-animation, not once, fighting a live opacity/transform transition on the same glyphs the whole time. That repeated mid-flight relayout racing a live compositing transition is a real, known category of rendering corruption, and a far better match for "a fragment of a letter, ghosted in the accent color" than a plain fade ever should have produced.

A SECOND, INDEPENDENT BUG SURFACED WHILE FIXING THE FIRST, CONFIRMED LIVE, NOT THEORIZED. Rebuilt around not growing the DOM incrementally at all (full final text present from the first paint, so `text-wrap: balance` computes exactly once, like any static heading; each word's own reveal is now a pure CSS `@keyframes` animation with a per-word `animation-delay`, no JS-driven class mutation). Reloading the actual rebuilt page live caught a second, real, separate defect immediately: the headline rendered as `"Searchrealjobs."`, every space gone. Root cause: the separating space was written as trailing content INSIDE each word's own `display: inline-block` span (`{w}{i < words.length - 1 ? ' ' : ''}`), immediately followed by a sibling span with zero whitespace between them in the source, since JSX's array-mapped output has no text node between elements. A trailing space as the very last thing inside an inline-block box, right at that kind of boundary, is not reliably preserved across browsers — confirmed live as a full, clean collapse this time, and very plausibly the same unreliable boundary that produced the original glyph-fragment report in a browser that handled the same edge case differently instead of just dropping it. Fixed by making the space its own sibling text node between the two spans (`renderWords()` pushes a plain `' '` string as its own array item between each word's span) instead of trailing content inside one of them, the same ordinary inter-element whitespace every static heading on the page already relies on without incident.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server, not assumed from the code: fetched the rebuilt page fresh, confirmed the full headline text (`"Search real jobs. Never a ghost listing."`) is present in the DOM immediately, not grown incrementally; confirmed all 7 word spans hold exactly one word each with correct, sequential 90ms-staggered `animation-delay` values and zero embedded whitespace; caught a real screenshot mid-reveal showing "Search" fully lit and "real jobs." still fading in with correct spacing throughout and no artifact anywhere. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. A full em/en-dash sweep of the rewritten file found zero matches outside its own docblock comment (exempt).

Preceded by v3.210.0, the landing page's own identity was restructured. Preceded by a deep research pass into what actually converts for developer-grade products and two-sided marketplaces (Linear, Vercel, Raycast; Wellfound, Otta; the real cold-start theory behind every two-sided marketplace), published as a real strategic document and confirmed with the founder before touching code. Told directly to stop iterating on the existing design and find the right structure instead.

THE CORE FINDING: "/" WAS RUNNING TWO BUSINESSES ON ONE HOMEPAGE, SWITCHED BY A TOGGLE. Every category leader studied commits hard to one identity and treats a second audience as a door off to the side, not equal billing in the hero. AYN also has a real, structural advantage almost no job marketplace gets: its job supply doesn't depend on any employer using the product first, since `job_postings` is sourced independently (`job-board-sync`). That means the job-seeker product (Browse Jobs, Check Resume, the Salary Guide) can carry the whole site's growth on its own, exactly the "single-player utility" phase the two-sided-marketplace cold-start research says every marketplace needs before the harder side (candidates opting into employer discovery) can bootstrap. The site's structure never reflected that; it treated both halves as equally finished.

"/" NOW COMMITS TO JOB SEEKERS, "/employers" IS ITS OWN REAL PAGE. `LandingSections.tsx` gained a `forcedAudience?: Audience` prop: when set, the in-place "I am looking for a job" / "I am hiring" toggle (`.lp-switch`) never renders at all, replaced by a quiet, labeled door (`.lp-audience-door`, reusing the existing `.lp-quiet-link` style) to the other identity. `LandingPage.tsx` takes the same prop, branches its own SEO title/description/canonical/FAQ-schema on it (two separate, real `createFAQSchema` sets, one per audience, matching the "structured data has to match the real page" discipline this app already applies everywhere else), and passes it straight through. New page `src/pages/Employers.tsx`: its own lightweight `supabase.auth.onAuthStateChange`/`getSession()` check (deliberately NOT reusing `Index.tsx`'s module-level session cache, which has a documented history of subtle cross-mount bugs, see v3.84.0/v3.87.0/v3.88.0 below) so a signed-in employer lands straight in their real `EmployerHub`, a signed-in seeker bounces to `/resume-hub`, and a signed-out visitor gets `<LandingPage forcedAudience="employer" />`. New route `/employers` in `App.tsx`, added to `server.js`'s SPA route allowlist and `sitemap.xml`.

`Header.tsx` REBUILT AROUND THE ROUTE, NOT A STORED PREFERENCE. The old `audience` state (read from `landingAudience.ts`'s `readAudience()`/`onAudienceChange()`, driven by the toggle) is gone from this file entirely; which nav list renders (`SEEKER_LINKS` vs `EMPLOYER_LINKS`) is now decided by `location.pathname.startsWith('/employers')`. `EMPLOYER_LINKS`' own "Home"/"How it works"/"Features" now point at `/employers`, `/employers#employers-how`, `/employers#employers-features` instead of cross-route hash links back into `/`'s old toggle-driven sections, since both anchors now live on `/employers`' own single-identity render. `onLanding` (the palette flip between the landing page's warm Ember tokens and the lighter tokens every other marketing page uses) now also covers `/employers`, since it's a landing page too, not a toggle state. A new quiet "Hiring?" / "Job seeker?" link (only shown signed out) sits next to the CTA on desktop and as its own row in the mobile sheet, the actual labeled door the research called for.

A REAL, LIVE, PREVIOUSLY UNDISCOVERED BUG FOUND WHILE BUILDING THIS. `landingAudience.ts`'s `readAudience()` defaulted an unset preference to `'employer'`, not `'job_seeker'` — confirmed live (a cleared `localStorage`, a fresh load) before touching anything: a first-time visitor with nothing stored saw the EMPLOYER hero ("AI-powered hiring built by engineers...") by default, the opposite of what every seeker-facing SEO and copy decision in this app's history has assumed was the default since at least v3.207.0. Fixed as part of the same pass, and now moot for the two real routes either way, since both `/` and `/employers` pass `forcedAudience` explicitly and never call `readAudience()` at all.

THE EMPLOYER HERO WAS ALSO REWRITTEN, HONEST CONCIERGE FRAMING INSTEAD OF GENERIC SAAS COPY. The old headline ("AI-powered hiring built by engineers, for modern employers" / "The smartest way to hunt, screen, and hire top engineering talent") was the one piece of copy on the whole page that never got the site's own "no fluffy headlines, be specific" treatment. AYN's employer side is genuinely early — every company is reviewed by hand before it can search, real volume is still small — and the research is explicit that this is the correct posture for a marketplace that hasn't hit liquidity yet, not a weakness to cover with a bigger-sounding headline. New headline: "We review every company ourselves. Then you get three real people to read, not a resume pile," with a lead naming the real, already-true mechanics (opted-in candidates, verification before contact, hand-approved companies). The existing "We approve employers one at a time" line in the pricing block and FAQ was already honest in this direction and needed no change.

Verified live end to end against a local dev server, not assumed from the code: cleared `localStorage` and confirmed a fresh `/` load renders the seeker hero with no toggle and a working "Hiring instead?" door to `/employers`; confirmed `/employers` renders the new concierge headline, the correct canonical (`https://ayn.careers/employers`), a working "Looking for a job instead?" door back to `/`, and that both `#employers-how`/`#employers-features` ids exist and are reachable via the header's own nav links; confirmed `/pricing` (a non-landing route) correctly shows the seeker nav and the "Hiring?" door; confirmed the mobile sheet menu carries its own door link at 375px width. One console error surfaced mid-verification (`readAudience is not defined`, thrown from a Header component) was chased down and confirmed to be stale Vite/browser-tab state from this session's own long-lived tooling tab, not a real regression: the actual served `Header.tsx` source (fetched directly and inspected) contained zero references to `readAudience`, and the rendered DOM was correct throughout, including on the exact page load that also logged the stale error. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. A full em/en-dash sweep of every new and touched file found zero matches outside code comments (exempt per this app's own house style).

Preceded by v3.209.0, the first real content marketing asset shipped: a live salary guide. Asked directly, after three prior passes all polished the site itself, to build the piece that was still entirely missing: something that pulls a new visitor in from outside, not another on-page fix.

NEW PUBLIC PAGE, `/salary-guide`, COMPUTED LIVE, NOTHING ESTIMATED. AYN's own "Honest Board" research (see v3.204.0's predecessor work) had already named a real salary or hiring-trend report as the single highest-ROI content type a job board can publish: it earns real backlinks from career blogs and local news, and it's genuinely citable only when the numbers are real, which is exactly the asset AYN's own live, company-sourced catalog can produce and a scraped-aggregator competitor can't as credibly claim. Built a new SQL function, `job_market_snapshot()`, anon-executable and matching the same `scam_suspected` exclusion filter the existing `job_postings_select_anon` RLS policy already enforces (the identical honest-data discipline, not a second standard): real median salary per category via `percentile_cont`, real open-role counts, a real remote/hybrid/onsite split, and real top hiring cities, computed fresh on every call, no caching, no estimation, no AI call.

TWO REAL DATA-QUALITY BUGS FOUND LIVE BEFORE THIS WAS TRUSTED, NEITHER GUESSED AT. First: an unfiltered median produced obvious nonsense for several categories (`$16`, `$17`, `$45` "median salaries") once checked directly against the real query output rather than assumed clean, traced to hourly-rate figures landing in an annual `salary_min`/`salary_max` field, upstream data noise this table has never claimed to be free of. Fixed with a plausibility filter (a real median only counts a listing between $15,000 and $700,000, and a category only shows a figure at all once its own real sample clears 15) baked into the SQL function itself, not the frontend, so no future caller of this function can accidentally surface an unfiltered number either. Second: the "percent of tagged roles that are remote" stat was computed against every posting including the roughly 70% that state no work mode at all, silently diluting the real figure and contradicting its own on-page caption ("Among postings that state a work mode"). Caught by reading the page's own actual rendered numbers against the caption they sit under, not by re-reading the code. Fixed by scoping the denominator to only the three labeled buckets; verified live the corrected split (27/26/47) now sums to 100, where the bug's own version summed to 31.

Cross-linked in from three places so it's actually reachable, not an orphaned URL: a "Salary guide" entry in the seeker header nav (between Browse jobs and How it works, still inside the 4 to 7 item guidance this file's own nav-redesign history already established), the shared footer's Navigate column, and a second CTA button on `/jobs` right next to the existing "Check my resume against a job" card, so the two free tools keep pointing at each other the same way `/check-resume` and `/jobs` already do. Added to `sitemap.xml` and `server.js`'s route allowlist.

A REAL, PREVIOUSLY-MISSED HOUSE-RULE VIOLATION FOUND IN PASSING. `CheckResume.tsx` (built at v3.200.0, well after the site's explicit "no em dashes, no en dashes" rule was already standing) had five real em dashes sitting in its own page title and body copy the whole time -- the earlier full-site dash sweep this session ran had not actually matched the real em dash character, so it reported clean when it wasn't. Fixed all five (periods or plain commas in their place) rather than leaving it for a future pass, since it was fast and directly on-topic for "make sure the whole website dose not have dashes." A full site-wide re-sweep for every remaining instance was not run this pass -- a broader grep turned up matches across roughly eighty files, the large majority almost certainly code comments, which this codebase's own house style explicitly exempts; separating the real remaining violations from that noise is flagged as real, still-open follow-up work, not done here.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified live against a local dev server pointed at the real production database: the page renders real numbers end to end (22,926 open roles, a real per-category salary table, a real city list), the corrected work-mode percentages sum to 100, and the SQL function itself was independently confirmed via direct psql against the VPS before the frontend was ever written.

Preceded by v3.208.0, a real bug in the kinetic headline fixed, and real marketing content added for both audiences. Reported directly against a screenshot: the hero headline's first letter was rendering as a smeared, glitchy shape instead of text, and broader frustration that the page's wording, layout, and marketing content still weren't landing.
