Verified live: `#employers` sits at ~2146px, `#employers-how` at ~3039px, `#employers-features` at ~3305px — a strictly increasing scroll position matching the nav's left-to-right order. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.90.0, reported directly right after v3.89.0 shipped: the employer nav looked "short" without How it works, and the Home button didn't seem to do anything.

TWO REAL GAPS IN THE JUST-SHIPPED AUDIENCE NAV, FOUND BY ACTUALLY USING IT. v3.89.0's employer list was Home/Features/Pricing/Contact, deliberately dropping "How it works" per the original ask ("if the user employer is should show the feautures and price and contact us"). Asked again right after seeing it live: employer should have "How it works" too. The `#employers` section already contains its own STEP 1/2/3 flow (`EMPLOYER_STEPS` in `LandingSections.tsx`) sitting inside the same block "Features" pointed at, so added a dedicated anchor, `id="employers-how"`, around just that step flow, and gave employer its own "How it works" entry pointing there — `EMPLOYER_LINKS` is now Home, How it works (`#employers-how`), Features (`#employers`), Pricing, Contact, matching the seeker list's shape. `LandingSections.tsx`'s hash-driven audience-flip effect was widened to also recognize `employers-how` as an employer-only anchor.

Second, a real bug, not a scope question: `Header.tsx`'s "Home" link is `<Link to="/">`, and React Router treats a click to the location you're already on as a no-op — no navigation, no re-render, no scroll. On the landing page itself, once a visitor has scrolled down into either audience's content, clicking "Home" in the nav did visibly nothing, reading exactly as "the button doesn't work." Fixed with a shared `handleNavClick(path)` on both the desktop pill nav and the mobile sheet nav: it still closes the mobile sheet as before, and additionally calls `window.scrollTo({ top: 0, behavior: 'smooth' })` whenever the clicked link is `/` and the visitor is already on `/`. Every other nav link is a real route change (or a hash on the same page landing on a different, unvisited section) and didn't need the same treatment.

Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Confirmed live that toggling to "I am hiring" now shows Home/How it works/Features/Pricing/Contact, that both `#employers-how` and `#employers` are real, correctly-positioned anchors in employer mode, and that clicking either keeps the page in employer mode rather than flipping back to seeker. The Home-button fix itself couldn't be watched complete smoothly in this session's automated browser tooling — `scroll-behavior: smooth` scrolls reliably start (confirmed the first animation frame moves the page) but the tooling's background tab doesn't run further animation frames to let them finish, a known limitation of driving a page this way, not a sign the code is wrong — so it was instead confirmed by direct inspection: the click handler fires, `window.scrollTo` is called with the right arguments, and an instant (non-animated) scroll to the same target lands in the correct place every time.

Preceded by v3.89.0, asked directly to make the header nav audience-relative instead of always showing "For employers" as a fixed link.

HEADER NAV NOW FOLLOWS THE SAME AUDIENCE TOGGLE THE LANDING PAGE ALREADY HAS. Reported directly from a screenshot of the top nav bar (Home | How it works | Features | For employers | Pricing | Contact): "For employers" should not be a permanent link, and the link set itself should follow whichever audience the visitor picked (`LandingSections.tsx`'s "I am looking for a job" / "I am hiring" switch, v3.17.0), same as the sections below the hero already do. `Header.tsx`'s `navLinks` had always been one fixed array shown to everyone regardless of audience, with no connection at all to that switch's state, itself only ever read locally inside `LandingSections.tsx` (a `useState` seeded from `localStorage`, with no cross-component signal when it changed). New shared `src/lib/landingAudience.ts` (`readAudience`, `writeAudience`, `onAudienceChange`) is now the one place both components read and write `ayn_landing_audience` — `LandingSections.tsx`'s `pickAudience`/`setAudienceForHash` now call `writeAudience()` instead of a raw `localStorage.setItem`, which also fires a `CustomEvent` (the same `window.dispatchEvent` pattern `CookieConsent.tsx`'s `openCookiePreferences` already uses) so a mounted listener can react in the same tab, not just across tabs via the native `storage` event. `Header.tsx` now holds two link lists instead of one: job-seeker (Home, How it works, Features, Pricing, Contact) and employer (Home, Features, Pricing, Contact — no "How it works"), picked by a local `audience` state seeded from `readAudience()` and kept live via `onAudienceChange`. The employer list's "Features" link deliberately points at `/#employers`, not `/#employers`'s old, separate "For employers" link's replacement of `/#features` — `#features` only ever mounts in job-seeker audience (v3.17.0's one-audience-at-a-time design), so an employer-mode "Features" link has to target the section that's actually mounted for that audience, which is `#employers`, the same section "For employers" used to point at. Verified live: toggling "I am hiring" on the landing page itself flips the header nav immediately, with no page reload, from the seeker list to Home/Features(`#employers`)/Pricing/Contact; clicking that "Features" link scrolls to the employer section and correctly stays in employer mode rather than flipping back; navigating to `/pricing` with employer already selected loads Header fresh from `localStorage` and shows the employer list from the first render, no flash of the seeker list. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.88.0, the exact same "stuck loading behind a Sign in required toast" symptom reported again — after the v3.84.0 fix for it was already live.

A REAL, DEEPER CAUSE THE v3.84.0 FIX NEVER COVERED. v3.84.0 fixed the module-level auth cache going stale after an explicit sign-out somewhere its listener wasn't mounted to hear. It never covered a session that goes bad on its own — an expired refresh token, a silent failed refresh — without a real `SIGNED_OUT` event to correct the cache. `Index.tsx` only checks whether a cached session is *present*, never whether it's still valid, so it kept routing to `/resume-hub` on stale trust; `ResumeHub.tsx`'s own real server-side check correctly caught the bad session, toasted, and bounced to `/` — which read the same untouched cache and routed straight back. Same visible loop, different root cause, not fixed by the earlier patch. Fixed by having `ResumeHub.tsx` call `supabase.auth.signOut()` before leaving whenever its own check disagrees with how it got there, forcing the cache to actually correct itself instead of repeating the same disagreement forever. The identical anti-pattern existed in `Billing.tsx` and `EmployerPending.tsx` (independent `getUser()` checks bouncing to `/` without correcting the shared cache) and got the same fix; `Settings.tsx` uses a local-only `getSession()` check that can't hit this specific disagreement, left alone. Separately, `ResumeHub.tsx`'s auth check had no error handling at all — a genuine network failure left it loading forever with nothing to recover it — now shows a real retry screen instead. Not verified live: the trigger needs a genuinely expired/failed-refresh session to force on demand, and the fix isn't deployed yet regardless. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.87.0, "sign in lag" and sign-out reported directly from a live screenshot of `ayn.careers/resume-hub` stuck on the loading screen with a "Sign in required" toast layered over it.

STALE-DEPLOY CHUNK RECOVERY HAD A REAL GAP, FOUND LIVE. Reproduced sign-in and sign-out step by step against production (real signup, real sign-in, real sign-out through the actual UI) — both worked cleanly every time, not reproducible on demand. What the browser console caught during that same session: `Failed to load module script: ... MIME type of "text/html"`, the signature of a tab holding an old build's chunk manifest after the server has redeployed to new file hashes (Lovable pushed 11 separate auto-deploys the single day this was reported, so a tab open across one of those hitting a not-yet-fetched route is a real, frequent trigger). `ErrorBoundary.tsx` already had a recovery path for exactly this failure class, but its string match only covered one browser wording (`"Failed to fetch dynamically imported module"`) — the literal phrasing caught live was different wording for the identical cause, and slipped past uncaught. Separately, `PreloadRoutes` in `App.tsx` (background-warms the Settings/Pricing chunks) had zero error handling at all — a bare `import()` with nothing catching it, which is an unhandled promise rejection outside any React render cycle, so the ErrorBoundary wrapping the whole app could never see it fail. Fixed both: one shared, exported `isStaleChunkError()` matching the common substring instead of two copies of an exact-phrase check that could (and had) drifted apart, and `PreloadRoutes` now catches a failed warm-up and self-heals with the same one-time reload if it matches. Verified directly against the exact real error string captured live: now caught; the original phrasing still works; unrelated errors still correctly ignored. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.86.0, asked directly to run a full end-to-end test of every button, every feature, backend and frontend, plus UX/UI, performance, stability, and AI quality — not a planning document, an actually-executed pass.

A FULL LIVE PASS, NOT A CODE READ. Created real throwaway accounts (seeker, employer, and a temporary admin-flagged account) and drove every core loop through the real UI against the live backend: signup through email confirmation, resume upload and parsing, the resume optimizer, Jobs tab score/tailor/cover-letter, the discoverability toggle and its consent record, the employer's required company-profile gate, the widget-driven candidate-search intake, a real assessment sent and answered and AI-graded, a real proposal sent and accepted, and a walkthrough of every top-level admin panel section. Two background code audits ran in parallel (frontend wiring, extension source against the pending Chrome Web Store submission) plus a Supabase advisor sweep, all synthesized against what was found live.

SIX REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED. One, the post-checkout confirmation page (`/subscription-success`, the real redirect target of the real, current `stripe-billing` checkout flow) was still wired to `SubscriptionContext`, the retired consulting product's message-tier billing system — a paying customer landing there after a real charge would see "Welcome to Free! ... 5 messages per day" instead of the plan they actually bought, and the domain migration made this worse by breaking the underlying `check-subscription` call's CORS in production too (previously silently "worked" only because prod was still `aynn.io`, the domain that function's CORS was still gated to). Rebuilt `SubscriptionSuccess.tsx` to read the real plan from `billingApi.plans()` using the `?plan=` query param `stripe-billing` already sets, with a graceful fallback when no match is found. Since that was the context's last real consumer, removed the whole `SubscriptionProvider` from `App.tsx` too — it was firing a doomed-to-CORS-fail `check-subscription` call on every signed-in page load for every real user, for a feature nothing legitimately needed any more.

Two, the "Company website" field on the employer signup modal (`AuthModal.tsx`) captured what a founder typed and then silently discarded it — never included in the `signUp()` metadata at all, confirmed live by checking `orgs.website` after a real signup came back null despite a real URL typed in. The founder re-types the same URL one screen later on the required company-profile gate regardless, so removed the dead field outright rather than build a second, redundant save path for a field the very next screen already owns.

Three, a real React warning caught live while completing that same company-profile gate: `EmployerHub.tsx`'s `handleOrgSaved` called `toast()` from inside a `setOrg(prev => ...)` functional updater, which trips React's "cannot update a component while rendering a different component" guard once the Toaster's own internal state update collides with the in-flight `setOrg`. Fixed by computing `was`/`now` from the existing `org` closure and calling `toast()` after `setOrg`, not inside its updater.

Four and five, in the Chrome extension, found by a background code audit and fixed ahead of the pending Web Store review: `BOOTSTRAP`'s error handler in `background.js` dropped the `code` field `sidepanel.js` needs to tell "outdated build" apart from "bad token" — meaning if the backend ever refuses this build (`extension_min_version`), both `bootAfterAuth` and `restoreSession` would wipe the still-valid device token and drop the user on a generic "Sign-in failed" screen, which mints a fresh token, gets refused again, and loops forever with no path to the real fix (reinstall). Fixed both call sites to check for `extension_outdated` first, matching the pattern already used elsewhere in the same file. Separately, `runScoreFlow`'s catch block suppressed the error element for automatic (page-load) scoring attempts, contradicting its own comment ("surfaced via err element") — a real network/credit/auth failure on the automatic first score left the job banner showing nothing at all, looking broken. Fixed to always surface it.

Six, removed the unused `webNavigation` permission and a stale `http://*/*` entry in `web_accessible_resources` from `manifest.json` — directly relevant given the extension has a broad-host-permissions review pending at the Chrome Web Store right now; an unused declared permission is exactly the kind of thing a reviewer doing an in-depth pass flags. Version bumped to 3.4.2 (manifest.json, content.js's `AYN_BUILD` fallback), rebuilt via `node extension/build.mjs`, `check-wiring` passed clean.

DATABASE PERFORMANCE, FROM THE ADVISOR SWEEP. `user_profile_canonical` — a hot-path table touched on nearly every resume-hub call — had two complete, overlapping sets of RLS policies: an old set (`upc_insert_own`/`upc_select_own`/`upc_update_own`) using raw, per-row-re-evaluated `auth.uid()`, sitting alongside a newer set that already used the correct `(select auth.uid())` form and enforced the identical rule. Dropped the old set, fixing both the auth-reevaluation finding and a redundant-policy finding on the same table in one move, zero behavior change. `support_tickets` had a literal duplicate: "Admins can view all tickets (2)" was a strict subset of "Admins can view all tickets," dropped. Two duplicate indexes on `extension_link_codes` dropped (one for `code`, already covered by its own unique constraint; one for `expires_at`, defined twice under different names). Added three missing FK indexes on real, live, frequently-joined tables: `employer_searches.created_by`, `employer_searches.org_id`, `subscriptions.plan_key`.

AI QUALITY, VERIFIED WITH A DELIBERATE TRAP. Built a test resume and a matching JD that both required Kubernetes, but the resume never mentioned it. Scoring, tailoring, and the cover letter all correctly identified the Kubernetes gap and never once claimed the candidate had it — the honesty rule holds through every one of the three surfaces, not just scoring. Every numeric figure in the original resume ("4 hours to 40 minutes," "2.1 million records," "35 percent," "40,000 daily active terminals," "28 percent") survived tailoring completely unchanged. The cover letter had zero em or en dashes and none of the banned cliché phrases. The employer-side candidate search independently produced a 95/100 match against the same profile with an accurate, grounded "why AYN picked them" writeup; the assessment it generated referenced the candidate's actual named employers from their resume, and AI grading scored the real answers 98/100 with an accurate summary, correctly withholding the score from the candidate-facing growth note per the existing design.

EVERYTHING ELSE, CONFIRMED HOLDING, NO REGRESSIONS. `error_logs` writes were reconfirmed live-working (a direct test insert succeeded and was cleaned up) after the trigger-chain fix from earlier the same day. Every credit-mint and auth-boundary fix from the v3.79.0 through v3.83.0 audits was re-checked directly against `pg_proc` grants and live curl calls: `credit_grant`/`credit_spend`/`billing_ensure` are still service-role only, `send-notifications` and `save-generated-image` still correctly reject unauthenticated calls, admin RPCs are still authenticated-but-self-checking. Sign-out (both seeker and employer surfaces) and the v3.84.0 redirect-loop fix both still land cleanly. The admin panel's 6-digit PIN entry, lockout counter, and the "who has admin access" self-protection (no remove-access button on your own row) all work as documented, tested via a throwaway admin-flagged account with the real PIN hash temporarily swapped and exactly restored afterward (a mistake mid-test — an `::jsonb` cast on a plain `text` column silently wrapped the hash in literal quote characters — was caught by checking the column type directly rather than continuing to guess, since repeated wrong PINs would have started eating into the account's own lockout counter).

FLAGGED, NOT FIXED — surfaced for a founder decision, not acted on in this pass. `content.js`'s generic JD-extraction fallback (used on any career site not in the explicit site map) skips the noise-stripping every selector-driven path routes through, and the Hub's manual Score/Tailor/Cover-letter buttons have no local `jdQuality()` gate the way the background auto-resolver does — a real risk of scoring against boilerplate on unsupported sites, but fixing it touches the core extraction pipeline this tooling cannot fully live-test (no way to load an unpacked MV3 extension or hold a real third-party session here). Four confirmed-orphaned exports in `src/lib/resumeHub.ts` — `parse`, `ingestJob`, `talentPoolSkillDelete`, and `mintToken` (`token_mint`) — zero callers anywhere in `src/` or `extension/`; `mintToken` in particular reads like it should have a UI entry point for a personal-access-token feature that was never built. `docs/map/extension.md` and this file still describe a "Tracker" tab and `ext_save_application`/`ext_get_applications`/`ext_update_application` actions that don't exist anywhere in the current code (confirmed by repo-wide grep) — stale documentation, not a runtime bug. 69 of 70 real accounts have no terms-consent record; confirmed live that the v3.33.0 fix genuinely works for real signups today (a fresh test account got a correct record instantly), so this is the old, pre-fix backlog the founder's own Overview dashboard already surfaces every time it's opened ("Should be zero"), not a new regression.

Preceded by v3.85.0, asked directly to move the whole product from aynn.io to a newly-bought domain, ayn.careers, as a hard cutover (aynn.io deliberately stops being supported, not a dual-domain transition).

DOMAIN MIGRATED, 48 FILES, VERIFIED LIVE BEFORE TOUCHING CODE, NOT JUST TRUSTED. The founder's own domain-level setup (DNS pointed at hosting, Supabase Auth's Site URL, Resend sending-domain verification) is outside this repo and had to happen first — changing code to point at `ayn.careers` before it actually resolved would have broken every real login/reset link for users still on the live `aynn.io` site in the meantime. Rather than take "done" at face value, independently confirmed the one piece checkable from here before writing anything: `curl`/`dig` against `ayn.careers` directly, confirmed real DNS resolution, a valid Let's Encrypt cert, and the actual AYN app's title in the response body, not a parked page.

Swept every literal `aynn.io` reference across the repo (`grep -rl`, not a guess at file locations): 40 non-extension files (`_shared/originGuard.ts`'s CORS allowlist, `_shared/sim-utils.ts`'s duplicate allowlist, `SEO.tsx`'s canonical/OG URLs, `index.html`'s meta tags and structured data, all 8 legal documents, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, and every edge function that references the domain in an email template or footer) plus 8 extension files (`manifest.json`'s `externally_connectable.matches` — the actual gate controlling which sites the extension is allowed to talk to — `background.js`'s `AYN_WEB` constant, `deep-link.js`, `sidepanel.js`, `sidepanel.html`). Confirmed zero remaining `aynn.io` references anywhere in tracked source afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout.

10 edge functions redeployed via the Supabase CLI to actually put the new CORS allowlist and email templates into production, not just committed locally: `admin-broadcast`, `stripe-webhook`, `resume-match`, `resume-hub`, `admin-auth-pin`, `send-email`, `auth-send-email`, `send-notifications`, `send-admin-email`, `stripe-billing`. Verified live with real CORS preflight requests, not just by reading the deployed code: a request from `https://ayn.careers` gets `access-control-allow-origin: https://ayn.careers` back; the identical request from `https://aynn.io` now gets the same `ayn.careers` value echoed back instead (i.e. does NOT match its own origin) — a real browser sitting on `aynn.io` today would now fail CORS against every one of these functions, exactly the hard cutover asked for.

The Chrome extension (mid-submission to the Web Store at the time, see v3.84.0's context) needed the same treatment: `externally_connectable.matches` previously gated the extension to only ever talk to `aynn.io` domains, so shipping the extension without this update would have meant a published, installable extension that could never actually connect to the live (now `ayn.careers`) site. Version bumped 3.4.0 to 3.4.1 (manifest.json plus the `AYN_BUILD` fallback literal in `content.js`, per the standing version-bump protocol), rebuilt via `node extension/build.mjs`, `check-wiring` passed clean.

Two lines in this file's own "What AYN is" overview and nervous-system diagram (below) were updated to say `ayn.careers` as current fact, since those describe the system as it stands today, not a dated historical event — every dated version-history entry above and below this one that mentions `aynn.io` was deliberately left untouched, since each describes what was true at the time it was written, and rewriting past entries to match current state would make this file's own history inaccurate.

Not yet done, needs the founder directly: nothing in the Stripe dashboard itself (business URL/branding shown at checkout) was touched, since that's dashboard-only, not code. The Chrome Web Store listing's own text fields (description, homepage/support URL, drafted together in this same conversation) still need pasting in with the `ayn.careers` version before submitting.

Preceded by v3.84.0, reported directly from a live screenshot of production: signing out flickered and never finished loading, showing a stuck "Loading" spinner and a "Sign in required" toast.

REAL, LIVE, PRE-EXISTING SIGN-OUT REDIRECT LOOP, FOUND AND FIXED — NOT CAUSED BY THE v3.82.0/v3.83.0 WORK, JUST SURFACED RIGHT AFTER IT SHIPPED. Root cause: `Index.tsx`'s module-level auth cache (`cachedSession`/`cachedUser`/`cachedInitialized`, added to skip re-checking auth on every visit to `/`) was only ever kept correct by a listener living inside `<Index>`'s own `useEffect` — meaning it only heard `SIGNED_OUT` while `<Index>` itself happened to be mounted. Sign out from anywhere else (Resume Hub, Employer Hub, Settings — every real place sign-out actually lives in this app) left the cache stale, still believing the old session was valid. The next `navigate("/")` (which `ResumeHub.tsx`'s own sign-out handler calls immediately) mounted a fresh `<Index>`, which trusted the stale "signed in" cache without ever rechecking (`if (cachedInitialized) return;` skipped the real session check entirely), rendered `AuthedShell`, and bounced straight back to `/resume-hub` — which ran its own real, correct auth check, found no session, toasted "Sign in required," and navigated back to `/` — which hit the exact same stale cache again. Infinite loop, visibly a flicker between the loading spinner and the toast, exactly matching the reported screenshot.

Fixed by moving the `onAuthStateChange` subscription to module scope in `Index.tsx`: it now runs once for the whole app's lifetime, independent of whether `<Index>` is currently mounted, so the cache can never go stale behind a sign-out happening on a different page. The component itself now just subscribes to a small local pub-sub (`cacheListeners`) that the module-level listener notifies, syncing its own React state whenever the shared cache changes while mounted — same "skip the loader flash on repeat visits" behavior preserved, just correct now regardless of where sign-out is triggered from.

Verified live by reproducing the exact failure path, not just reading the fix: created a throwaway account, signed in, navigated to `/resume-hub` (so `<Index>` unmounts), signed out from Resume Hub's own account menu — before the fix this is precisely the loop condition; after the fix, landed cleanly and stayed on `/`, real `LandingPage` content rendered, zero console errors beyond the already-known local-dev `SubscriptionContext` CORS noise. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Test account erased after.

Preceded by v3.83.0, asked directly to delete three categories of confirmed-dead code the v3.82.0 audit surfaced: the orphaned PIN-bypass edge-function subsystem, the ~45 dead frontend files, and the ~20 dead old-consulting DB functions.

THREE DEAD-CODE CATEGORIES DELETED, ONE FUNCTION SAVED BY RE-VERIFYING LIVE DATA BEFORE DROPPING IT. `verify-admin-pin` (containing the hardcoded `5765b3bb`/`1234` PIN bypass), `set-admin-pin`, `admin-notifications`, and `approve-pin-change` — the whole orphaned, superseded PIN-verification/PIN-change subsystem — deleted via the Supabase CLI, re-confirmed zero frontend callers first. None had local source in this repo.

22 confirmed-dead frontend files deleted: the `src/components/support/` cluster (`SupportWidget.tsx`, `AISupportChat.tsx`, `FAQBrowser.tsx`, `TicketList.tsx`, `UserTicketDetail.tsx` — `TicketForm.tsx` in the same folder stays, it's the real live `/contact` form), the `src/components/dashboard/` pair, and 15 orphaned `src/components/ui/` primitives (`BetaBadge.tsx`, `premium.tsx`, `spline.tsx`, `spotlight.tsx`, `calendar.tsx`, `collapsible.tsx`, `form.tsx`, `form-error.tsx`, `popover.tsx`, `progress.tsx`, `sidebar.tsx`, `skeleton-layouts.tsx`, `slider.tsx`, `use-toast.ts`, `avatar.tsx`). Re-verified every file with precise import-path grep before deleting, not the v3.82.0 audit's basename-substring grep, which had real false positives (`form.tsx`, `progress.tsx`, `popover.tsx`, and `avatar.tsx` all looked orphaned by basename but are genuinely imported elsewhere — those four were kept). The now-fully-unused `@splinetool/react-spline`/`@splinetool/runtime` dependencies were also removed from `package.json`.

CORRECTION to the v3.82.0 entry: `support-bot` does **not** have a live caller after all. Its only frontend caller, `AISupportChat.tsx`, itself had zero importers anywhere — the whole `support/` cluster was already unreachable dead UI before this pass touched it, so `support-bot`'s wrong-product system prompt was never actually shown to a real user. The v3.82.0 "real users get a wrong answer" framing was inaccurate; downgraded here to "confirmed dead code, deleted."

~32 dead old-consulting-era `SECURITY DEFINER` functions dropped (message-tier billing, chat memory, an encrypted-alert subsystem, old client-intake fields, generic admin-audit helpers superseded by `get_admin_*`, and the World-Intelligence-era functions already referencing tables the v3.81.0 cleanup dropped) — full list in `supabase/migrations/20260807201500_drop_dead_old_consulting_functions.sql`. One function was pulled from the drop list after re-checking real data rather than trusting the earlier audit: `check_visitor_analytics_rate_limit` looked identical in shape to its sibling `check_webhook_rate_limit` (both flagged "dead, generic" in v3.82.0), but `visitor_analytics` actually holds 1,926 rows with 235 written in the last 7 days, gated by a real INSERT policy that calls this function directly — it is genuinely live and was left alone. `check_webhook_rate_limit`, by contrast, references a table (`webhook_rate_limits`) that doesn't exist at all — confirmed actually dead and broken, dropped. `check_application_rate_limit` needed `CASCADE` (a real but empty, 0-row `service_applications` table's own INSERT policy called it directly); `extract_memories_from_message()`'s trigger was dropped alongside it, safe since its only writer, `ayn-unified`, was already deleted in v3.81.0. `call_agent_if_not_debounced()` and the 9 `trigger_*` functions that call it were deliberately left alone — that's the live, currently-broken `error_logs` bug from v3.82.0, a separate founder decision, not a dead-code sweep.

Verified after all three: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a fresh dev server and fresh browser tab showed zero console errors on `/`, `/help`, and `/contact` (the page most likely to break, since it renders `TicketForm.tsx` from the same directory two of the five deleted support files lived in).

Preceded by v3.82.0, the four-area audit from the v3.81.0 request completed (edge functions, legacy SECURITY DEFINER functions, src/components/, extension source), run as four parallel investigations. Four real, live, unauthenticated security bugs found and fixed immediately; everything else surfaced is reported below for a founder decision, not yet acted on.

FOUR REAL, LIVE, UNAUTHENTICATED SECURITY BUGS FOUND BY A FULL SOURCE READ AND FIXED. The v3.79.0 audit had curl-tested ~30 edge functions for auth-boundary *behavior* (does it reject a bad token) but never read their full source; this pass did, and reading the actual logic (not just probing the boundary) is what caught these — none of the four rejected a bad token differently than expected, they simply never checked auth at all.

`send-notifications` had `verify_jwt:false` and zero auth check in its body: anyone with the public anon key could POST `{type, subject, content, recipient_email}` and have it send a real "AYN System" branded email to that address from aynn.io, or pass a `user_id` instead to resolve an arbitrary account to its real email via `auth.admin.getUserById` and message that person directly — with `content` interpolated into the outgoing HTML with zero escaping, a phishing/HTML-injection primitive riding on AYN's own sending domain. It has no legitimate frontend caller; its one real caller, `cost-monitor`, is itself dead (references a `track_user_cost` RPC that doesn't exist, already flagged in v3.79.0). Locked to service-role-only (the same "is this call internal" check `ayn-unified` already used), plus `content`/`metadata` now go through `escapeHtml` regardless.

`send-email` had the identical missing-auth problem, but this one has a real, live caller: `AuthModal.tsx`'s post-signup welcome email. Unauthenticated, anyone could send any of its 9 branded templates — including a fake "Payment Successful" receipt or a fake "Password Reset Requested" notice — to any address. Requiring full auth would have broken the one legitimate caller: this project has email confirmation on, so `signUp()` returns no session at the exact moment the welcome email fires, meaning that call is structurally unauthenticated today. Fixed by restricting every template except `welcome` (the one low-sensitivity template actually sent without a session) to require the real service-role key; `welcome` is deliberately left reachable without auth, a residual low-severity risk (someone could still spam an arbitrary address a generic "Welcome to AYN!" email) noted here rather than fully closed, since closing it means moving the trigger server-side (into the `auth-send-email` hook, which fires unspoofable, or similar) — a bigger architectural change, not a same-pass patch.

`send-admin-email` checked only that the caller held *any* valid session, never that they were an admin — unlike every sibling admin-email function (`admin-broadcast`, `custom-auth-emails`, `send-reply-email`, `send-ticket-reply`, `verify-admin-pin`, all of which check `has_role(...,'admin')`). Any freshly signed-up account could send email "from" `admin@aynn.io` to anyone. Confirmed zero frontend callers (orphaned from the deleted v3.20.0 admin dashboard) but still directly reachable. Fixed with the identical `has_role` check `admin-broadcast` already uses. Reproduced live before and after: a real throwaway account's own session got a real `200 success` before the fix and a real `403 Admin access required` after, using the exact same request.

`save-generated-image` had no auth check and zero frontend callers (dead feature), and its only gate on what URL it would fetch was `imageUrl.includes('oaidalleapiprodscus.blob.core.windows.net')` — a substring check, not a hostname check. A URL like `https://attacker.example/x?q=oaidalleapiprodscus.blob.core.windows.net` satisfied it while the real fetch target was attacker-controlled; the function then fetched that URL and re-uploaded whatever came back into the public `generated-images` bucket, unauthenticated. This was an open, anonymous fetch-and-publicly-host primitive. Fixed with a real `new URL(imageUrl).hostname` comparison plus service-role-only auth, same pattern as `send-notifications`.

All four had no local source anywhere in this repo before this pass (pulled live via `get_edge_function`, matching the same "closing the tracked-source gap" pattern as `auth-send-email` in v3.43.0); all four are now committed under `supabase/functions/<name>/`. Verified live end to end via curl against the deployed functions (not just read): `send-notifications` and `save-generated-image` both now return `401` for the exact request shape that used to succeed; `send-email` returns `401` for `payment_receipt` but still `200` for `welcome` (the one real caller, unchanged); `send-admin-email` returns `403 Admin access required` for a real non-admin session that previously got a real `200`. Test accounts created for verification were fully erased after (`erase_account_core`).

EVERYTHING ELSE THE AUDIT FOUND, NOT YET ACTED ON — surfaced for the founder to prioritize, not fixed in this pass:

- **A real, live, currently-broken piece of the product, pre-existing and unrelated to the v3.81.0 World Intelligence deletion**: a trigger on `error_logs` (where `ErrorBoundary.tsx` writes every real production crash) calls `call_agent_if_not_debounced()`, which references a table, `agent_event_debounce`, dropped back in mid-June (`20260612201549_decommission_dead_features`). Every real error report since ~18 May has been silently failing to save (wrapped in a client-side try/catch, so no user ever sees it) — `error_logs` has 1,172 rows, none newer than 18 May, despite heavy continuous live testing since. The admin System → Error monitoring pane has been reading an empty table this whole time. Same root cause also breaks `resend-inbound-webhook`'s Telegram lead-notification trigger, lower severity (12 rows total, pre-pivot sales infra). Fix is either dropping the now-pointless agent-society trigger machinery entirely or restoring the table.
- ~~`support-bot` (live caller: `AISupportChat.tsx`) has a system prompt describing the *old* consulting/chat platform~~ — RESOLVED in v3.83.0: `AISupportChat.tsx` turned out to have zero importers itself, so `support-bot` was never actually reachable by a real user; the whole dead `support/` component cluster was deleted, this is now moot rather than fixed.
- `send-ticket-reply` is fully built and admin-role-gated correctly, but the admin panel's real reply flow calls `admin_insert_ticket_message` directly instead — so an admin's reply to a support ticket saves to the database but never emails the user.
- ~~`verify-admin-pin` ... still contains a literal hardcoded bypass~~ — RESOLVED in v3.83.0: deleted, along with the rest of the orphaned PIN-verification subsystem it was part of.
- The extension's stated "read only, never clicks a page" rule has one real, live exception: `expandSeeMore()` in `content.js` clicks "see more" buttons on job pages on every JD extraction (to expand truncated text), with guardrails (negative-match regex, 3-click cap) but a literal `.click()` on third-party page elements nonetheless. `handoff-hydrate.js` also appends a floating toast div to any HTTPS page's `document.body` (cosmetic, auto-removed after 6s) — a smaller, second instance of the same kind of exception.
- `keep-warm` pings three targets that no longer exist (`ayn-market-intelligence`, `resource-monitor`, `ayn-unified`) — fails safe, but is now 100% non-functional.
- `create-checkout` accepts a client-supplied `credits` value into Stripe's checkout metadata unchecked, with wide-open CORS unlike its siblings — flagged as needing a follow-up check against whether `stripe-webhook` trusts that value verbatim for top-up grants (that function was out of scope for this pass).
- ~45 fully orphaned frontend files confirmed via import-grep, zero regressions found in anything reachable: a dead `src/components/support/` cluster (superseded by `/contact` since v3.53.0, `TicketForm.tsx` itself is NOT dead and stays), the already-known-orphaned `src/components/dashboard/` pair, and 12 unused `src/components/ui/` primitives including two off-brand leftovers (`BetaBadge.tsx`, purple/violet; `spline.tsx`, a 3D Spline wrapper in gold `#C9A84C`, which is also why `@splinetool/react-spline`/`@splinetool/runtime` sit unused in `package.json`).
- ~20 more old-consulting-era `SECURITY DEFINER` functions confirmed via raw `pg_proc.proacl` (not just `information_schema`, which can miss default grants) to be `service_role`-only — none directly callable by any signed-in or anonymous user, so none are a `credit_grant`-shaped exploit. A few reference tables dropped by past cleanups and would error if ever called (`get_global_intelligence_dashboard`, `get_predictions_by_domain`, `ayn_adjust_trust`, `mark_email_opened`'s old `custom_orders`-referencing overload, `increment_template_usage`) — dead and broken, but unreachable, so not fixed here.

Details in docs/map/platform.md and docs/map/extension.md.

Preceded by v3.81.0, asked directly to delete the ayn-unified edge function and "The World Intelligence / prediction-engine backend," as old build, in the same message as a request to audit four previously-unread areas (most of the edge functions, the legacy SECURITY DEFINER functions, most of src/components/, the extension's own source) — the audit is not done yet, the deletion is.

WORLD INTELLIGENCE, THE AGENT SOCIETY, AND ayn-unified DELETED — TRUE SCOPE WAS THREE TIMES THE FIRST ESTIMATE. Investigated both names named in the request before deleting anything. `ayn-unified` turned out NOT to be old build: pulled its live source (no local copy existed in this repo at all) and found a real, modern, well-engineered v3.7.0 job-search-copilot chat endpoint — grounded server-side in the user's own profile/resume/jobs/matches via `jobContext.ts`, a real PII boundary (never loads email/phone/address into the prompt), prompt-injection defense, per-chat and daily rate limits, memory extraction. Its own header comment says it replaced ~1500 lines of the actual old build (crypto/commodity scanning, an autonomous paper-trading loop, a structural-engineering assistant) in a prior rewrite. Reported this plainly rather than deleting on the label alone; the founder's call was to delete it anyway, since `grep -rln "ayn-unified" src` came back empty — real code, but unreachable from anything in this repo, so dead to the product either way.

"World Intelligence" itself was first estimated at ~20 tables from a `public`-schema-only query. A foreign-key dependency check done before writing the deletion migration surfaced an entire second Postgres schema, `archive`, invisible to any `information_schema.tables where table_schema='public'` query — 48 more tables nobody currently working on this app knew existed: the pre-pivot engineering-consulting tool's data (building codes, calculation history, climate zones, material prices, stress-test metrics) plus old pivot-era company/founder-context/outreach data. Real scope was 72 tables across two schemas, not ~20 in one — stopped and re-confirmed the expanded scope with the founder before touching it rather than silently expanding or under-scoping.

Deleted, in one migration (`20260807183526_delete_world_intelligence_and_ayn_unified.sql`) plus a follow-up (`20260807183650_drop_missed_world_intelligence_tables.sql`) for two companion tables (`ayn_job_market`, `ayn_supply_chain`) a post-deletion verification query caught that the first pass missed: 21 dead `pg_cron` jobs (crypto/commodity/geopolitical/health/real-estate/trade/tech/consumer-sentiment intelligence gathering, the agent-society simulation loop — all calling edge functions that no longer exist), the dead `refresh_intelligence_brief()` placeholder function, the entire `archive` schema (`DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE`, cleanly handling the one real cross-schema foreign key), and 26 public-schema tables total. The `ayn-agent-society` and `ayn-unified` edge functions were deleted via the Supabase CLI (`supabase functions delete`); neither had local source in this repo, so no file deletion was needed for them. `ENGIN_URL` (`src/config.ts`), a leftover pointer to `ayn-agent-society` with zero real callers — its only historical consumer, `src/lib/enginApi.ts`, was already deleted in the v3.57.0 World Intelligence page removal — was also removed.

Deliberately left untouched: `ayn_activity_log`/`ayn_error_log` (real, used by `cleanup_old_logs()`/`refresh_daily_summaries()`), 4 crons that call real live generic functions (`ayn-daily-report`, `daily-log-cleanup`, `keep-warm`, `refresh-daily-summaries`), `resource-monitor-hourly` (also dead, but not World-Intelligence-domain-named, out of this specific scope — flagged for a future pass, not fixed here), and several ambiguous tables (`cc_inbox`, `cc_updates`, `ats_config`, `ayn_sales_pipeline`, `ayn_mind`, `news_cache`, `agent_telegram_bots`) not part of what was described and confirmed.

Verified live: TypeScript types regenerated from the live schema (down from ~175-183K to 143,723 chars) and confirmed by grep to hold zero references to any deleted table; `tsc --noEmit` clean; a fresh dev server showed no build errors; a genuinely fresh browser tab (not one with stale console history from earlier testing) loaded the homepage with zero console errors. CLAUDE.md's own prior "Edge functions" section note flagging `ayn-unified` as mysterious and unresolved is now resolved by this entry. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.80.0, asked directly to delete the NDA/contract-signing system and anything else left from the old consulting-era build.

NDA AND CONTRACT-SIGNING DELETED — THE LAST LIVE PIECE OF THE OLD BUILD. Asked directly, right after the v3.79.0 audit surfaced it, to remove it entirely rather than patch around it. Checked for real data first rather than assuming it was all dead test content, same discipline as the v3.77.0 account cleanup: `nda_agreements` held 4 rows, 3 clearly the founder's own testing (company names "dragon"/"deask"/"ghs", contact "Ghazi") but **one a real, fully-executed legal agreement** with an external company (Establishment MAGAMRAT AL-ASAFA For Tourism / STORM, signed by both sides); `custom_orders` held 1 real signed contract. Presented this plainly and got explicit confirmation before touching anything — the founder chose to delete both real records with no export needed, and confirmed the cleanup should stay scoped to NDA/contract-signing only, not a broader old-build sweep.

Removed completely, in order: the 12 real signature image files under `generated-files/signatures/` (the Supabase CLI's experimental `storage rm` silently no-opped — reported success, deleted nothing, confirmed by re-listing — so a one-time temporary edge function using the service-role storage client did the actual deletion instead, verified empty afterward, then deleted itself); `nda_agreements` and `custom_orders` dropped outright (`20260807132552_drop_nda_and_custom_orders_signing.sql`, confirmed zero inbound foreign keys first); the `sign-document` edge function deleted via the Supabase CLI (`supabase functions delete`); `src/pages/NDASign.tsx` and `src/pages/ClientSign.tsx` deleted; the `/nda/:token` and `/sign/:token` routes and their lazy imports removed from `App.tsx`; `/sign/` and `/nda/` removed from `server.js`'s SPA route allowlist; TypeScript types regenerated from the live schema (both tables gone from `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts`). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified live: the app builds and loads with zero console errors on a fresh tab, and hitting the old `/nda/anything` URL now lands cleanly on the real 404 page (which already had exactly the right copy for this: "it belonged to something we retired") instead of any broken or crashed state. The old migrations that originally created these tables (`add_nda_agreements`, `add_contract_fields`, etc.) were left untouched, matching this codebase's standing rule of never rewriting migration history.

Preceded by v3.79.0, asked directly whether the backend and frontend are solid, wired correctly, and free of hacker-penetrable gaps. Found and fixed the most severe bug of this whole audit series: any signed-in user could mint themselves unlimited free credits.

CRITICAL — credit_grant() AND credit_spend() HAD NO OWNERSHIP CHECK AT ALL. A systematic sweep of every SECURITY DEFINER function granted to authenticated/anon (looking specifically for the credit_balance-shaped bug: a function taking an arbitrary target user id, with no auth.uid() check in its own body) turned up two much worse siblings that the earlier audit's narrower, per-function spot-checks had missed. `credit_grant(_user_id, _amount, _reason, _ref)` and `credit_spend(...)` both just trusted whatever `_user_id` was passed, no ownership check, `SECURITY DEFINER`, directly callable by any authenticated user. Reproduced live and unambiguously: a throwaway account called `credit_grant` with its own id and minted itself 999,999,999 credits for free, in one call, no payment, no approval. `credit_spend` had the mirror risk — draining another real user's balance. `billing_ensure()`, which both call internally, had the same missing check with a smaller blast radius. Checked `credit_ledger` for signs this had already been exploited before fixing: clean, only small legitimate and prior-test amounts, no evidence of real abuse. Fixed by revoking `authenticated`/`anon` execute on all three, restricting to `service_role` (`20260807112909_restrict_credit_mutation_functions_to_service_role.sql` — matches how `resume-hub` and `stripe-webhook` already call them, confirmed zero client-side callers first). Re-verified live: the exact same exploit attempt now gets a hard `permission denied`, and the real service-role billing path was re-confirmed still working immediately after.

The same systematic sweep, broadened to catch any SECURITY DEFINER function granted to a public role with no `auth.uid()`/`has_role` check anywhere in its body (not just ones shaped like `credit_balance`), came back clean afterward: only `get_feature_flags()` (intentionally public, no arguments, holds nothing sensitive) and `has_role()` itself (a pure yes/no check, by design) remain unguarded, both already reviewed and accepted.

EDGE FUNCTION SWEEP: ONE REAL PRIVACY FINDING, ONE OPERATIONAL FINDING, ONE REAL PRODUCT-BREAKING BUG. Curl-tested all ~30 previously-unaudited edge functions unauthenticated. `check-email-exists` genuinely does what its name says for anyone with the anon key — `{"exists":true/false}` for any email, no rate limit observed — a real, if fairly low-severity, account-enumeration primitive (very likely intentional, backing an "account already exists" signup UX, a common trade-off; flagged, not changed, since removing it needs a product decision about that UX, not a security patch). `admin-pin-alert` leaks a raw Resend error ("aynn.io domain is not verified") to an unauthenticated caller — minor info disclosure, but the real story underneath it is operational: if the domain is genuinely unverified with Resend, admin PIN-security alert emails may be silently failing to send in production right now, undermining a real security control; needs the founder's own look at the Resend dashboard, not something fixable from here. `cost-monitor` similarly leaks a broken internal RPC reference (`track_user_cost` does not exist) — a real, if minor, broken-cost-tracking bug, info-disclosure only.

REAL, LIVE PRODUCTION BUG FOUND BY ACCIDENT WHILE VERIFYING AN XSS FIX: `/nda/:token` and `/sign/:token` (the legacy contract/NDA signing pages, still linked from old client emails) were completely broken for every real visitor. `sign-document`'s CORS `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` never included `x-application-name`, a header the project's own Supabase client (`src/integrations/supabase/client.ts`) attaches to every `supabase.functions.invoke()` call — so the browser's own CORS preflight silently blocked the request before it ever reached the server, surfacing to a real visitor as a permanent, generic "This link is invalid or has expired." `resume-hub` and `resume-match` had the exact same missing header in their CORS config, but were never actually broken in practice, for a specific, verified reason: their one real caller (`src/lib/resumeHub.ts`) uses a raw `fetch()`, not `supabase.functions.invoke()`, so it never picks up the client's global header config in the first place — confirmed by literally reproducing the CORS block with a manual fetch carrying that header, then confirming resume-hub's real caller doesn't send it. Fixed all three (added `x-application-name` to the allow-list, purely additive, zero risk) and redeployed via the Supabase CLI (`supabase functions deploy`, since `resume-hub` is a ~249KB file with shared `_shared/*.ts` dependencies, impractical to push through a single tool call). Verified live end to end: a real NDA row with a `<script>` payload in it now loads correctly through the actual page, and the payload renders as visible literal text, not executable script (see next finding).

REAL, REPRODUCED STORED-XSS FIX: `NDASign.tsx` rendered five NDA text fields (`nda_purpose`, `confidential_info`, `obligations`, `exclusions`, `additional_clauses`) as raw HTML through a local `nl2br()` helper that converted newlines to `<br/>` with zero escaping. Reproduced live: inserted a real NDA row with a `<script>` payload, loaded the real page, confirmed the payload was one render away from executing. Real-world exploitability was already low going in — the admin UI that used to create NDA rows (`NDAManager`) was deleted in v3.20.0, and grep confirms zero remaining code path writes to these columns, so there is currently no live way for an attacker to get content into them — but the missing escaping was still a real landmine, not a false alarm, and a trivial, zero-behavior-change fix. Fixed by adding a proper `escapeHtml()` step before the newline-to-`<br>` conversion. Verified three ways: `tsc --noEmit` clean, then live in a real browser against a real seeded row — the literal text `<script>window.__xss_fired = true</script>` renders visibly on the page, `window.__xss_fired` stays `false`, and `document.querySelectorAll('script').length` shows only the app's own legitimate script tags, none injected.

METHOD NOTE: this pass leaned on one technique throughout — for any SECURITY DEFINER function suspected of a missing auth check, query `pg_proc`/`information_schema.routine_privileges` directly for the full population of functions matching the risk shape (arbitrary target id parameter + granted to a public role), rather than checking the handful already flagged by an earlier audit or an advisor tool. That systematic sweep is what caught `credit_grant`/`credit_spend` — a per-function, ad hoc review had already looked at `credit_balance` right next to them and missed its two much worse siblings. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.78.0, asked directly to actually execute a real security test pass rather than hand over a QA planning matrix, then to fix a vulnerability it reproduced live.

REAL, EXECUTED SECURITY TEST PASS (NOT A CHECKLIST), TWO NEW BUGS FOUND AND FIXED. First produced a 69-case QA/security test matrix as a static document; told directly this wasn't useful ("i want a full test not reporting") and to delete it. Agreed, deleted the source, and instead actually ran the tests: created 4 disposable test accounts (2 seekers, 2 employers with their own orgs) directly in the database, seeded one real row in every isolation-sensitive table (resume, job match, discovery listing, pending proposal, a real assessment with a real score/rubric), then became each user via `SET LOCAL request.jwt.claims` (the exact mechanism PostgREST itself uses to enforce RLS from a JWT — not a simulation of it) and tried to reach across every boundary. 13 for 13 real isolation attempts blocked: cross-seeker resume/job-match/discovery reads, cross-org reads, an employer reading their own sent proposal or assessment score directly (both correctly blocked even for the sender), a candidate reading their own assessment score, a wrong candidate accepting someone else's proposal, self-escalation to admin via direct table write, calling admin-only RPCs and the vector-search RPC as a non-admin/non-service caller, cross-user storage object access, unauthenticated/garbage-token/forged-signature calls against the live edge functions and Stripe webhook via real curl. `assessment_rubrics`/`assessment_results`/`profiles` turned out to have zero `authenticated`/`anon` grant at all (stronger than RLS filtering) rather than the row-level lockdown assumed from reading policy definitions alone — confirmed live, not just read from code.

TWO REAL BUGS SURFACED BY RUNNING THE TESTS THAT WOULD NOT HAVE SURFACED BY READING CODE. One, a regression from the same day's earlier `duty`-role removal: the 16 rewired policies now call `has_role()` directly, but unlike `has_duty_access()` (which the v3.40.0 audit had confirmed was anon-executable), `has_role()` had no anon grant. Invisible today because `TicketForm.tsx`'s guest insert already avoids `.select()`/`RETURNING` specifically because of a past bug of this exact shape — but `INSERT ... RETURNING` re-checks the new row against SELECT policies, and the next person who adds `.select()` to any guest insert on the five duty-touched tables would hit a hard `permission denied for function has_role` crash instead of a clean result. Fixed: granted `has_role(uuid, app_role)` to anon, restoring the exact anon-safety `has_duty_access` already had (`20260807021213_grant_has_role_to_anon_for_returning_safety.sql`).

Two, live-reproduced, not theoretical: opened a guest support ticket as one anonymous session, then, as a second, unrelated anonymous session, successfully posted a message into the first one's ticket — confirmed via real HTTP requests from an actual browser origin, not just SQL. `ticket_belongs_to_caller()`'s guest branch was "caller is anonymous AND the target ticket has no owner," true for every guest ticket, not just the caller's own, since guests have no session to tie a ticket to. Fixed with a per-ticket secret: `support_tickets.guest_token` and `ticket_messages.guest_token`, both uuid, generated client-side the same way `ticket_id` already is (`crypto.randomUUID()`, never read back from the server) and required to match on every guest write from here on (`20260807022017_guest_ticket_access_token.sql`; `TicketForm.tsx` updated to generate and send it). Verified from three angles: SQL-level legit-guest-succeeds / wrong-token-fails / no-token-fails, then a genuine browser-origin `fetch()` against the live API replaying `TicketForm.tsx`'s exact request shape (real `201 Created` on both inserts, real `401` with the actual RLS-violation body on the impersonation attempt). Practical risk was always low — ticket ids are unguessable UUIDs and no read path exposes them to a non-owner — but it was real and is now closed, not just documented. TypeScript types regenerated from the live schema afterward so `guest_token` is properly typed rather than passed through untyped. All test accounts, seeded rows, and ticket data cleaned up after; one inert leftover (a storage-object metadata row with no real file behind it, tied to an already-deleted test user) that Postgres's own `protect_delete` trigger correctly refused to let go via direct SQL. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.77.0, asked directly for a security audit, then to delete the dead 'duty' role, then to erase every real account except one plus the admin account.

SECURITY AUDIT, DUTY ROLE REMOVED, 25 OLD ACCOUNTS ERASED, A REAL ERASURE BUG FOUND LIVE. Full pass: Supabase's own advisor plus a manual review of what it doesn't catch. One real, confirmed bug fixed: `credit_balance(_user_id uuid)` was `SECURITY DEFINER`, took an arbitrary target user id with no ownership check, and was granted to `authenticated` — any signed-in user could read anyone else's credit balance via a direct RPC call; revoked, service-role only now (matches the only real caller, `resume-hub`'s service client). One narrower gap flagged but deliberately not patched blind: the guest branch of `ticket_belongs_to_caller()` lets an anonymous visitor message into any guest support ticket, not just their own, since there's no session tying a guest to their ticket — low real risk (ticket ids are unguessable), but the actual fix (a per-ticket access token) is a design decision, not a mid-audit patch. Everything else the advisor flagged was already reviewed and accepted in v3.40.0, unchanged.

Then asked directly to delete the dead `'duty'` staff role (confirmed inert since v3.26.0/v3.40.0: zero holders, `useAuth.ts` — its only reader — already had zero importers) rather than leave it disabled. `has_duty_access()` is dropped; the 16 RLS policies that called it now check `has_role(auth.uid(),'admin')` directly; `useAuth.ts` and its test, the last app code that ever read it, are deleted. The bare `'duty'` label is still technically present in the underlying Postgres enum type — `has_role()`, which ~100+ other policies across old legacy tables also depend on, takes that type as a parameter, and Postgres has no `DROP VALUE` for enums, so fully removing the label means rebuilding the type and every dependent function/policy. A first attempt at that failed safely (correctly refused, rolled back cleanly) rather than risk 100+ unrelated policies for a cosmetic cleanup. The label is inert either way; truly deleting it is separate, larger work, not done here.

Then asked directly to erase every real account except `crossmint7@gmail.com` and the `ghazi@aynn.io` admin account. Investigated and presented the full account list (including the session's own logged-in email) before touching anything, given how irreversible this is; got explicit confirmation. Running the erasure surfaced a real, previously-unknown bug in `erase_account_core` itself (the same routine behind the actual Delete Account button): it tried to anonymize `user_usage_daily`/`usage_logs` by nulling `user_id`, but both columns are `NOT NULL` — the exact shape of bug the v3.36.0 audit already caught once in `llm_usage_logs`, just in two tables that slipped through then. Confirmed the failed batch rolled back with zero partial erasure before fixing it (delete the row instead of nulling, matching every other content table in the function). Re-ran clean: all 25 accounts erased and verified (banned, email scrubbed), only the two kept accounts remain untouched. This bug meant real users hitting Delete Account could have silently failed if they'd ever triggered those two usage tables — fixed for everyone now, not just this cleanup. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.76.0, asked directly to remove the "What is kept, and why" section from the delete-account dialog, and why Settings feels heavy to load.

DELETE-ACCOUNT DIALOG TRIMMED, COLD-LOAD CAUSE RE-CONFIRMED. Removed the three-bullet "What is kept, and why" list (payment records, already-sent proposals/assessments, security logs) from `PrivacySettings.tsx`'s delete-account confirmation dialog, plus the now-unused `DELETION_KEPT` export from `src/lib/account.ts` — the dialog now goes straight from "What is removed" to the "Pause instead" option. The actual deletion behavior (`self_delete_account`/`erase_account_core`) is unchanged; this only removed the on-screen explanation. On the load-time question: re-checked the v3.35.0 finding live rather than assuming it still held — `curl` against the deployed `health` function still returns `x-sb-edge-region: us-east-1` today while the project's Postgres is still `eu-west-2`, the same cross-region mismatch, unchanged six days later. Not re-fixed (it's a Supabase project region setting, not application code, same conclusion as before), just re-verified rather than left stale. `tsc --noEmit` clean; verified live on a fresh throwaway account that the dialog section is gone with no layout gap, test account deleted after. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.75.0, asked directly to delete Settings and rebuild it, since it "was for the old build."

SETTINGS REBUILT OFF THE OLD CONSULTING/CHAT PLATFORM, EMBER-BRANDED. Every one of the five tabs was audited against what it actually read or wrote, not just how it looked. Deleted, all confirmed dead or broken: the "Memory" tab (`MemoryManagement.tsx`, an "AYN Memory" viewer for the retired chat product — its own code branches on parking-lot engineering calc fields like `keySpecs.siteArea`, nothing a job seeker or employer would ever see, rendered twice — once as its own tab, once again embedded inside Privacy); Privacy's "Delete chat history" button (posted to a `messages` table that does not exist in the schema — would have thrown, always); Notifications' "in-app sounds"/"desktop notifications" toggles (zero other callers anywhere in `src/`, no live in-app event stream in this product for either to ever fire on); and the search bar plus "unsaved changes" warning dialog (`SettingsContext.tsx`, `useUnsavedChangesWarning.ts` — already flagged as dead in the v3.73.0 entry below, now confirmed and removed: `registerFormChange` had zero callers, so the warning could never fire, and every real control here already saves instantly, there was never a draft to lose). Rebuilt, not just trimmed: Notifications now shows `user_settings.email_marketing`, a real column with zero UI anywhere before this, instead of toggles for a feature that doesn't exist — honestly scoped, and honestly noted as not yet enforced by any email sender, a follow-up not done here. Branding: new `.settings-surface` CSS scope (same recipe as `.contact-surface`, AYN's real ember `#e85d3a`) fixes the page rendering in plain black — same root cause the v3.10.0/v3.12.0/v3.63.0 surface fixes already solved once each (`Button`'s `bg-foreground`/`border-foreground` aren't token-driven), plus a fix for the Tabs component's hardcoded `bg-black` active state nobody had caught before. Verified live with a real throwaway account signed up through the actual auth modal: exactly 4 tabs now (Account, Notifications, Privacy, Sessions), every button and the active tab pill in ember not black, the Notifications toggle genuinely flipped `user_settings.email_marketing` in the database with a real save toast, Sessions showed a real live row, and the delete-account dialog (a Radix portal to `document.body`, outside this page's own tree) also rendered in ember, confirming the scope reaches portaled content. `tsc --noEmit` clean; test account fully deleted after. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.74.0, "Edit in Profile" / "Edit company profile" on Settings > Account correctly routed to the right hub but always landed on that hub's default tab (Home / Search), not Profile or Company — a one-shot `sessionStorage.ayn_open_tab` flag, read and cleared by `ResumeHub.tsx`/`EmployerHub.tsx` on mount, now lands on the actual tab the button names. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.73.0, Settings > Account reported directly from a screenshot: "Unlimited Plan", "Unlimited messages", a "Top Up — 500 extra messages for $10" card, and a profile form asking for "Business type: consulting/ecommerce/agency/saas" — the retired consulting product's billing and client-intake UI, still live and shown to real AYN users.

SETTINGS > ACCOUNT REBUILT ON REAL DATA. `AccountPreferences.tsx` was still reading `SubscriptionContext` (Starter/Pro/Business message tiers that don't exist in AYN's real pricing) and writing to `profiles.contact_person`/`company_name`/`business_type`/`business_context` — consulting-agency client fields, meaningless for a job seeker or an employer. Rebuilt role-aware on the real data every other part of the product already uses: `billingApi.seeker()`/`billingApi.employer()` for a real plan-and-credits (or plan-and-usage) summary with a link to `/billing`, and real name/company plus email for the profile card, with a link to wherever that's actually edited (Resume Hub's Profile tab, or the employer dashboard) rather than a second editable copy of the same facts. Avatar upload was dropped entirely — `profiles.avatar_url` has zero readers anywhere in the live product, so it only ever wrote a value nothing ever showed; its two supporting components in the legacy `dashboard/` folder are now fully orphaned. Change password, already real, was left untouched. Verified live with a real seeker account (showed "Free · active", "6 credits — 2 for a tailored resume, 1 for a cover letter", the real seeded name, correct link to Resume Hub) and a real employer account with an approved org (showed "Free month · trialing", real proposals/assessments/searches usage, the real company name, correct link to the employer dashboard) — both "Manage billing" links landed on `/billing` showing the matching real data. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.72.0, asked directly to check whether scoring and tailoring are synced. The web app's own Score/Tailor/Cover letter buttons in Resume Hub's Jobs tab turned out to be running on a completely different, much older code path than the browser extension's equivalents.

SCORE THIS JOB / TAILOR RESUME / WRITE COVER LETTER WERE NEVER TOUCHED BY THE v3.1.0 REBUILD. The extension's scoring and tailoring (`ext_job_score`, `smart_tailor`, `ext_cover_letter_text`) were rebuilt in v3.1.0 on a shared pipeline: the full canonical profile, a deterministic (non-AI) gap analysis of what the JD asks for versus what's evidenced, figure-preservation verification, and caching. The Hub's own Jobs tab — the actual primary seeker-facing surface, not the extension — was calling three separate, much older actions (`match`/`tailor`/`cover_letter`) that had none of it: no canonical profile (so certifications, skill levels, and everything else Profile collects were invisible to the AI), no deterministic grounding, no figure checking, no cache, and no consistent temperature (the identical "score wobbles on identical input" bug already fixed once for the resume optimizer, present here too). Testing also caught a real, visible defect: the cover letter's bare prompt let the model print a literal "[Hiring Manager name, if known, otherwise title]" placeholder into the letter.

All three now run through the same shared pipeline the extension uses, with the same quality bar (rubric, honesty rule against crediting unevidenced skills, figure preservation, 24h/7-day caching so a repeat click costs zero credits). The output each one hands back to the app is unchanged on purpose — `tailor` still returns a structured resume (JobsTab builds a real PDF/DOCX from that shape) rather than switching to the extension's flat text, since that would have broken the download pipeline for no benefit. Verified live: seeded a certification that exists only in the profile, never in the resume text, and confirmed scoring, tailoring, and the cover letter all correctly picked it up — something structurally impossible before, since the old code never read the profile at all. Then drove the real Score this job / Tailor resume / Write cover letter buttons end to end and confirmed the same behavior through the actual UI. Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.71.0, asked directly to audit every field and toggle on the Profile form against what it actually asks the AI and the employer pool for, and fix whatever was confusing or broken.

PROFILE FORM AUDIT, ONE REAL DATA BUG FOUND. The biggest one: the AI-facing profile summary used in every score/tailor call read a field name (`start_date_availability`) the frontend has never written to, so a seeker's Availability answer never once reached the AI — always read as unset, no matter what was picked. One-line backend fix. Visa type had the identical shape of bug one level worse: the AI prompt has always asked about it, and there was no field anywhere to answer it, so it was permanently "n/a" for every user; added the missing field. Also fixed: Seniority and Primary function were free-text boxes with comma-separated placeholders that read like multi-select instructions, now datalists using the same vocabulary the backend already scores seniority against; Current title/company were the only two resume-derived fields in "About you" missing the "From your resume" / "Edited by you" badge and revert every sibling field has; the "Current role" toggle could be switched on and do nothing if an old date was still sitting in the End field, now clears it automatically; and the three separate "where" fields (home location, desired work locations, legal work eligibility) got one-line cross-references so they don't read as repetitive. Verified live: the availability fix confirmed against the exact database row the AI digest reads from, visa type field appears and saves correctly, every other fix confirmed rendering in a real account. Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.70.0, the employer-preview card that moved into Profile in v3.69.0 (what employers see, skills breakdown, freshness, findability) was reported as clutter, and Certificates had no field anywhere in Profile despite already existing end to end in the schema.

TALENTPOOLCARD DELETED, CERTIFICATES ADDED UNDER EDUCATION. Asked directly to remove the card shown in a screenshot and add a Certificates section under Education. Confirmed by a follow-up question that "remove" meant the whole card (what employers see, skills by provenance, freshness, "how findable you are"), not just one piece of it — `TalentPoolCard.tsx` is deleted outright, along with the `groupGaps` computation in `ProfileTab.tsx` that only existed to feed it. The green/grey "Let employers find me" switch itself (v3.67.0) is untouched and still the only discoverability UI in the Hub. Certificates (`career.certifications`, already read from and written to the database, just never given a form) got one: name, issuer, year, directly under Education in the "Your experience" group, same add/remove pattern as every other repeatable field on the page. Verified live: Profile goes straight from the toggle to "Your resume" with no card in between, and "Certificates (0)" renders right after "Education (0)". Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.69.0, the "Get discovered" nav tab had nothing left in it once the toggle moved to Profile, and was reported as redundant.

GET DISCOVERED TAB REMOVED, FOLDED ENTIRELY INTO PROFILE. Asked directly to remove it, since "Let employers find me" already lives in Profile (v3.67.0). Rather than leave a half-empty tab, the rest of what it held — the "what employers see" preview, skills by provenance, freshness, and the findability checklist — moved with it. `DiscoveryTab.tsx` is deleted; `TalentPoolCard.tsx` is now detail-only (no switch, no badge, no header at all — those live in `ProfileTab.tsx`'s own toggle card) and renders directly beneath it in Profile when discovery is on, returning `null` otherwise. `ResumeHub.tsx`'s nav went from seven items to six. Verified live: the nav rail no longer shows a "Get discovered" icon, and Profile alone — toggle plus full detail breakdown — covers what used to be two tabs. Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.68.0, the "Oops! AYN hit a snag" error screen rendered flush at the top of the page with a generic icon, not centered or on-brand.

ERROR BOUNDARY REBUILT AS A REAL FULL-SCREEN STATE. Reported directly from a screenshot. `ErrorBoundary.tsx`'s fallback was a bare `Card` with no layout wrapper, so it rendered top-left, full width, instead of a proper centered error screen, and used a generic lucide `Brain` icon instead of the real AYN mark. Rebuilt to match the same `min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center` pattern already used by `PlatformMaintenanceScreen` and `AynLoaderScreen`, with the actual `/ayn-mark.svg` in the icon badge. The ember colors were already correct (`.ayn-ember-card` etc. in `src/index.css` already used the site's real ember hue) — only the layout and icon were off. Verified live by temporarily forcing the error state and screenshotting the centered result. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.67.0, "Let employers find me" was one tab away from Profile and easy to miss, and did not read as obviously on or off.

DISCOVERY TOGGLE MOVED INTO PROFILE, COLORED GREEN/GREY. Asked directly to move the switch into Profile and make its state obvious. `ProfileTab.tsx` now owns the toggle itself (own `talentPoolGet`/`talentPoolSet` calls, own confirm dialog), rendered as the first card on the page, right after the readiness banner. The whole card, badge, and switch turn solid green when on and grey when off — a deliberate override of the app's default black/ember switch color, since this is a bigger decision ("employers can see and contact me") than a normal settings toggle. `TalentPoolCard.tsx`, still shown in the Get discovered tab for the detailed breakdown (what employers see, skills by provenance, freshness, findability), lost its own switch entirely and now links back to Profile with a "Manage in Profile" button, so there is exactly one place the value can actually change. Verified live with a real throwaway account: off is grey on load, turning it on shows the same consent dialog as before, the card turns green immediately after, and the Get discovered tab shows the same state. Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.66.0, the resume optimizer's score bounced around on identical input (reported directly: 75/100, ran it again, 65/100).

SCORE CONSISTENCY: SEPARATED WRITING FROM GRADING. A first attempt added a fixed point-deduction rubric (`ATS_RUBRIC`) shared by `resume_diagnose` and `rewrite`, plus a low `temperature`. That genuinely fixed `resume_diagnose` (same input now scores within a few points every time) but not `rewrite` — measured directly, two identical calls scored 90 then 70, wider than before. Root cause: `rewrite` was writing new resume content and grading its own writing in the same completion, two stochastic jobs at once. Real fix: scoring pulled into one shared function, `scoreResumeContent()`, the same low-temperature rubric call `resume_diagnose` already made; `rewrite` now generates (temperature raised slightly, since natural phrasing needs some variation and no longer has to also hold a score steady) then calls `scoreResumeContent()` on its own output as a separate step, so a diagnosis and an optimize's score are always computed by the literal same function. Verified live: 3 consecutive `rewrite` calls on identical input scored 75, 75, 80, verdict "Good" every time, versus the prior 90-then-70 swing. Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.65.0, three real quality problems in the resume optimizer's actual output, reported directly against the founder's own downloaded PDF: it spilled to a second page, nothing was bold except section headers, and the writing read like generic AI-resume copy.

RESUME DOWNLOADS REBUILT FROM STRUCTURED CONTENT, NOT FLATTENED TEXT. `resumeDocs.ts`'s PDF/DOCX builders took a pre-flattened plain-text string and guessed at bolding by regex-matching ALL-CAPS lines, which only ever caught section headers ("EXPERIENCE", "EDUCATION", "SKILLS") — the person's name and every job title rendered in the same plain weight as a bullet. Rebuilt around the structured `ResumeContent` directly: `buildResumeBlocks()` produces typed blocks (name, contact, summary, section header, job/school title, bullet, plain line) with a style table driving bold and size per block kind, so the name and every job title are bold by construction, not by pattern-matching. `buildTextPdfBlob`/`buildTextDocxBlob` are gone; `buildResumePdfBlob`/`buildResumeDocxBlob` replace them, both call sites (`ProfileTab.tsx`, `JobsTab.tsx`) updated. `resumeToText` is untouched and still feeds `ResumeDiffViewer`, which genuinely needs plain text for its diff.

ONE PAGE, FOR REAL. The PDF builder now measures the whole document at a candidate font size before drawing anything, and walks down a size ladder (10.5pt to 8pt) until the content actually fits in one page's height, then draws once at that size — the same "shrink to fit" approach real resume tools use, not the old fixed 10.5pt with a silent second page when it ran long. A genuinely very long resume still falls through to a real page break as a safety net rather than clipping content. The DOCX builder reuses jsPDF purely as a measuring ruler to pick the same size the PDF settled on (Helvetica metrics are close enough to Calibri for this) — Word does its own pagination, so this cannot *guarantee* one page for DOCX the way it does for PDF, but starting from an already-shrunk size gets there in practice.

WRITING THAT SOUNDS LIKE AN AI WROTE IT. The `rewrite` prompt got real teeth: banned a specific list of resume-cliché phrases ("proven ability to", "results-driven", "leveraging", "spearheaded transformational initiatives"), told to vary sentence structure like an actual person, and — the concrete bug this caught — required `skills` entries to be atomic ("React", not "Technical Stack: React, TypeScript, Supabase, Postgres..." crammed into one array item with a colon and a comma list, which is what the model had been doing and is exactly what rendered as a dense, robotic wall of text).

Verified directly against the founder's own real resume content (reconstructed from the PDF he showed, not a synthetic test case): rebuilt the PDF client-side and inspected the raw bytes, not just a screenshot — `/Type /Pages /Count 1` confirms exactly one page, `/F2` (Helvetica-Bold) selected immediately before the name and every job/school title line, `/F1` (plain Helvetica) for contact info, summary, and every bullet. DOCX builder smoke-tested separately, produces a valid `.docx` blob. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.64.0, a free resume diagnosis and a paid (15 credit) resume optimizer, on Profile, plus a warning surfaced in the tailoring flow when the base resume is weak.
