RESUME OPTIMIZER. Asked directly: does AYN handle weird resume formatting, and can we build a feature that scores a resume, offers to fix it, and lets the person download or replace it. Investigation found the hard part already half-built and completely unused: `rewrite`, a `resume-hub` action returning `{resume, ats_score, suggestions}`, had zero callers anywhere in `src/`, and `resumeDocs.ts` already builds real single-column PDF/DOCX output (the actual ATS-friendly format, by construction) for anything downloaded from the app. Extraction itself was already solid: DOCX via `mammoth`, PDF sent whole to a vision-capable model, both reading real-world layouts rather than parsing rigid rules; the one honest gap is a pure scanned image with no text layer, which correctly falls back to "paste your text" rather than failing silently.

Decided with the founder: diagnosis free, fix is 15 credits, and paying automatically replaces the resume in AYN (same `is_primary` swap as a fresh upload), not just a download. New action `resume_diagnose` (free, `DEFAULT_MODEL`, content-quality only — vague bullets, missing quantification, thin sections; deliberately not visual layout, since AYN's own output is always single-column regardless of what was uploaded). `rewrite` gained credit gating (`COST_OPTIMIZE = 15`, previously free and ungated since nothing called it) and a stricter prompt (never invent a metric that is not already implied, consistent "Month YYYY" dates, no em/en dashes). New `resumes.ats_issues` column (jsonb) alongside the already-existing but previously-unused `ats_score`, both cached on the row so the tailoring flow can show a warning without spending another AI call. `ProfileTab.tsx` auto-diagnoses right after every upload (free, silent) and offers a manual "Check my resume" button plus the paid "Optimize my resume" card, showing specific issues and, after optimizing, a "what changed" list. `JobsTab.tsx` shows an amber warning below the tailor/cover-letter buttons when the base resume scores under 70, linking back to Profile (`onOpenProfile` prop threaded through `ResumeHub.tsx`).

Verified live end to end with a real throwaway account and a deliberately weak seeded resume ("Responsible for various marketing tasks."-style bullets): diagnosis correctly scored it 35/100 Poor with six specific, concrete issues naming the actual weak bullets; optimizing charged exactly 15 credits (ledger confirmed, balance 106 to 91), produced a 55/100 Fair rewrite that added a summary and stronger verbs but correctly refused to invent a fake metric (flagging in its own suggestions that the person should add one), replaced the primary resume (old row confirmed `is_primary: false`, new row `is_primary: true`), and the Jobs tab warning banner correctly appeared and linked back to Profile. Test account and data fully deleted after. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.63.0, two branding fixes: the header's "Start Free" button was black on every page except the landing page, and the Contact form's submit button and input focus rings were plain black instead of AYN ember.

HEADER CTA WAS BLACK EVERYWHERE EXCEPT THE LANDING PAGE. `Header.tsx`'s `ctaBackground` was `onLanding ? EMBER : '#0a0a0f'`, a deliberate v-something choice ("other marketing pages stay on the light surface") that read, reported directly from a screenshot, as the "Start Free" button just being wrong-colored on every page but home. Now always ember, and the ember drop shadow that used to be landing-only is on unconditionally too. The mobile sheet's own "Start free" button already hardcoded ember regardless of page and did not need touching.

CONTACT FORM WAS BLACK TOO, SAME ROOT CAUSE AS THE EMPLOYER SURFACE FIX. `TicketForm`'s "Send message" button and every `Input`/`Select`/`Textarea` focus ring read the site's default black `--primary`/`--ring` tokens, since this project's shadcn `Button` is not token-driven (`bg-foreground`/`border-foreground`, not `bg-primary` — the same fact the v3.10.0/v3.12.0 `.employer-surface` fix already documented and solved once for the employer side). Applied the identical pattern for `/contact`: new `.contact-surface` scope in `src/index.css` re-points `--primary`/`--ring` to the site's actual ember (`13 79% 57%`, matching the existing `--lp-ember` token, not the employer surface's brighter orange) and retints `button.bg-foreground`. Class applied both to `Contact.tsx`'s root wrapper and to `document.body` for the page's lifetime (a `useEffect`, same as `EmployerHub.tsx` already does), so Radix's portaled Select dropdown and the sonner toast also pick it up. Scoped to `/contact` only; `TicketForm` has no other live caller since `/help`'s embedded copy was removed in v3.53.0. Verified live: submit button computed `background-color: rgb(232, 96, 59)` (effectively identical to `#e85d3a`), `--ring`/`--primary` both resolve to `13 79% 57%` inside the scope, `document.body` carries the class, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.62.0, /contact's "Specific requests" section removed entirely. Follow-up to v3.61.0: asked directly to remove it rather than just shorten it further. `Contact.tsx` is now just the hero and the form; the privacy/copyright/security/employer-access pointers it held (subject-line conventions, the 30-day privacy response window, the security good-faith-reporting line) aren't lost information, since each is already the authoritative content of its own legal document (`/privacy`, `/copyright`, `/security`) — Contact.tsx was only ever duplicating a pointer to them. Verified live: page goes straight from the form to the footer, `tsc --noEmit` clean, no real console errors.

Preceded by v3.61.0, /contact trimmed for wordiness. Reported directly: too much text. `Contact.tsx` cut from five sections to two — dropped the separate "Before you write" section (folded its one useful line into the form's trailing paragraph) and "What we do not have" (not essential, cut outright) — and every "Specific requests" card shortened from two to three sentences down to one, keeping every fact (30-day privacy response window, self-service export/delete from Settings, the Copyright Policy link, the security good-faith-reporting line, employer access routed through the employer page). Preceded by v3.60.0, the /pricing headline changed from "Pay for the writing, nothing else" to "Less time formatting. More time applying." Exact copy supplied directly. `src/pages/Pricing.tsx`'s H1, nothing else on the page touched.

Preceded by v3.59.0, the header nav's three hash links ("How it works", "Features", "For employers") were structurally broken for most visitors, fixed at the root cause.

HEADER NAV AUDIT: THREE OF SIX LINKS WERE BROKEN FOR MOST VISITORS. Asked directly to check whether the header menu works and is in good sequence. The sequence itself (Home, How it works, Features, For employers, Pricing, Contact) was fine; the links were not. `LandingSections.tsx` renders the landing page in exactly one audience at a time (`{seeker && <section id="features">}` / `{!seeker && <section id="employers">}`, same split on `#proof`), a deliberate v3.17.0 design so a job seeker never scrolls into employer copy. The header's `handleNavClick` never accounted for this: `/#how` pointed at an id that has never existed anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep, always a silent no-op); `/#features` and `/#employers` only worked when the page already happened to be in the matching audience, which is not the default for either one on a fresh visit. Compounding it, the cross-page branch called `navigate('/')` with no hash at all, dropping the target entirely, and the same-page branch set `window.location.hash` directly and immediately called `scrollIntoView` in the same tick, racing React's own re-render before the target section could even mount.

Root-caused rather than patched around: `LandingSections.tsx` already had a `hashchange` listener meant to flip the audience to match an incoming `#employers`/`#features` link, but native `hashchange` never fires for React Router's `history.pushState`-based navigation, so it was already dead for the cross-page case and racy for the same-page one. Replaced it with a `useLocation().hash`-driven pair of effects: one flips the audience (silently, no scroll-to-top) when the hash calls for a mode the page isn't in, the other scrolls to the hash target 60ms later, after that mount has had a chance to land — covering `#proof`, `#features` and `#employers` alike (`#proof` turned out to be seeker-gated too, found while testing the fix). `Header.tsx`'s `handleNavClick` is gone entirely: a hash link is now just a plain `<Link to="/#x">`, which React Router already navigates correctly whether you're on another page or the same one, so there was no need for the custom preventDefault/navigate/scrollIntoView logic at all once the destination page owns its own scroll behavior. `/#how` retargeted to `/#proof`, the closest thing this page has to a "how it works" explainer (there is no dedicated how-it-works section; asked for nothing narrower, so this was judged close enough rather than left broken).

Verified live, not by clicking (the browser automation's coordinate clicks kept missing the small pill nav targets, which is a tooling limitation, not an app bug — confirmed by dispatching a real `element.click()` instead, which is indistinguishable from a user click from the app's point of view): "For employers" clicked from `/pricing` correctly navigated to `/#employers`, flipped `localStorage`'s stored audience to `employer`, and scrolled to `y=2147`, landing exactly on the employer section, not the top of the page. "How it works" and "Features" both correctly resolved from a fresh `job_seeker` default. The same "For employers" click from the mobile sheet menu also worked and closed the sheet. No new console errors; the one `DialogContent`/`DialogTitle` accessibility warning seen while testing is pre-existing in the Sheet component and unrelated to this fix, not touched here.

RESUME MATCH DELETED, REVERSING THE v3.57.0 FIX. Same navigation audit, same page, opposite outcome from the first answer: rather than keep the nav link just added, delete the page entirely. Removed `src/pages/ResumeMatch.tsx`, the route and lazy import in `App.tsx`, the "Resume Match" entry just added to `Header.tsx`'s `navLinks`, and the path from `server.js`'s route allowlist and `public/sitemap.xml`. `ResumeDiffViewer` and `ResumeUpload`, the two components the page imported, are both still genuinely used by real Resume Hub tabs (`JobsTab.tsx`, `ProfileTab.tsx`) and were left alone. Frontend only, same reasoning as World Intelligence: the `resume-match` edge function is still deployed and now has zero callers anywhere in `src/`, joining `delete-account` on the list of orphaned-but-live functions noted under Edge functions below, rather than being torn out here. Verified live: `/resume-match` 404s cleanly, `tsc --noEmit` clean, no console errors, "Resume Match" confirmed gone from the header nav.

Preceded by v3.57.0, a full site navigation audit (every route checked against every `Link`/`navigate()` call in the codebase, not a click-through) found four real gaps, all fixed, plus World Intelligence deleted since nothing linked to it.

SITE NAVIGATION AUDIT, FOUR GAPS FIXED, ONE PAGE DELETED. Asked directly to map every page: how it's reached, and how you get back. Read `App.tsx`'s route list against every `Link` and `navigate()` call in `src/` rather than clicking through the live site, and built a visual audit (published as an Artifact) grouping all 29 real pages by cluster with a status per page: connected, orphaned (nothing links to it), or by-design-external-only (Stripe/email/extension redirects, external document-signing links). Found four real gaps and one clearly dead page:

`/settings` WAS COMPLETELY UNREACHABLE. Not in the Resume Hub account dropdown (Profile, Billing, Sign out), not anywhere in the employer surface (which had no account menu at all). It holds real self-service features: notification preferences, privacy/export/delete account, active sessions. Fixed by adding a Settings item to `ResumeHub.tsx`'s account dropdown, and a full Settings entry (icon rail on desktop, bottom bar on mobile, next to Plan and Sign out) to `EmployerHub.tsx`, which had never had an account menu of any kind before this. `Settings.tsx`/`SettingsLayout.tsx` have no role gating, so both work correctly.

`/do-not-sell` HAD ZERO LINKS ANYWHERE. A real CCPA rights page, but the only place it was ever listed (`LEGAL_LINKS`) has had zero importers since the v3.51.0 footer simplification. Added a "Do Not Sell or Share My Info" link to both footers' bottom bar, next to Privacy Policy and Cookie choices (the conventional placement for this kind of link, and simpler than forcing it into the markdown-driven `LEGAL_DOCS`/`LegalPage` system it was never part of, since `DoNotSell.tsx` is its own bespoke component, not a rendered `.md` file).

THE LEGAL DOCUMENT CLUSTER HAD NO WAY BACK TO THE SITE. `/legal` and its 8 documents (`LegalIndex.tsx`, `LegalPage.tsx`) rendered with no `<Header/>` at all — no logo, no nav bar, just a `<Footer/>` at the very bottom. Added the shared `Header` to both (wrapped in the existing `legal-noprint` class on `LegalPage.tsx` so it correctly disappears from the print stylesheet), and bumped both pages' top padding from `py-12/16` to `pt-32 pb-12/16` to clear the fixed header.

`/resume-match` WAS ALSO ORPHANED, LINKED IN NOW. A fully built, no-login match-and-rewrite tool with zero links anywhere in the app. Added "Resume Match" to `Header.tsx`'s shared `navLinks` array (used by both the desktop pill nav and the mobile sheet), between "For employers" and "Pricing".

`/world-intelligence` WAS ORPHANED TOO, DELETED INSTEAD. Also zero inbound links, but the founder's call here was to remove it rather than wire it in. Deleted `src/pages/WorldIntelligence.tsx`, the whole `src/components/dashboard/simulator/` directory (10 files, confirmed exclusively used by that one page), and `src/lib/enginApi.ts` (exclusively used by that directory); removed the route, lazy import and idle-preload call from `App.tsx`; dropped the path from `server.js`'s route allowlist and `public/sitemap.xml`. Frontend only — the `ayn-agent-society` edge function and its backend tables were deliberately left alone, a separate and much bigger decision than deleting an unreachable page. Details in `docs/map/platform.md`.

Verified live: `/world-intelligence` now 404s cleanly, `tsc --noEmit` clean, no console errors on a fresh load of `/`, `/pricing`, `/legal`, and `/terms`; the Do Not Sell and Resume Match links confirmed present with correct `href`s on both footers and the header nav.

Preceded by v3.56.0, the Help Center's Q&A list collapsed into an accordion so the search bar is the actual way to find an answer, not a wall of pre-expanded text.

HELP CENTER WAS TOO MUCH AT ONCE. Reported directly: all ~24 questions across all 5 categories rendered fully expanded, answer text and all, the instant the page loaded, so the search box that already existed sat above a full page of content it was supposed to be filtering. `Help.tsx`'s Q&A cards are now native `<details>/<summary>` elements (no new dependency; this project has no accordion component and `@radix-ui/react-accordion` isn't installed, so plain HTML disclosure elements do the job with real keyboard and screen reader support for free): collapsed by default, question only, a `ChevronDown` that flips on open, native marker hidden with `list-none marker:content-none`. Typing in the existing search box still filters sections/entries by keyword as before, and now also force-opens every entry left in the filtered result (`open={hasQuery || undefined}`) so a search actually surfaces the answer immediately instead of just a shorter list of collapsed questions to click through one more time. With no query, everything starts collapsed and the reader opens only what they came for. Verified live in a fresh tab: default view is ~24 one-line rows instead of a full page of text, clicking a question toggles it opens correctly, searching "refund" filtered to the two matching questions in Credits and billing with both auto-expanded and their answers visible, no console errors, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.55.0, /about's opening rewritten with a real founding-story paragraph and a labeled "Mission and vision" statement.

ABOUT PAGE GOT A REAL OPENING STORY AND A NAMED MISSION STATEMENT. Follow-up to v3.54.0, with exact copy supplied directly. The generic "we believe applying should be about whether you can do it" opening paragraph is replaced with a concrete founding story: AI made applying effortless so everyone did, a hiring manager who used to read forty applications started opening six hundred and reading none of them properly, the one person who could do the job was buried in that pile with nobody able to find them, so AYN got built. Directly under that, a `SectionHeading`-labeled "Mission and vision" block was added (the one exception to v3.54.0's "no pitch labels" rule, since this is a genuine single mission statement, not a three-part narrative): "Replace volume with evidence. Build a hiring market where being seen depends on what you have done, not on how many places you applied." The existing "what it does for job seekers and employers" and discoverability paragraphs, the hero card, and the CTA are all unchanged, just pushed down to follow the new opening. Verified live in a fresh tab: renders correctly, no console errors, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.54.0, /about rewritten from a three-part pitch (The problem / How we got here / What it does) into a single plain mission statement.

ABOUT PAGE READ LIKE A PITCH, NOT AN ABOUT PAGE. Reported directly: the three section labels from the v3.50.0/v3.52.0 body ("The problem", "How we got here", "What it does") made the page feel like a sales narrative rather than an actual page about the company. Asked what real content should replace it; the founder chose keeping the mission statement, dropping the pitch-deck structure. `About.tsx`'s three `SectionHeading`-labeled sections are gone, replaced with three unlabeled flowing paragraphs directly under the hero card (same underlying facts, no section framing): what AYN believes and why it exists, what it does for job seekers and employers in one plain paragraph each, and the discoverability privacy note. The hero card itself (mission line, "AYN is built by a team in Canada.", ember accent bar) and the "Start free" CTA are unchanged. `SectionHeading` (v3.52.0) is untouched and still used on `/help` and `/contact`, just no longer imported here. Verified live: renders correctly, no console errors in a fresh tab, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Preceded by v3.53.0, /contact is a real form instead of a raw mailto link, and a genuine RLS gap that made guest ticket submission structurally impossible was found and fixed underneath it.

CONTACT PAGE IS A FORM, NOT AN EMAIL ADDRESS. Asked directly: drop the postal address, replace the plain "email us" block with an actual form, and point every other page that told someone to contact AYN at that same form instead of a raw mailto link. `Contact.tsx`'s "Postal" section (the registered office address) is deleted outright; its "Email" section is replaced with `TicketForm` (already existed, already wired to `support_tickets`/`ticket_messages`/`send-ticket-notification`, previously only ever used signed-in inside Help's own "Send us a message" panel). `Help.tsx`'s raw `mailto:support@aynn.io` links (the empty search state and "Still stuck") now point to `/contact`; its own signed-in-only embedded copy of the same form was deleted as redundant now that `/contact` covers everyone, along with the now-unused `signedIn` state. `TermsModal.tsx` still has a bare `support@aynn.io` mention but is confirmed dead code (zero importers besides the also-dead `useAuth.ts`, per the v3.31.0 finding), left untouched. Legal document markdown (`terms.md`, `privacy.md`, etc.) and the registered address inside them are a different, deliberately separate legal-notice channel and were not touched.

THE GUEST PATH THROUGH THAT FORM HAD NEVER ACTUALLY WORKED. `TicketForm` was written years ago with a real guest branch (`guest_email`/`guest_name` when `!user`), and `support_tickets`'s own insert policy already allowed a guest row, but nothing had ever exercised that path in production since every real caller was signed in. Simulating the exact anon insert directly against Postgres surfaced three compounding bugs, found and fixed as a single migration (`supabase/migrations/20260804120000_allow_guest_ticket_messages.sql`): (1) `has_duty_access(uuid)`, called by a `{public}`-role SELECT policy on both tables, was never granted `EXECUTE` to `anon`, so any `insert().select()` as a guest threw a hard permission error before a row could even be returned; (2) `INSERT ... RETURNING` re-checks the new row against the table's own SELECT policies, and no SELECT policy grants a guest visibility into a ticket it just created (there is no session tying an anonymous row back to its author), so `.select()` failed RLS even once the insert itself was allowed; (3) `ticket_messages`' insert policy re-queried `support_tickets` by hand in a subquery, which is itself subject to `support_tickets`' RLS, so the same invisible-guest-row problem blocked a guest's own opening message specifically. Fixed with a `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO anon`, a new `SECURITY DEFINER` helper `ticket_belongs_to_caller()` (same pattern as `has_duty_access`/`has_role`, bypasses RLS internally while enforcing the identical boundary) replacing the subquery, and a client-side fix in `TicketForm.tsx`: the ticket id is now generated with `crypto.randomUUID()` and both inserts drop `.select()` entirely, so nothing ever needs to be read back under RLS. The email field is now `required` in the UI, matching the guest insert policy's own `guest_email` requirement, and the form's copy was reworded from internal "ticket" language ("Submit Ticket", "Ticket Created!") to plain "Send message" / "Message sent" for a public-facing surface. Verified live end to end as a real signed-out visitor: submitted the form on `/contact`, confirmed both rows landed in `support_tickets` and `ticket_messages` by direct query, then confirmed as a separate negative test that an anonymous caller still cannot post into a real authenticated user's ticket (the same RLS boundary that protected real accounts before this change still holds). Test rows cleaned up after.

New shared `src/components/shared/SectionHeading.tsx`, a small ember accent bar beside the heading text, used for every section `<h2>` across all three pages (9 headings total: About's "The problem"/"How we got here"/"What it does", Help's per-category headings plus "Still stuck"/"Send us a message", Contact's "Email"/"Before you write"/"Specific requests"/"Postal"/"What we do not have"). Preceded by v3.51.0, the footer's flat "Company" column split into a "Navigate" column and a real "Company" column.

FOOTER COMPANY COLUMN. Asked directly to organize the pages and group About Us, Help Center, Legal and Contact Us under a "Company" heading. Both footers' "Company" column had drifted into `NAV_LINKS`, a flat six-item mix of primary nav (Home, How it works, Pricing) and company pages (About, Help Center, Contact) with no Legal entry, while the separate "Legal" column held Privacy Policy, the `/legal` hub link and Cookie choices. Split `NAV_LINKS` back down to the three primary-nav items and added a new `COMPANY_LINKS` array (`src/components/shared/siteLinks.ts`): About Us, Help Center, Legal, Contact Us.
