UPDATE v3.81.0: `ayn-unified` is resolved, not mysterious. Pulled its live source: a real, modern v3.7.0 job-search-copilot chat function with zero callers anywhere in this repo. Deleted along with `ayn-agent-society` (the World Intelligence backend) and everything both fed — see the version entry at the top of this file for the full writeup. Both functions and their tables are gone from the live project as of this entry; the "39 ACTIVE functions" count above is now stale by these two.

UPDATE v3.113.0: `send-admin-email` and `send-notifications` deleted for real, both re-confirmed dead first (zero references anywhere in `src/`, re-grepped immediately before deleting). `send-admin-email`'s own `admin_emails` table turned out not to exist in the live database at all — querying it directly returned `relation "public.admin_emails" does not exist`, so that function's logging path had been silently broken since before this map ever described it. `admin@ayn.careers` (the identity that function used to send from) is now a real, working reply identity inside `admin-inbox-reply` instead.

UPDATE v3.132.0: `error-alert-check` added — a new, real edge function, cron-scheduled every 10 minutes (`net.http_post`, anon key bearer, same pattern as `ayn-daily-report`/`resource-monitor-hourly`), reading `error_logs` and emailing `NOTIFICATION_EMAIL` on a burst. See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup, including the stale-secret finding (that address is still `noreply@aynn.io`, the retired domain, pending the founder confirming the real one).

UPDATE v3.134.0: `job-board-sync` added — a new, real edge function, cron-scheduled every 4 hours, pulling real job postings from company career pages (via freehire's public API, never LinkedIn or Indeed) into the new `job_postings` table, filtered against a real aggregator-domain denylist and a non-Latin-script check, pruned past a 7-day freshness window every run. See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup, including the two real, live-caught data-quality bugs (aggregator links hiding in freehire's own results; a truncated-description endpoint) fixed before this was trusted.

UPDATE v3.166.0: `ats-direct-sync` added — a second, independent job-posting source alongside `job-board-sync`'s freehire feed, reading Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby's own public job-board APIs directly for companies already discovered via freehire's `apply_url`s. Cron-scheduled every 2 hours (offset 20 minutes from `job-board-sync`'s own slot). See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup, including the real worker-memory ceiling found and tuned around live (not guessed at) and the dedup mechanism that keeps one row per job regardless of which source found it. Also that same pass: `job-board-sync` and `error-alert-check`'s cron entries were found still pointed at the pre-domain-merge `supabase.ayn.careers` subdomain and fixed to `ayn.careers` via `cron.alter_job` — neither was ever registered through a migration, so no migration needed correcting.

UPDATE v3.195.0: `ai-openai-bridge` added — a small, internal-only, service-role-key-gated function letting the new `job-checker` service (see below) reach the same `AI_RELAY_URL`/`RELAY_SECRET` gateway `resume-hub`'s own `callAI()` already uses, without that credential ever leaving the edge runtime. Not a public-facing function; nothing outside AYN's own infrastructure has any reason to call it. Also new this pass, but NOT an edge function at all, worth noting so it isn't missed on a future edge-function inventory: `job-checker` (`job-checker/` in this repo) is a standalone Docker container running a Python/FastAPI service (ScrapeGraphAI + headless Chromium), reached by `job-board-sync` over the internal Docker network only, never deployed as a Supabase edge function and never reachable on any public port. See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup of what it does and how it's wired in.


## Global rules (apply everywhere)

1. Never write to the applications or job_applications tables. Both trackers are deprecated and the Tracker UI was deleted in v3.0.1.
2. User-facing writing style: no em dashes, no en dashes, ranges use "to". This rule is also baked into the AI system prompts in cc-generate and resume-match.
3. servers: server.js is the express static host for dist/ (SPA fallback, caching for /assets and /frames). backend/server.py is a FastAPI health stub only, not a real backend. The real backend is Supabase edge functions.
4. memory/PRD.md describes an older platform-era snapshot; trust the docs/map files over it.
5. scripts/check-wiring.mjs must pass; it verifies every action src/lib/resumeHub.ts calls has a matching handler in the resume-hub edge function.


## The nervous system (how signals actually flow)

```
                        ayn.careers (Resume Hub)
                                   │
              ┌────────────────────┴────────────────────┐
              ▼                                          ▼
   Browse Jobs / Add job manually              Employer chat (EmployerHub)
   (job-board-sync cron ingestion,             (intake widget → JobSpec)
    or resolveJobJd on a pasted URL)                      │
              │                                           ▼
              ▼                                employer_match (candidate_index,
   resume-hub edge fn                            pgvector recall, grounded rerank)
   (match / tailor / cover_letter,                        │
    resume_versions, ai_result_cache)                     ▼
                                                 reveal_requests (proposals)
                                                            │
                                                            ▼
                                                 inbox_messages (screened both ways)
```

Five loops carry everything:

1. JOB INTAKE LOOP: a job enters Resume Hub either automatically (Browse Jobs, job-board-sync's own cron ingestion, no action needed) or by hand (Add job manually, a pasted URL or text resolved server-side by resolveJobJd/parseJobMeta). Either path lands the same job_url/description in the jobs table that match/tailor/cover_letter all read from. The Chrome extension that used to read a live page for this was retired in v3.164.0; there is no live-page-reading path any more, by design.
2. GAP LOOP (v3.1.0): _shared/tailoring.ts computes matched / missing / nice-to-have deterministically from the JD against structured sections, the model only surfaces and phrases, and the same analysis is returned to the client so the user sees what is genuinely missing from their background.
3. MATCH LOOP (v2.9.0-B): seeker opts in -> indexCandidate builds anonymized profile_text + 768d embedding + extracted/inferred skills -> the employer's intake widget distills a JobSpec -> employer_match runs extracted-only prefilter (must-haves), pgvector recall (top 12), then a single grounded rerank on opaque refs (inferred capped at 10 pts) -> top 3 anonymous cards. The ref_map that binds refs to real users never leaves the edge function.
4. PROPOSAL LOOP (v3.6.0): employer opens a card in EmployerHub and reads the full anonymous reasoning -> sends a job proposal (title, location, type, salary, link, message ≤1000 chars) through employer_reveal_request, rate limited to one open proposal per org and candidate and blocked for 30 days after a decline -> the row lands in reveal_requests -> the seeker sees it on the Resume Hub Proposals tab, badged in the rail and first in Home next-actions -> reveal_decide records accept or decline -> ONLY on accept does employer_reveal_status return name, email and phone. Everything before that step is anonymous, enforced in the edge function, not in the UI.
5. INBOX LOOP (v3.163.0): either side sends a message on an existing proposal thread through inbox_send -> screenMessageBody blocks any link, phone number, email address, or off-platform-app mention before it's stored, rules only, no AI call -> a blocked message is still written (status: 'blocked') so the employer's own side can see what was attempted and why, but the candidate's read path (status = 'sent' only, enforced by RLS) never sees it -> a candidate can only ever reply, never initiate a new message or a call invite, and only when the employer has opened that thread two-way and not blocked them.


## Honest assessment (strengths, weaknesses, what is actually smart)

STRENGTHS AND MOAT
- Everything AYN now promises runs on our own backend: JD extraction, match scoring, tailoring, cover letters, talent pool matching. No hostile territory, no probability disguised as a guarantee.
- Unified identity (_shared/identity.ts) feeds scoring, tailoring, and cover letters from one place, so quality fixes land everywhere at once.
- Job intake is now entirely first-party: Browse Jobs ingests real company-sourced postings on a cron, and a pasted URL or text is resolved server side — no dependency on a person having a specific browser extension installed to use the product at all.

WEAKNESSES AND RISKS
- supabase/functions/resume-hub/index.ts is still a large monolith with no unit tests for most of it (e2e/ covers the legacy platform).
- The legacy platform roughly doubles repo weight, confuses tooling and new contributors, and its e2e suites can mislead CI signals.
- Bus factor of one (solo founder). These map files are the mitigation; keeping them current is not optional.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY SMART HERE (for an AI forming a mental model fast)
The standing architectural rule that actually found this app's real bugs, over and over: code decides facts, the model only phrases them. The deterministic gap analysis (computeGap), the ATS rubric's point deductions, the figure-preservation check, the semantic-recheck threshold, the assessment scoring split (a fixed rubric plus a boolean/count the model reports, never a number it states on its own) — none of these ever ask the model to discover or invent a fact; they ask it to write well about a fact code already computed. Judge any proposed change against that split: let the model write, never let it decide.
